Its stated meaning, as the lane brief of round 8 relays it: stop the lanes S1-B1 and G-A, and PR #1024 if it depends on them; delete the VM `artifact-builder-vm-1` (OVH BHS5, d2-4); amend this plan with no VM bridge; builds go to the k8s pool or to the V8 isolate.

Owner answer to decision D1 (verbatim, 13/11 ~14:20, relayed by immo i-cond) — binding, applied by revision 5:

> « D1: artefacts - statiques / et live du coup (il faut que le v8 fasse des updates live au sens update par une ia en qq millisecondes), basé sur le code commité - bien sûr en anticipation il faut migrer immo vers le bon langage du coup »

`[JUGEMENT]` It is read as one decision, two requirements and one instruction of sequencing: (1) decision D1 is taken — the V8 scope is the artifact builds, static (publication) and live; (2) a live update made by an AI is in the range of a few milliseconds (section 2.13); (3) the build input is the committed code, for static and for live (section 2.12); (4) the migration of the immo UI to the language that V8 builds is anticipated now (section 5.6).

`[JUGEMENT]` Requirement (2) is never relaxed silently. Since revision 6 it is applied by three rules, stated in section 2.13: "a few milliseconds" has one numeric meaning, less than 10 ms; no criterion of a quantity that this plan claims in that range is 10 ms or more, in any class, any engine and any gate; what cannot be in that range — the network, the display — is named with its own number, outside every claimed figure, and what this plan cannot put in it goes to the owner as a numbered decision with its numbers (decisions D12 and D13). Revision 7 closes two gaps that the round-6 review found in their application: a claimed figure is never a difference between two runs — the component's own render is delimited by marks of the page in every edit — and no gate applies a figure that is before the owner until the owner has answered: decision D13 is a prerequisite of every gate that applies its ceiling. Revision 8 answered the gap that the round-7 review found in the page — the two marks of revision 7 enclosed the whole hot-replacement call of Vue, so that the runtime's own work left the claimed figure with the render — by leaving outside the claim only the intervals delimited around the functions of the component itself. The round-8 review showed that such an interval still holds work of the hot replacement: the registration of the children that a render creates. Revision 9 stops separating, on the conductor's decision: the replacement in the page is claimed whole, from the patch received to the DOM mutated — the runtime's work, that registration and the render of the component included — under the criterion that the swap had; the render is measured apart for information only, never to excuse an overrun, and an overrun opens decision D12 with its numbers.

The four verbatims come from the briefs of this planning lane. A written record elsewhere: `unknown`.

The additions G-2, G-4 and G-8 that the brief also lists are DS gaps of the app plan, which carries them as temporary exceptions `[FAIT: APP-SPEC:770-776]`; no lot of this plan waits for them.
- `T+h` = h hours after 13/11 10:00 Montréal, the origin of the schedule.
- Decisions: "decision D1" to "decision D14" are the owner decisions of section 7. Decision D1 is taken (13/11 ~14:20). Decisions D7, D8 and D10 concerned the VM and its bridge: the owner's directive of 13/11 ~17:55 leaves them without an object, and section 7 keeps their numbers with what closes each. Decision D12 is conditional and exists only if a measurement misses a latency criterion (section 2.13). Decision D13 is not conditional: it puts before the owner what this plan already knows to be outside the range, and since revision 7 its answer is a prerequisite of every gate that applies its ceiling (section 5.10). Decision D14, added by revision 8, is the sizing of the k8s builder pool during the transition (section 4.4). A decision of the builder dossier is always written "of the builder dossier". "Lot D7" is a lot of the DS plan (Tailwind removal), not a decision.
- Latency words (section 2.13). *A few milliseconds* = less than 10 ms. A *stage* is one step of the path of one live edit. A *mark* is a monotonic timestamp taken by one process. *Processing* = an interval between two marks of the same process: the only kind of figure that this plan claims in the range. A claimed figure is such an interval, or a sum of such intervals of one edit; it is never a difference between two runs. The *replacement* = the whole interval of the page for one edit, `p0 → p2`, from the patch received to the DOM mutated: claimed whole since revision 9, render included (section 2.13); revisions 6 to 8 claimed a part of it, which they called the swap. A *render interval* = the interval between the entry and the return of one outermost call, by the Vue runtime, of a `setup` or a `render` function of a component. Since revision 9 it is information only: it lies inside the claimed replacement, is subtracted from nothing and excuses no overrun (section 2.13). *Transit* = what lies between the send mark of one process and the receive mark of the next: network, never claimed, with a number of its own. *Warm* = the compile context of the live session already holds the compiler and has compiled the module graph once; *cold* = that context must be created first. The *live size profile* = an SFC of at most 600 lines (size classes S and M).
- All times are Montréal time (EST, UTC−5).

## 1. Résumé owner (FR)

Constats : [FAIT], sections 2 à 4. Cibles et dates : [JUGEMENT], au plus tôt, heure de Montréal. Rien n'est mesuré.

**Votre directive du 13/11 ~17:55 est appliquée et la revue du tour 8 corrigée (sections 15 et 16).**

- **Étape 1 arrêtée**, G-A annulée sans verdict, PR #1024 non fusionnée. Plus de pont par la VM.
- **VM supprimée** par k8s dès que le pool est confirmé apte à servir les builds (K-0).
- **Builds sur le pool k8s** jusqu'à la bascule V8, sans optimisation. Pool à 0 nœud au repos : 5 à 8 minutes d'attente à froid (décision 14).
- **Code de l'étape 1**, fusionné et inutilisé : retiré à l'étape 7, sauf le contrat, qui porte le profil d'isolat du V8.

**Décision 1 (prise).** Le V8 construit les artefacts, statiques et live.

- **« Quelques millisecondes » = moins de 10 ms**, composant de 600 lignes au plus : de l'édition reçue au module prêt (5 ms, p95 9 ms), puis chaque étape de calcul jusqu'au remplacement dans la page (2 ms, p95 5 ms), tenu en entier, runtime de Vue et rendu du composant compris. Le rendu, mesuré à part pour information, n'excuse rien : tout dépassement ouvre la décision 12, chiffres à l'appui. Somme sous 10 ms à la médiane ; au p95, son plafond est le vôtre (décision 13). Hors plage, chiffrés à part, jamais retranchés : réseau (p95 5 ms), affichage (16,7 ms), démarrage à froid (500 ms). Aucune porte ne passe au-dessus de 10 ms sur un chiffre tenu.
- **Au-delà de 600 lignes**, hors plage et hors porte : 8 fichiers sur 69 chez immo, 53 % des lignes.
- **Code commité.** Publication sur un commit exact, live sur un commit plus son brouillon. Même commit, même empreinte, ou un refus.
- **Immo anticipé.** Règles de source dès maintenant, Focus-signal publié en V8 le 20/11.

**Cible (inchangée).** h2a et Focus publient des artefacts que cluster-mesh livre, immo d'abord. Un seul cœur de compilation, navigateur et isolat V8 du serveur MCP : ni VM, ni pod, ni `npm install`.

**Phases** (heures de build, date au plus tôt).

1. Dossier et artefacts h2a en Vue Vapor, 16/11 16:00.
2. Build V8, deux modes, live, 341 h, qualifié le 21/11 14:00.
3. h2a via cluster-mesh, 46 h, 19/11 14:00.
4. Focus livré à immo, 28 h, 20/11 05:00.
5. Immo en V8, 92 h, Focus-signal en live le 22/11 00:00.
6. geo, 48 h, 23/11 04:00.
7. Retrait du pool et du code de l'étape 1, 28 h : bascule le 23/11 18:00, pool supprimé le 30/11 21:00 (votre GO), code retiré le 01/12.

**Décisions à prendre** (section 7, recommandation citée).

- Maintenant. **14** pool à 1 nœud jusqu'à la bascule (votre décision du 07/11 ; 24 CAD par mois, le coût de la VM, 8 CAD d'ici le 23/11), sinon 0 nœud et l'attente à froid. **5** isolat dans chaque processus MCP, 2 sessions live chaudes par hôte. **6** trois branches sentropic dédiées. **9** miroir ESM au Canada.
- **11** candidat Vue sous `ui/**` (14/11). **4** le serveur recompile depuis le commit (17/11). **3** Tailwind en feuille figée (18/11). **2** artefact immo par point d'entrée (23/11).
- **13**, deux réponses. Plus de 600 lignes (16/11) : décomposer avant la qualification live (10 h, puis environ 34 h) ; sinon exception jusqu'à 62,5 ms au p95, ou refus de l'édition live. Somme au p95 (21/11 14:00) : plafond de 15,5 ms proposé, ou 9 ms ; sans réponse, aucune porte de latence n'est prononcée.
- **12**, conditionnelle : une mesure manque une cible (dès le 14/11).
- Sans objet depuis votre directive : **7**, **8**, **10** (pont, agent gVisor, préavis de la VM).

## 2. Target architecture

### 2.1 Reading of the directive, vocabulary, scope

[JUGEMENT] The directive is read as four requirements, in the owner's order:

1. **Delivery chain.** h2a is built on `@sentropic/artifacts` and delivered through cluster-mesh. Focus, the decision-dossier engine, is built on artifacts. Cluster-mesh delivers Focus to consumers, immo first, as artifacts.
2. **Vue Vapor order.** Decision dossier and artifacts first; then the immo UI and geo.
3. **No VM.** Every artifact build runs in V8, in two mandatory modes: (a) a UI component that builds in the browser; (b) the MCP server that builds in a V8 isolate, server-side.
4. **Build optimisation leaves immo.** Immo consumes what the chain delivers; it no longer tunes a build.

Vocabulary:

| Term | Meaning | State |
|---|---|---|
| Artifact, release | The `@sentropic/artifacts` model: an artifact, a binding, immutable releases `[FAIT: sen:packages/artifacts/src/contracts.ts:58,68,75]`; a release is a manifest plus bytes stored under digest-addressed keys `[FAIT: sen:packages/artifacts/src/server/publication.ts:186-190]` | exists |
| Focus | The decision-dossier engine owned by h2a: `@sentropic/focus` (model, validation, string renderers) and the portable renderer `focus-kit` `[FAIT: h2a:packages/focus/package.json:2-4; h2a:packages/h2a/focus-kit/package.json:2-13]` | exists |
| Focus-signal | An immo **application** artifact: branches `artifact/p/bbfae795/focus-signal-*`, which edit `SignauxMapView.svelte` and `SignalFocusPanel.svelte` inside the immo application `[FAIT: DOSSIER:88]`. It is not the Focus engine. The fixture `F-FOCUS-OPT` is this artifact `[FAIT: FFO:1-4]` | exists |
| Compile unit | One call of `compile(recipe)`: a bounded set of sources compiled to ES modules, CSS and assets, identified by a recipe key `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:132-135,175]` | target, approved in the h2a spec |
| Build plan | The ordered compile units of one artifact, with one root manifest (section 2.9) | new |
| Closure | The pinned pre-built ESM files an artifact imports (Vue runtime, DS packages, third-party packages), listed in the mirror manifest | target |
| Compile core | The single pure-JavaScript library that parses, transforms and links; it runs unchanged in both hosts | new |

Scope. `[FAIT: owner answer of 13/11 ~14:20, header]` Decision D1 is taken: "artefacts - statiques / et live". `[JUGEMENT]` Reading, which is option A of revision 4 with two requirements added:

- **In scope:** every build the artifact builder performs today — publications ("static") and live sessions of git-mode artifacts — plus h2a dossiers and h2a visual artifacts. Two requirements come with the decision: the build input is the committed code (section 2.12), and a live update made by an AI is in the range of a few milliseconds (section 2.13).
- **Out of scope, by decision D1:** the production images of the product UIs, built by their own CI: the sentropic application `[FAIT: APP-SPEC:590]`, the geo site `[FAIT: geo:.github/workflows/pages.yml:55-69]`, and the immo application, whose production build is `vite build` `[FAIT: immo:ui/package.json:8]` (its CI pipeline was not read: `unknown`).
- **Out of scope:** the gVisor `sandbox` class of builder step 2 for remote CLI hosting and agent loops — the gVisor lane is stopped by the owner's directive of 13/11 (section 4.3), and no lot of this plan prepares it; the Preact passes; the immo datastore redesign (W6), except its Tailwind removal lot when decision D3 requires it.

### 2.2 Delivery chain

Internal specification view; an owner-facing diagram derived from it must be drawn with the DS `DiagramSceneFigure` `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:114]`.

```
 sources (dossier JSON · SFC graph inside devScope)              pinned closure (Vue, DS, third-party)
        │                                                                   │
        ▼                                                                   ▼
 ┌────────────────── V8 build — one compile core, two hosts ──────────────────────┐
 │ (a) browser: Web Worker inside the          (b) server: isolate inside the      │
 │     ArtifactBuilder UI component                MCP host process                │
 └───────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────┘
                 │      same recipe key, same manifest digest   │
                 └───────────────────────┬─────────────────────┘
                                         ▼
          @sentropic/artifacts — release = manifest + digest-addressed bytes
          (h2a dossiers and visual artifacts · Focus · immo and geo artifacts)
                                         │
                                         ▼
          cluster-mesh — MCP registry (providers: artifacts, Focus) · compile · serve
                                         │
               ┌─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┐
               ▼                         ▼                          ▼
         immo MCP host            sentropic MCP host        geo (hosted by the sentropic host, VD-6.3)
   public origin per artifact (release; live session by admission) · MCP `ui://` resource · offline single-file export (h2a)
   a live session serves the drafts of both modes and the preview of the browser mode (2.11)
```

`[JUGEMENT]` Responsibilities (the owners of the existing parts are facts cited in section 3):

| Layer | Owner | Role in the chain |
|---|---|---|
| `@sentropic/artifacts` | sentropic (s-conductor) | Storage, releases, access, content origin, git mode, live sessions; receives V8-built outputs |
| cluster-mesh | sentropic (s-conductor) | Compile core and its two bindings, mirror client, cache and serve, MCP registry and providers; the delivery channel |
| Focus and h2a | h2a (h-cond) | Dossier contracts, validation, Vue renderer sources, publication of dossiers and visual artifacts as releases, offline export. h2a publishes releases with release notes; each consumer pins on its side `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:11]` |
| Design system | DS | Vue Vapor components, elements on Vapor, catalogue and validator, content of the package closure |
| Consumers | immo (i-cond); geo (geo-cond, repository `rhanka/geo`) | Select providers, author sources inside their devScope, accept |
| k8s session | k8s | Mirror storage and route, pod budgets, the deletion of the VM (K-0), the builder pool during the transition and its retirement (K-7) |

### 2.3 Focus and h2a on artifacts

Today `[FAIT]`:

- `buildFocusDossier` validates the document, resolves diagrams, substitutes the payload into a prebuilt `renderer.html` and returns one HTML string `[h2a:packages/h2a/src/focus-build.ts:110,148-153]`. That renderer is built by Vite in IIFE format with the Svelte plugin `[h2a:scripts/build-focus-dossier.mjs:51-61]`; the committed file weighs 368,344 bytes (`git cat-file -s`).
- h2a visual artifacts are MCP App shells that mount DS custom elements from a vendored snapshot `[h2a:packages/h2a/src/runtime/mcp/ui-render/registry-entry.ts:88-96; h2a:packages/h2a/src/runtime/mcp/ui-render/assets.ts:1-5]`; those elements depend on Svelte `[ds:packages/elements/package.json:57]`.
- h2a declares `@sentropic/artifacts` only as an optional peer at `0.2.6`, and no `src` file imports it `[h2a:packages/h2a/package.json:94,97; git grep "@sentropic/artifacts" h2a:'packages/*/src' → 0 hit]`. It depends on `@sentropic/cluster-mesh` `0.25.0` `[h2a:packages/h2a/package.json:80]`.
- Immo keeps three private copies of a Svelte dossier kit under `docs/`, each built by Vite in Docker under a 1,536 MB cap `[immo:docs/architecture/focus/package.json:2-14; immo:docs/architecture/focus/Makefile:8]`.

Target `[JUGEMENT]`:

1. **A dossier is an artifact release.** Publishing a dossier creates a release whose manifest lists the HTML shell, the document payload (the exact bytes of the existing h2a serializer, never re-encoded `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:89]`), the derived diagram views, the evidence files, the compiled renderer modules and CSS, and the closure files the renderer imports. Identical bytes are stored once per storage scope, because the blob key is the digest `[FAIT: sen:packages/artifacts/src/server/publication.ts:189]`: embedding the renderer and its closure in every release costs storage once.
2. **The renderer is compiled by the chain, not vendored.** Its Vue sources are an h2a-owned source graph; its outputs come from the compile service under a recipe key; hosted, MCP and offline paths use the same outputs `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:245]`.
3. **h2a visual artifacts take the same shape.** The `BarChart`, `BpmnCanvas` and `DecisionDossier` resources keep their URIs and envelope contracts `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:81]`; their code closures become compile outputs served through the mesh adapter.
4. **Delivery is a cluster-mesh provider.** A Focus provider is registered beside the existing `sentropic.artifacts` builtin `[FAIT: sen:packages/cluster-mesh/src/integrations/mcp/artifacts.ts:18-60]`. A consumer that selects it gets the Focus tools on its own MCP endpoint, and a published dossier lands in its own storage scope. The namespace `/focus` is already reserved in the closed namespace list `[FAIT: sen:packages/contracts/src/cluster-mesh-namespace.ts:6]`.
5. **Offline export stays an h2a product property.** `h2a focus build` produces one file without network, registry, mesh or toolchain, from a frozen closure shipped in the h2a release `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:243-247]`.
6. **Immo deletes its three kits** once a real immo dossier is published through the delivered Focus (lot VD-4.2).

### 2.4 The V8 build: one compile core, two hosts

`[FAIT]` Decided boundary: `compile(source, kind vue-sfc | vue-template, compilerVersion) → {module, css, digest, diagnostics}` and `serve(digest)`, reusing the `@sentropic/skills` SandboxRuntime, with no second isolate stack `[NIGHT:175]`. The h2a spec refines it into a versioned contract that sentropic confirmed as design answers: API version 1, a closed recipe, a recipe key computed as SHA-256 over a domain prefix and the JCS encoding of the recipe, a separate output-manifest digest, one shared set of diagnostic codes, and limits `[H2A-SPEC:130-151,175-196,297-324]`. The DS plan asks for the same kit in a Web Worker and in the isolate, with the same digest `[PLAN:377-383,1027]`.

`[JUGEMENT]` Rule: **one compile core, executed by two hosts, producing the same bytes.**

| | (a) Browser | (b) Server |
|---|---|---|
| Host | `ArtifactBuilder`, a UI component (Vue Vapor SFC on DS components) placed in a product UI. The DS documentation reuses its compile worker for island examples (lot V13c), outside the preview of section 2.11 | The MCP host process that composes cluster-mesh: the sentropic API for tenant sentropic, the immo MCP host for tenant immo (it already depends on artifacts and cluster-mesh `[FAIT: immo:packages/immo-mcp/package.json:38-39]`). A package bump does not wire a host: each host injects its own ports. Immo's host admits the executor `sandbox` only and composes the HTTP clients of the remote builder for builds and for live `[FAIT: immo:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/compose.ts:167-172,181,273,302; immo:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/build-api.ts:31-33,162-164]`. The composition is made of lots per host: VD-2.8, VD-2.14 and VD-2.9 for the sentropic host, VD-5.6 (build port) and VD-5.4 (live ports) for the immo host |
| Execution | A dedicated Web Worker per build session, terminated on cancel or deadline; it stays loaded between the edits of the session | An `isolated-vm` isolate through the adapted SandboxRuntime: warm pool, fresh context per job `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:212]`. A live session is the one exception: it keeps an isolate of its own for as long as it lives (section 2.13) |
| Compiler code | The compiler bootstrap bundle, fetched from the mirror with SRI | The same bundle bytes, loaded as a script: the current adapter compiles scripts, not ES modules `[FAIT: sen:packages/skills/src/sandbox/runtime.ts:392]` |
| Inputs | Committed code (section 2.12): the blobs of the base commit of the session, read through the product API inside the session's devScope, plus the draft overlay held by the UI | Committed code (section 2.12), read by the host and projected through devScope: for a publication, the git snapshot at one commit; for a live session, that commit plus the session's draft. MCP tool arguments carry data (a dossier document), or a source blob under a virtual-source grant of the compile service; they are not an input of a git-mode artifact |
| Dependencies | The mirror manifest (import map and digests); nothing is installed | The same manifest, resolved locally; the isolate fetches nothing |
| Outputs | Modules, CSS, assets and manifest in the browser cache, pushed by digest to the draft store of the live session; preview in a top-level window on the artifact's public origin, served there as a live session (section 2.11) | Verified outputs in the tenant cache, then in a release |
| Used by | A person editing in a UI: immediate preview, no server compile | An agent calling MCP tools without a browser; every publication; the packaging of the offline export |
| Authority | Preview only (decision D4) | Publication |

What runs nowhere `[JUGEMENT, on the cited facts]`: no `npm install`, no Node file system, no subprocess, no bundler binary, no WebAssembly tool — the toolchain is `@vue/compiler-sfc` and Sucrase, both pure JavaScript `[FAIT: PLAN:370-371,1025,1030]` — no network from the compiler, and no execution of compiled user code at compile time `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:230]`.

The compile core `[JUGEMENT]`:

- **Steps:** parse the SFC; compile script and template (Vapor output for the browser profile); strip TypeScript; compile styles and resolve stylesheet imports (section 2.6); resolve relative, alias and workspace imports inside the virtual source graph (section 2.5); leave the remaining bare specifiers external for the import map, except inside a worker graph (section 2.6); emit one ES module per source module, plus CSS and assets; build the output manifest.
- **Neutral:** no Node built-in, no DOM API. One pure-JavaScript hashing implementation serves both hosts; `@stablelib/sha256` is already a dependency of cluster-mesh `[FAIT: sen:packages/cluster-mesh/package.json:258]`.
- **One bundle:** the core and its compilers are delivered as one pre-built bootstrap bundle. Its SHA-256 is the `sha256` carried by the `compilers` entries of the recipe `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:186]`. Identical compiler bytes in both hosts are the first determinism guarantee.
- **One implementation:** the DS lot V13a ("island contract and compile kit" `[FAIT: PLAN:597]`) and the sentropic lot L2-compiler `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:420]` describe overlapping work. This plan gives the implementation to cluster-mesh (owner decision: owner and merger = s-conductor `[FAIT: NIGHT:175]`) and re-scopes V13a to the island profile, the imported-type bundles and the reference corpus. **DS agreement: obtained** on 13/11 at about 11:50 `[FAIT: lane brief, section DS AGREEMENT]` — the mirror owns the import map and the signed manifest and never builds; V13a is re-scoped as written here, with no `dist-esm` or CDN bundle. The agreement carries four commitments of the DS (section 2.5). An agreement and a commitment are not deliveries: what stays to confirm is the format of the types entry, the dates, and the evidence of each delivery (sections 5.10 and 8.3). Revisions 1 to 3 stated this agreement as not obtained.

### 2.5 Module graph, import map and dependency resolution

`[JUGEMENT]` unless tagged.

- **Source graph.** A recipe carries exact source bytes at normalised relative paths `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:184,194]`. Three kinds of specifier resolve inside it: relative imports, declared aliases and workspace packages. Everything else is a bare specifier.
- **Aliases resolve from the admitted snapshot. A workspace package resolves in one declared mode: compiled from its sources in the snapshot, or taken from the mirror at a declared version — never by an implicit fallback.** `[FAIT]` Immo's UI imports three private workspaces that export TypeScript sources: `@radar/domain` (39 import statements in 32 non-test files), `@radar/scoring` (5) and `@radar/sources/municipalities` (3) `[immo:packages/radar-domain/package.json:4,8-13; immo:ui/package.json:18-20; git grep on immo:ui/src]`. Its Focus view is one of the importers `[immo:ui/src/lib/components/maps/SignauxMapView.svelte:169-174]`. Its build also relies on two aliases: `$lib` → `ui/src/lib`, and, in artifact mode only, the replacement of the empty module `./lib/net/artifact-boot` by the one that installs the reader transport `[immo:ui/vite.config.ts:83-89; immo:ui/src/lib/net/artifact-boot.ts:7; immo:ui/src/lib/net/artifact-boot.artifact.ts:6-10]`. A private package cannot come from an npm mirror, and without the replacement the built artifact reaches no reader host. `[FAIT]` Geo's local packages are the opposite case. The site imports `@sentropic/geo-core`, `@sentropic/geo-sources-americas`, `@sentropic/geo-sources-europe` and the subpath `@sentropic/geo/catalog` `[geo:apps/site/src/lib/catalog.ts:13; geo:apps/site/src/routes/sources/+page.ts:1-3]`. They export `./dist/*.js` `[geo:packages/geo-core/package.json:16-23; geo:packages/geo-map-engine/package.json:16-23]`; `dist/` is ignored by git `[geo:.gitignore:6]` and exists only after a build of every workspace, which the production workflow runs before the site `[geo:.github/workflows/pages.yml:60-66]`. The lockfile records such a package as a link, with neither version nor integrity `[geo:package-lock.json:1764-1767]`. The same packages are also published, and a published version is not the workspace at a commit: immo's lockfile resolves `@sentropic/geo-core` 0.1.2 and `@sentropic/geo-map-engine` 0.6.3 from the npm registry, while both workspaces are at 0.6.5 `[immo:package-lock.json:2398-2400,2404-2406; geo:packages/geo-core/package.json:3]`. The rule of revision 2, which read `exports` only, found no file for them in a snapshot. `[JUGEMENT]` Rules:
  1. *Workspace map and selection.* The host builds the map at the snapshot commit from the workspace manifests, which it reads itself: package manifests are refused paths for a session `[FAIT: immo:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/registry.ts:28-29]`, so they are host inputs and never recipe sources. Each workspace package the graph reaches resolves in exactly one mode. The mode is selected by a fixed priority; the first match wins, nothing is probed on a file system and nothing falls back:
     - (a) *declared `source`*. The descriptor (version 2) lists the package with a closed table "exported subpath → source path in the snapshot" (geo: `.` → `packages/geo-core/src/index.ts`). The package is compiled from those sources;
     - (b) *declared `mirror`*. The descriptor lists the package with one exact published version. It resolves as a closure package from the mirror manifest, like a third-party package, and the copy in the snapshot is not read;
     - (c) *implicit `source`*. No declaration, and every `exports` target the graph reaches is a file of the snapshot — immo's `@radar/*`, which export `./src/*.ts`. Compiled from sources;
     - (d) otherwise the build is refused with `workspace_unresolved`, naming the package and the missing target. A workspace package never comes from the mirror without a declaration: the lockfile pins nothing for it.

     A module reached in a `source` mode is a compile-read source of the build plan like any other (section 2.8): it is compiled, not installed, and it must pass rule 5. Under `mirror` the artifact carries the published bytes, which may differ from the sources at the commit: the receipt states the mode, so the difference is declared and never silent.
  2. *Aliases.* The descriptor (version 2) declares a closed table with two forms: a path-prefix alias, and an exact module replacement tied to a build profile. No pattern, no function, no file-system probe.
  3. *Extensions and JSON.* A `.js` specifier that names a `.ts` source resolves to it, and an extensionless relative specifier is tried against a fixed ordered list: the two conventions of immo's sources `[FAIT: immo:ui/src/main.ts:11; immo:ui/src/lib/components/maps/SignauxMapView.svelte:34]`. A local `.json` import is emitted as a module whose default export is the parsed document `[FAIT: immo:packages/radar-sources/src/municipalities.ts:15; geo:apps/site/src/lib/catalog.ts:14]`.
  4. *Identity.* The workspace map, the alias table and the extension list are host-derived entries of the recipe `options`, which are part of the recipe key `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:191]`. For each workspace package the map records its mode, and with it the subpath table (`source`) or the exact version and the digest of its entry in the mirror manifest (`mirror`). The resolved sources are recipe `sources`. Changing a mode, a table, a version or a source changes the key, and the receipt lists mode, version and digests per package.
  5. *Refusals.* A reachable workspace module that imports a Node built-in is refused with `unsupported_import`. Seven files of `radar-sources` do; the only subpath the UI imports reaches none of them `[FAIT: git grep "node:" on immo:packages/radar-sources/src; immo:packages/radar-sources/src/municipalities.ts:14-15]`. `import.meta.env` is a Vite construct, read at eight keys by the UI (`git grep` on `immo:ui/src`): the candidate reads a runtime configuration module instead (VD-5.1), and the linker refuses the construct at compile time.
  6. *Witness.* A build in artifact profile contains the transport installer and relays an application call through the reader host: gate of VD-2.12 on a fixture, gate G5a on immo. A package that exports build outputs builds from a clean snapshot, with no `dist` directory, in each declared mode, and is refused when it is not declared: gate of VD-2.12 on a fixture, gate G6 on geo's own packages.
- **Every other bare specifier resolves only through the mirror manifest:** an import map (specifier → immutable URL) plus, for every resource, byte length, MIME type, SHA-256 and SRI `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:189]`. An unlisted specifier is the typed refusal `unsupported_import` `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:145]`.
- **The mirror** is the SRI-pinned ESM mirror at OVH BHS agreed with the DS `[FAIT: .tmp/builder-dossier/ds-needs.md:6; DOSSIER:403]` and confirmed as design in the h2a spec: s-conductor delivers it, k8s operates its storage, the DS supplies the closure through its normal npm publication `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:334]`. It is still an open owner decision in the builder dossier `[FAIT: DOSSIER:401]`; decision D9 closes it.
- **The mirror never builds.** It syncs lock-pinned npm tarballs, verifies their integrity, extracts the admitted browser-ESM files, records digests and signs the manifest. A package that needs a bundling step to become browser ESM gets that step in its own publisher's CI and is published to npm in that form. The supply chain stays: npm tarball integrity → mirror digest → SRI in the page.
- **What the DS agreed and committed to** `[FAIT: lane brief, section DS AGREEMENT, 13/11 ~11:50]`. Agreed: the mirror owns the import map and the signed manifest (specifier → immutable URL, bytes, MIME type, SHA-256, SRI) and never builds. Committed, each row being a commitment and not yet a verified delivery:

  | # | Commitment of the DS | State read in round 4 | Carried in the schedule by |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | Every transitive dependency of the DS is published as browser ESM with explicit `.js` specifiers, enforced by a new publish gate that walks the closure. `@sentropic/design-system-elements` stays Svelte-compiled ESM until V12a and V12b, so its closure includes `svelte/internal` | The gate is announced as new; no run of it was read: `unverified`. `elements` 0.2.0 depends on `svelte` `[FAIT: ds4:packages/elements/package.json:3,57]` | Node `DS-ESM`, a prerequisite of the lots whose build reads DS packages from the mirror: VD-2.5, VD-2.6, VD-5.1 and VD-6.2. Until V12b a closure that contains `elements` carries the Svelte runtime: G1 already waits for V12b |
  | 2 | CSS stays a separate export | True today `[FAIT: ds:packages/components-vue/package.json:26]` | Nothing to deliver; section 2.6 |
  | 3 | Each package gets one rolled-up, self-contained types entry (for example `./types` → `dist/types.bundle.d.ts`). The exact format — one `.d.ts` per package, or a JSON map from specifier to `.d.ts` text — is fixed by the spike VD-2.0 and sent to the DS | Not delivered; the format is open | Question 5 of VD-2.0, then V13a, which now has VD-2.0 as a prerequisite |
  | 4 | `vue-publish` refuses to publish when a dependency version is not on npm. The gate exists because `next.1` went out before `themes` 0.14.0 and `diagram-canvas` 0.2.0 | Confirmed for `next.1`: the two versions were not on npm at the read (section 3.6). The gate itself: no run read, `unverified` | Node `R-DEPS` for `next.1`; node `DS-ESM` for the gate |
- **Resolution in the page.** The HTML shell carries an inline import map and `modulepreload` links with `integrity` for every module of the graph. The application CSP allows `script-src 'self'` only `[FAIT: sen:packages/artifacts/src/content/headers.ts:38-53]`, so every module is served from the artifact's own origin: closure files are entries of the release manifest (section 2.3, point 1). The inline import map is admitted through the existing per-release script hashes `[FAIT: sen:packages/artifacts/src/content/headers.ts:57-60]`.
- **Imported types.** `defineProps<Imported>()` needs type resolution. The DS build passes a file-system hook to the compiler today `[FAIT: ds:scripts/vue-sfc-build.mjs:34-37]`. In V8 the hook reads the virtual graph and a declared type bundle shipped with the admitted packages `[FAIT: PLAN:372]`. Qualified by the spike (VD-2.0), which also fixes the format of that bundle and sends it to the DS (commitment 3 above).
- **Closure shape.** A release accepts at most 255 files, 16 MiB per file and 64 MiB in total `[FAIT: sen:packages/artifacts/src/server/build-output.ts:20-24]`. Third-party packages enter the closure as one file per entry point; DS packages as per-component files, the rule the DS accepted for its Vue package `[FAIT: .tmp/builder-dossier/q1-findings.md:8]`. Whether the file ceiling must be raised for V8 releases is measured by VD-2.10.

### 2.6 CSS, assets, workers, sourcemaps

`[JUGEMENT]` unless tagged.

- **CSS.** SFC `<style>` blocks are compiled by the SFC compiler; each unit emits an ordered list of CSS files `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:134]`. DS styles come from the closure as a separate file `[FAIT: ds:packages/components-vue/package.json:26]`. No preprocessor, no Tailwind scan, no source-selected plugin `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:163]`. The application CSP allows inline styles and same-origin fonts `[FAIT: sen:packages/artifacts/src/content/headers.ts:41,43]`.
- **Stylesheets outside SFC blocks.** `[FAIT]` The sources to migrate use three constructs that an import map cannot serve, because it maps JavaScript specifiers only:
  - a stylesheet imported from JavaScript, by path or by package name: `import "./app.css"` and `import "@fontsource-variable/inter"`, whose package entry is a CSS file `[immo:ui/src/main.ts:7-8]`; `import '@sentropic/chat-ui/content.css'`, required by the app plan `[APP-SPEC:517]`;
  - a package stylesheet imported from CSS: `@import "@sentropic/design-system-themes/css/sent-tech.css"` `[immo:ui/src/app.css:9; geo:apps/site/src/app.css:3]` and `@import "maplibre-gl/dist/maplibre-gl.css"` `[geo:apps/site/src/app.css:5]`;
  - font files referenced by a relative `url()` inside a package stylesheet: the Inter package, version 5.2.8 `[immo:package-lock.json:1554-1555]`, has 42 font files under `./files/` (files read at `radar-immobilier/tmp/focus-signal-main/node_modules/@fontsource-variable/inter/`).

  `[JUGEMENT]` Rules, all in the linker:
  1. *Classification.* An import whose resolved resource has a CSS MIME type, in the source graph or in the mirror manifest, is a stylesheet dependency of the importing module, whatever the spelling of the specifier. The statement is removed from the emitted module. A default or named import from a stylesheet is refused.
  2. *`@import`.* Its specifier goes through the resolver of section 2.5 and is rewritten to the same-origin URL of the resolved file. Files are not inlined, so the conditions and layers of the rule keep their native meaning.
  3. *`url()`.* Inside a closure stylesheet, relative references stay as written: the closure keeps the directory layout of the package, so they resolve against the stylesheet's own URL. Inside a source stylesheet, a reference to a local asset is rewritten to the emitted URL of that asset. A URL to another origin is refused.
  4. *Cascade order.* The shell lists the stylesheets as `link` elements in one deterministic order: depth-first order of the static import graph from the entry, each file at its first occurrence, the `<style>` blocks of a module after the stylesheets that module imports. Stylesheets reached only through a dynamic import follow, in discovery order. For the static graph this is the order of a bundled build; for dynamic imports it replaces a load-time order with a fixed one.
  5. *Tailwind directives* (`@import "tailwindcss"`, `@source`, `@theme` `[FAIT: immo:ui/src/app.css:10-15]`) are not processed: decision D3.
  6. *Tooling.* The rewriting is a first-party pass on the syntax tree of the CSS parser that the SFC compiler already contains and opens to its callers `[FAIT: sent-tech-design-system/node_modules/@vue/compiler-sfc/dist/compiler-sfc.d.ts:240-241, version 3.5.35; its browser build includes the parser]`. Same structure at the 3.6 pin: `unverified` (VD-2.0, question 11).
  7. *Verification.* Computed styles of a fixed witness list equal those of the Vite build of the same sources; the variable font is reported as loaded from the artifact's origin; an order witness (two rules of equal specificity in two files) resolves as in the Vite build. Gates G-V8-PREVIEW, G-V8-APP and G5a.
- **Assets.** Local assets are sources of the recipe and are emitted under their digest `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:184,196]`. The linker accepts a closed set of import forms: `new URL('./file', import.meta.url)`; the `?raw` suffix on a local file; and the `?url` suffix on a local file or on a closure file listed in the mirror manifest, which resolves to the same-origin URL of that file — the form immo's PDF view already uses `[FAIT: immo:ui/src/lib/components/maps/SignalPdfOverlay.svelte:21]`. Any other form is refused, `?worker` included (see Workers). Runtime data (tiles, GeoJSON, PDF) stays a runtime read through the artifact's reader operations; it is not a build input.
- **Workers.** At compile time there is none: the isolate compiles and never executes user code. At run time the application CSP allows same-origin workers and refuses `blob:` workers `[FAIT: sen:packages/artifacts/src/content/headers.ts:32-36,46]`; a worker enforces the policy of its own script response, which every application asset carries `[FAIT: sen:packages/artifacts/src/content/headers.ts:94-96]`. A worker does not use the import map of the document that starts it: "A global object has an import map, initially an empty import map. For now, only Window global objects have their import map modified from the initial empty one" `[FAIT: HTML Standard, html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html, "Realms, settings objects, and global objects", read on 13/11]`; the three engines are witnessed by VD-2.0, question 6. A worker graph must therefore be closed without the import map. Two cases:
  1. *Third-party worker: a complete pre-built closure.* The mirror admits a worker entry only with its whole static import closure, and only if every import inside that closure is a relative URL to a file of the same package. A bare specifier inside a worker closure refuses the package (`unsupported_import`). `[FAIT]` Both workers immo uses pass the rule as published. MapLibre 6.4.1 ships three ES modules; the runtime and the worker each have exactly one static import, `./maplibre-gl-shared.mjs`, which has none (`ml:dist/maplibre-gl.mjs`, `ml:dist/maplibre-gl-worker.mjs`, `ml:dist/maplibre-gl-shared.mjs`: the only `from"…"` clauses of the three files). The pdf.js worker is one file with no import (`pdf:build/pdf.worker.mjs`, `pdf:build/pdf.worker.min.mjs`). `[JUGEMENT]` The closure keeps the three MapLibre files side by side, so the worker and its shared module resolve by relative URL on the same origin.
  2. *Application worker: a compiled entry linked without the import map.* A worker written in the source graph is linked in a second mode, `resolved-url`: every specifier of its graph, closure imports included, is rewritten to a relative URL at link time. A module shared with the document graph is emitted a second time under the worker's path, because its bytes differ. The mode is a recipe option, hence part of the key. `[FAIT]` Neither immo's UI nor geo has such a worker today (`git grep "new Worker"` on `immo:ui/src`, `geo:apps/site/src` and `geo:packages/geo-ui-svelte/src`: no hit outside tests). The case is qualified on a fixture.

  Worker URL: the application gives MapLibre its worker URL, the contract `geo-ui-svelte` already imposes `[FAIT: geo:packages/geo-ui-svelte/src/maplibre-worker.ts:36-42]`. The Vite-only `?worker&url` import `[FAIT: immo:ui/src/lib/maps/maplibre-worker.ts:7]` becomes a `?url` import of the closure file, the form that package's own documentation names `[FAIT: geo:packages/geo-ui-svelte/src/maplibre-worker.ts:8-9]`. Gate: a worker test loads the transitive imports of the worker, not only its entry file (G-V8-PREVIEW, items 2 to 4). Live: live serving refuses workers today (`worker-src 'none'` `[FAIT: sen:apps/artifact-content/src/live.ts:43]`); V8 live uses the application policy (`worker-src 'self'`), without the `blob:` profile that the execution-node data plane of step 1 adds for Vite `[FAIT: c1:apps/artifact-content/README.md:96]`. Outside the qualified set: the MapLibre paths that build a `blob:` URL — a worker URL on another origin, and a script that is not an `.mjs` file handed to `importScriptInWorkers` (`ml:dist/maplibre-gl.mjs`, `ml:dist/maplibre-gl-worker.mjs`: the `URL.createObjectURL(new Blob(…))` branches).
- **Sourcemaps.** One external map per emitted module, as a recipe option, hence part of the key `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:191]`. Maps contain sources: they are private outputs, served only to authorised readers and never embedded in the offline export.

### 2.7 Cache by digest and determinism

- **Keys** `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:175,196]`: the recipe key is the SHA-256 of a domain prefix and the JCS encoding of the closed recipe; each output file has its own SHA-256; the output manifest has its own digest. One recipe key maps to one immutable manifest; different bytes for the same key are a reproducibility failure, never an overwrite.
- **Levels** `[JUGEMENT]`: (1) recipe key → manifest in the tenant cache of the host, disk-backed and bounded `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:218]`; (2) inside a build plan, a per-module memo in two stages (below); (3) in the browser, Cache Storage keyed by the same digests; (4) over HTTP, release files are immutable by digest.
- **Memo identity** `[JUGEMENT]`. Source and toolchain digests alone are not an identity: scoped-style identifiers derive from the path, imported types are inputs of an SFC, and several profiles and option sets coexist. Two stages, each keyed by the SHA-256 of the JCS encoding of its inputs:
  1. *Compile memo*, per module. Key: virtual path; source digest; kind; profile; digest of the effective options; toolchain digest; and, for each type the module resolves from another file or from a type bundle, the digest of that file or bundle. Output: the compiled module with unresolved specifiers, its specifier list and its CSS.
  2. *Link memo*, per module. Key: the compile key; and the resolution of each of its specifiers under the active link mode — target path or URL, and the digest of the target when the emitted URL carries it (assets). Output: the final bytes.
  3. *Block memo*, inside the compile of one SFC of the live profile, for the latency of live edits (section 2.13). The script, the template and each style block are compiled apart — in the live profile the template is not inlined in the script — and memoised apart. Revision 5 keyed each result by the digest of its own block and derived the scope identifier from the whole source. Both were wrong (round-5 review, finding 2): the blocks of an SFC are not independent, and an identifier that follows the source changes the CSS selectors and the template at every edit.

     `[FAIT]` At the pin, the compiler makes these inputs cross the blocks `[sfc36:dist/compiler-sfc.cjs.js, lines in the table]`:

     | Result | Depends, beyond its own block, on | Lines |
     |---|---|---|
     | Template | the binding metadata that the script exposes; the scope identifier, passed only when a style is `scoped`; whether a scoped style contains `:slotted(` | 3285-3290; 1531-1532 |
     | Script | for a TypeScript `<script setup>`, which of its imported bindings the template uses — the package ships that test as `hmrShouldReload`; the list of the `v-bind()` expressions of the style blocks, injected in the script as CSS variables whose names derive from the scope identifier; in Vapor output, the names of the `<style module>` blocks | 15785-15787 and 1624-1628; 1530, 16040-16043 and 110-113; 15830-15836 and 16060 |
     | Style block | the scope identifier, when the block is `scoped` | 11966-11974 |

     The scope identifier itself is an option of the caller, not a value the compiler computes `[FAIT: sfc36:dist/compiler-sfc.cjs.js:15748]`, and the browser build of the compiler refuses `<style module>` `[FAIT: sfc36:dist/compiler-sfc.esm-browser.js:41511]`.

     `[JUGEMENT]` Rules:
     - *A stable scope identifier.* It is derived from the virtual path of the SFC alone — the DS build already passes the relative path `[FAIT: ds4:scripts/vue-sfc-build.mjs:35]` — in every profile, and it is the hot-replacement identifier of the live profile (section 2.9). No edit moves it; the same bytes at another path get another one.
     - *Keys.* Each result is keyed by the compile key of item 1 with, in place of the source digest, the digest of its own block and the digest of every input that the table gives it: for the template, the binding metadata, the `scoped` state and the `:slotted` flag; for the script, the set of its imported bindings that the template uses and the ordered list of the `v-bind()` expressions; for a style block, its attributes. The module is assembled from the results. An unchanged script result makes the revision a re-render of the component; a changed one, a re-creation.
     - *What an edit recompiles.* A *plain style edit* — one that changes neither the `v-bind()` list, nor a `scoped` attribute, nor the `:slotted` flag — recompiles that style block alone. A *plain template edit* — one that leaves unchanged the set of imported bindings the template uses — recompiles the template alone. A *script edit* recompiles the script, and the template when the binding metadata changed. A *crossing edit* recompiles what the table names: a style edit that changes the `v-bind()` list, the script and that block; one that changes the `scoped` state or the `:slotted` flag, the template and that block; a template edit that starts or stops using an imported binding, the script and the template.
     - *Fail closed.* Whatever the table does not name recompiles the whole SFC: a block with a `src` attribute, a custom block, a parse error, a change of the block list. A `<style module>` block is refused in version 1.
     - *Equality.* For every edit of a fixed matrix — each case above, on each fixture — the emitted bytes are those of a compile of the whole SFC in a fresh context. The gate of VD-2.15 asserts it with the counters of each case. The table was read on the package; on the Vapor output of the pin it is `unverified` until the spike runs the same matrix (VD-2.0, question 13). A dependency that the matrix finds and the table lacks is added to the keys before VD-2.15 starts.

  Invalidation: the plan keeps a reverse index (importers, type importers, asset users) and recomputes the keys of the dependents of a changed input. Four cases are distinguished, and the gate of VD-2.7 asserts each: a body or template edit recompiles one module and nothing else; an edit of a type that K modules resolve recompiles 1 + K modules; the same bytes at another path are a different module and are compiled again; a profile or option change invalidates every module. An asset change re-links its users and recompiles nothing.
- **Determinism requirements** `[JUGEMENT]`: identical bootstrap bundle bytes in both hosts; scope identifiers derived from the virtual path alone, never from the content of the file and never from a counter (item 3 above); no clock, random source or locale in the core; sorted iteration wherever order reaches output bytes; one hashing implementation.
- **Same commit, same digest** `[JUGEMENT]`: the three pins a build uses — mirror manifest, compiler bundle, compile policy revision — are named by the commit it builds, so that a commit determines its recipe keys and its root manifest digest, or is refused (section 2.12, rule 4).
- **Known hazard** `[JUGEMENT]`: path normalisation to NFC `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:194]` depends on the Unicode tables of the engine, which may differ between the Node isolate and three browser engines. Until the spike proves equality, version 1 restricts source paths to the characters `A-Z a-z 0-9 . _ / -` and refuses any other path with `invalid_source_path`.
- **Gate:** byte equality of the output manifest between the isolate and Chromium, Firefox and WebKit on the reference corpus (G-V8-a, section 5).

### 2.8 Security boundary, tenancy and devScope

`[FAIT]` Already fixed by the h2a spec, as a target that sentropic confirmed at design level:

- All version-1 compilation is isolated, first-party code included `[H2A-SPEC:153]`.
- Limits per compile: 128 source files, 2 MiB of sources, 64 output files, 4 MiB of outputs; isolate memory 128 MiB by default and 256 MiB at most; one whole-request deadline, 5 s by default and 30 s at most; one active compile per tenant, two per host `[H2A-SPEC:303-324]`.
- No file, database, network, environment or credential bridge; a same-process isolate is not OS-level confinement `[H2A-SPEC:224,236]`.
- The execution context is built by the host — `tenantId, workspaceId, principalId, …, devScope, virtualSourceManifest, …` — and is never supplied by a caller `[H2A-SPEC:137]`. Private outputs are partitioned as `private-v1:<tenantId>:<workspaceId>:<devScopeDigest>` `[H2A-SPEC:192]`.

`[FAIT]` What the code offers today:

- Runtime ceilings of 60 s and 256 MiB, defaults of 30 s and 128 MiB `[sen:packages/skills/src/sandbox/policy.ts:8-20]`.
- A new isolate per call `[sen:packages/skills/src/sandbox/runtime.ts:372-374]`; a timeout that covers the script run only `[sen:packages/skills/src/sandbox/runtime.ts:394-398]`; a `signal` option that is declared and never read `[sen:packages/skills/src/sandbox/runtime.ts:96, single occurrence in the file]`.
- No egress by construction: inside the isolate, `files`, `db` and `fetch` exist only when the host injects an adapter `[sen:packages/skills/src/sandbox/runtime.ts:192-200]`, and a test asserts that `require`, `process` and `fs` are undefined `[sen:packages/skills/tests/sandbox/runtime.test.ts:109-118]`. The compile profile injects none.
- No production caller: document generation in the API still runs on `node:vm` `[sen:api/src/services/docx-generation.ts:6]`.

`[JUGEMENT]` Additions of this plan:

- **Identifier.** The h2a spec names the isolate-profile amendment `AMD-17` `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:206,285]`. On `main` that number already designates the memory-floor amendment `[FAIT: sen:spec/SPEC_EVOL_SANDBOX_NODE.md:18; DOSSIER:780]`, and the builder dossier has reached `AMD-27` `[FAIT: DOSSIER:790]`. This plan writes `AMD-ISO` until s-conductor assigns the next free number.
- **Browser mode.** Compilation there needs no server-side isolation: the worker runs the pinned compiler over sources held as data. The exposure is the preview, which executes compiled code. It never runs in the origin of the product UI — the principle that keeps untrusted output away from authority-bearing origins `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:232]`. It does not run in an opaque-origin frame either, the choice of revision 1: such a frame can start neither library worker. The preview is a top-level document on the public origin of the artifact, served there as a live session: a tuple origin, distinct from the origin of the product UI (section 2.11).
- **A browser build proves nothing to the server.** Scope, limits and integrity are enforced where authority lives. At publication the server reads the sources itself, re-derives the outputs in the isolate and compares manifest digests (decision D4). A draft pushed by a browser is served for preview only and never becomes a release.
- **devScope: compile read, write and publication are three separate authorisations.** The effective scope of a session is computed at admission, separately for reading and for writing, and is never a caller argument `[FAIT: CTX-SPEC:88,500]`.
  - *Write.* For immo the ceiling is the registry file, referenced by repository, path, commit and digest `[FAIT: CTX-SPEC:92,340]`: writable `ui/**` and three `packages/radar-*/src/**` globs, with refused paths taking precedence `[FAIT: immo:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/registry.ts:15-39]`. A draft write of a live session and a commit on an artifact branch are writes and stay inside it. This plan names no other path. A file that a live session must edit and that lies outside the ceiling stays read-only until the registry itself changes: that is an owner decision in immo's repository (decision D11). It takes effect through a graph build at the new commit and fresh admissions; a running session keeps its pinned commit and digest and is invalidated, never silently updated `[FAIT: CTX-SPEC:667-669]`.
  - *Compile read.* A recipe contains the sources the scope can read for compilation: blobs of the admitted repository and commit, inside the source subtree and outside the refused paths, with no writable-pattern requirement; caller-submitted blobs under an explicit grant; mirror dependencies `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:137,210; rv-sentropic.md:153-157]`. The referenced registry constrains writing only: what lies outside its write list stays eligible as read-only context `[FAIT: CTX-SPEC:669]`. A build may therefore compile a module that the session cannot edit.
  - *Publication.* It has its own destination authorisation, checked at the publication boundary and never inferred from the scope `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:137; rv-sentropic.md:158]`.
- **Residency.** On the server path, tenant sources and outputs stay inside the BHS boundary `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:202]`. In browser mode the sources stay in the authenticated user's browser; the built outputs, and the sourcemaps when the option is on, are pushed to the draft store of the session, inside the same boundary (section 2.11). No public CDN at run time `[FAIT: .tmp/builder-dossier/ds-needs.md:6]`.

### 2.9 App-scale builds and live sessions

`[JUGEMENT]` The compile contract was sized for a dossier renderer. An application exceeds one unit: the immo UI has 69 `.svelte` files (28,628 lines) and 116 non-test `.ts` modules `[FAIT: immo: git ls-tree and wc -l on ui/src; PLAN:144-145]`. Two additions make it buildable without changing the per-unit limits:

1. **Build plan.** The source graph is partitioned into units under the version-1 limits (by directory, then by size). Each unit is compiled independently; imports that cross units stay external and are resolved by the generated import map, exactly as closure imports are. The root manifest lists the recipe keys and manifest digests of the units, the import map, the shell and the asset inventory; its digest identifies the build. Admission is granted once for the plan, with one whole-build deadline; units run one at a time per tenant, as the per-tenant limit requires `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:315]`.
2. **No bundle.** The output is one module per source module: no tree-shaking across units and no minification in version 1. The load cost is addressed by preload links and immutable caching, and it is measured against the Vite-built output of the same sources (VD-2.10), not assumed.

**Live sessions without a pod.** Today a live session is a Vite dev server inside a builder pod `[FAIT: sen:api/src/services/artifacts/live/recipes.ts:67-69]`; step 1, stopped by the owner on 13/11, would have run it inside a VM sandbox `[FAIT: DOSSIER:4]`. The builder pool cannot be retired while live sessions need it. Target: a draft write recompiles the modules it touches (in the worker in browser mode, in the isolate in server mode) and switches the draft manifest of the session. The content host serves the draft outputs by digest, behind the edge of the consumer's host and on the public origin of the artifact, under the application CSP (section 2.11), and announces each new revision on the session's existing same-origin socket path `[FAIT: sen:apps/artifact-content/src/live.ts:33,38-43]`. There is no dev server and no pod. The input of a live revision is a commit plus the draft of the session (section 2.12); the path of one edit, stage by stage and with its targets, is section 2.13. Two pod-bound contracts change with it: the live assertion that the consumer's host signs names a pod today `[FAIT: sen:apps/artifact-content/src/live-assertion.ts:130-136]`, and the edge admits the URL shapes of a Vite dev server only `[FAIT: sen:apps/artifact-content/README.md:49]`. VD-2.9 gives both a form without a pod; each host then adopts it — VD-5.4 for immo, VD-6.3 for the sentropic host.

**Hot replacement is a version-1 requirement.** `[FAIT]` The consumer contract makes a full reload a blocking failure of an incremental edit and judges the visible DOM: gate G-WRITE, N = 20, 0 reload, visible DOM p95 ≤ 250 ms (maximum 500 ms) or ≤ 500 ms according to decision D4 of the builder dossier; the consumer's contractual bound is p95 < 5.0 s `[DOSSIER:575]`. Revision 1 reloaded the page and measured the readiness of the manifest: it changed a consumer requirement without an amendment. `[JUGEMENT]` V8 live keeps the requirement and replaces components in place:

1. The live profile of the linker gives every SFC a stable identifier — the scope identifier of section 2.7, derived from its virtual path alone — and registers it with the hot-replacement runtime of the Vue development build. A bundler plugin emits that registration today; the linker emits it itself.
2. On a revision, the client runtime — a first-party module of the live shell — imports each changed module from a same-origin URL and hands it to the Vue runtime, which re-renders or re-creates the affected component instances. The URL is the pre-armed patch request of section 2.13 when one is pending, and a revision-stamped URL otherwise. A style-only revision is applied from the frame itself, with no request. What lies outside the replaced components is untouched. `[FAIT]` What the pinned runtime keeps inside them depends on the edit, as read in round 8. A re-render of a Vapor component runs its render function again in a fresh scope: the state of the component itself is kept, and the components that its template holds are created again. A re-creation of a Vapor component under Vapor parents re-renders its parent: the components that this parent holds — the siblings of the edited one — are created again as well. Under a classic parent the instance alone is re-created `[rt36:@vue/runtime-vapor/dist/runtime-vapor.esm-bundler.js:3907-3947,3968-3972,4268-4278; rt36:@vue/runtime-core/dist/runtime-core.esm-bundler.js:536-554,581-617,6021-6031]`. `[JUGEMENT]` A state witness of the consumer protocol — map camera, selection, open overlay — is therefore kept when it lies outside the instances that an edit creates again, or when the page holds it outside the component tree. Which witness survives which edit kind is `unverified`: question 10 of VD-2.0 answers it, and a witness that does not survive follows the rule of the amendment stated below.
3. A changed module that is not a component is re-imported together with its importers up to the nearest component boundaries. Only their specifiers are re-linked (link memo, section 2.7); nothing else is recompiled.
4. A change that reaches no component boundary — a module evaluated once at start-up, the shell, the import map — reloads the page. In the edit set of a gate it counts as a failure; elsewhere it is reported.
5. Live runs the development build of the Vue runtime; publication runs the production build. Live/publish parity is a parity of rendering, as it is today between the Vite dev server and the Vite build.

The gates measure the consumer's clock, not only the server's. Revision 4 put one bound on it: visible DOM p95 ≤ 500 ms, with ≤ 250 ms reported. The owner's answer of 13/11 replaces that bound by the stage budget of section 2.13: the V8 update — from the edit received to the patched module ready — and each processing step after it take less than 10 ms, at p50 and at p95 — the replacement in the page among them, whole, with the component's own render inside it; the network and the display have numbers of their own and are inside no claimed figure. The budget is carried by VD-2.15, VD-2.16 and VD-2.13 on fixtures, by gate G-LIVE-MS, then by G5a on the ported Focus views. The consumer protocol of G-WRITE (N = 20, 0 full reload, state witnesses intact) is run unchanged on the same build: its thresholds are wider than this budget and replace none of its criteria. Whether the pinned Vapor runtime replaces a re-imported component without a bundler, and which state witness each edit kind keeps (item 2), is `unverified` (VD-2.0, question 10). If it does not replace, the requirement is not dropped: the change goes to immo as an explicit amendment of G-WRITE, with its consequence (page state lost at every edit) and its saving (the 28 h of VD-2.13), and V8 live does not replace the pod builder for immo before immo answers. If it replaces and a witness of the protocol is lost for an edit kind, that loss goes to immo in the same way, with the edit kind and the witness named: no gate counts as intact a state that the replacement re-creates.

`[FAIT]` Targets set by the owner: an artifact builds in less than one minute `[NIGHT:154]`; a live session answers in less than one second, a statement whose clock — the warm opening of a session, from the accepted request to the useful render — is decision D4 of the builder dossier `[DOSSIER:182-184]`; and, since 13/11, a live update made by an AI takes a few milliseconds (header). `[JUGEMENT]` For the target, the third statement governs edits, and section 2.13 budgets every stage on both clocks. The opening of a session is measured and reported by gate G-LIVE-MS, cold and warm, against the bound of the builder dossier; this plan adds no gate on it. Decision D4 of the builder dossier set the thresholds of the step-1 path, which the owner stopped on 13/11: its bound stays the reference that the report of G-LIVE-MS quotes, and nothing else of it applies to the target.

### 2.10 A Vue Vapor SFC build in V8, compared with the current immo build

| Need | Vue Vapor SFC build in V8 (target) | Current immo whole-app build (Svelte, Vite) |
|---|---|---|
| Compiler | `@vue/compiler-sfc` at the exact 3.6 pin (browser ESM build) and Sucrase, both pure JavaScript `[FAIT: PLAN:370-371]` | Vite 6 with native esbuild and Rollup binaries, the Svelte plugin, and a custom plugin that reads `node_modules` through `node:fs` `[FAIT: immo:ui/package.json:35,45; immo:package-lock.json:1215,2029; immo:ui/vite-plugins/lucide-deep-imports.ts:17-18]` |
| Dependencies | Import map against the pinned mirror; nothing installed | `npm ci` on the root lockfile (854 `node_modules` entries, the API workspace included `[FAIT: immo:package.json:8-12; grep -c on immo:package-lock.json]`), then `npm run build:artifact` `[FAIT: immo:sentropic.artifact.json:3-6; immo:package.json:53; sen:api/src/services/artifacts/live/recipes.ts:62-63,91-92]` |
| File system, processes | A virtual source graph; no process | The Node file system and a wrapper that spawns a child process `[FAIT: immo:tools/make/ui-artifact-env.mjs:10-18]` |
| CSS | SFC styles, DS files and stylesheet imports resolved by the linker (section 2.6); no Tailwind | Tailwind 4.3.3 scanning `./**/*.{svelte,ts}` with a native scanner `[FAIT: immo:ui/src/app.css:10-12; immo:ui/package.json:36,44; immo:package-lock.json:2795]` |
| Workers | Complete closures on the artifact origin, loaded without the import map (section 2.6) | Vite `?worker&url` and `?url` imports `[FAIT: immo:ui/src/lib/maps/maplibre-worker.ts:7; immo:ui/src/lib/components/maps/SignalPdfOverlay.svelte:21]` |
| Memory | An isolate of 128 MiB by default, 256 MiB at most, per compile `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:312]` | `memory.peak` 1,516 MiB and Node RSS 1,314 MiB for 1,338 modules in 10.02 s (median of 3 runs, 2 CPUs) `[FAIT: FFO:4]` |
| Unit of work | A module: a body edit recompiles one module, a type edit also the modules that resolve that type (section 2.7) | The whole application: `build:artifact` compiles `ui/index.html → App.svelte` with every view imported `[FAIT: DOSSIER:88]` |
| Output | Modules, CSS and a manifest, by digest | A Rollup bundle under `ui/dist` `[FAIT: immo:sentropic.artifact.json:4]` |

Why the current immo UI cannot build in V8 before its migration `[JUGEMENT, each point resting on a fact above]`:

1. The compile service admits two kinds, `vue-sfc` and `vue-template` `[FAIT: NIGHT:175; H2A-SPEC:185]`. No Svelte compiler is admitted, and the owner's direction is Vue Vapor.
2. Its toolchain is made of native binaries and Node APIs (esbuild, Rollup, the Tailwind scanner, a Node plugin, a spawning wrapper). None exists in an isolate or in a browser worker, and WebAssembly substitutes are excluded by the toolchain rule `[FAIT: PLAN:1030]`.
3. Its styles need a file-system scan at build time. The as-is Vue port planned by the DS keeps Tailwind and its class lists `[FAIT: PLAN:273]`, so the port alone does not remove this blocker (decision D3).
4. Its dependencies are not a browser-ESM closure: `@sentropic/geo-ui-svelte` ships raw `.svelte` and `.ts` sources `[FAIT: geo:packages/geo-ui-svelte/package.json:19-26]`, and `svelte-streamdown` is a Svelte package `[FAIT: immo:ui/package.json:31]`.
5. Its build is one indivisible job whose measured peak is about six times the hard cap of an isolate (1,516 MiB against 256 MiB).

### 2.11 Browser mode: origin and transport of the preview

`[FAIT]` What the code and the libraries impose:

- **Existing responses deny framing and stay as they are.** Release and live responses carry `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY` and `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin` `[sen:packages/artifacts/src/content/headers.ts:49,102,104; sen:apps/artifact-content/src/live.ts:39-44]`. The h2a spec limits untrusted-source output to standalone artifact content on its separate origin and forbids relaxing the standalone headers `[H2A-SPEC:232-234,482]`.
- **A live session is served on the public origin of the artifact. Routing belongs to the consumer's host; the downstream server only serves live routes.** The public host of an immo artifact is `<artifactId>.<pages domain>` (preprod: `pages.preprod.immo.sent-tech.ca`), and immo's content host answers on it `[immo:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/live-edge.ts:124-131; sen:deploy/k8s/overlays/preprod/artifact-content-live-edge-networkpolicy.yaml:1-6]`. On that one stable host, for each request:
  - a path whose first segment starts with `__`, or `/api`, is served by immo itself — reader, application relay, tile relay — and is never forwarded `[immo:api/src/artifacts/live-edge-forward.ts:57-58,167]`;
  - any other `GET` or `HEAD` is served live only after immo's admission of the principal, who is verified by the same code path as a release reader; otherwise the release is served, on the same host `[immo:api/src/artifacts/live-edge-forward.ts:15-21,164-181]`;
  - an admitted live request is forwarded to `artifact-content` with the browser's Host, an allow-list of headers and a fresh signed assertion; cookies and `Authorization` never leave immo `[immo:api/src/artifacts/live-edge-forward.ts:21-26,183-189]`.

  Downstream, `artifact-content` treats that pages domain as live-only: it re-verifies the assertion against the key identifiers registered for the domain, and it has no release, reader or activation path there `[sen:apps/artifact-content/src/host.ts:95-98; sen:apps/artifact-content/src/live-assertion.ts:99,130-134; sen:apps/artifact-content/README.md:38-39]`. "Live-only" is therefore a property of the downstream server, not of the public origin: for an immo artifact, the live session and the release share one origin, and the admission of each request selects which one answers. Revision 2 described the public origin itself as live-only; that was wrong.
- **A sentropic-hosted artifact has no live path today.** Its releases are served by `artifact-content` itself, on `<id>.<content base domain>` `[sen:apps/artifact-content/README.md:11; sen:deploy/k8s/overlays/preprod/patch-artifact-content-config.yaml:6]`. A pages domain may neither lie under the content base domain nor overlap another pages domain `[sen:apps/artifact-content/src/live-config.ts:95-105]`, and preprod registers one pages domain, immo's `[sen:deploy/k8s/overlays/preprod/patch-artifact-content-config.yaml:15]`. A live session of a sentropic-hosted artifact — geo's — therefore needs a pages domain of its own, a host key ring, and an edge that plays immo's part: lots VD-6.3 and K-6 (section 5.7).
- **Both libraries start a real worker only from the same origin.** MapLibre 6.4.1 calls `new Worker(url, {type: "module"})` when the worker URL has the origin of `location`; otherwise it wraps the URL in a `blob:` module, which the application CSP refuses (`ml:dist/maplibre-gl.mjs`: the test `new URL(e,t.href).origin!==t.origin`, then the `Blob` wrapper). pdf.js 4.10.38 does the same and, on any failure, falls back without an error to parsing on the main thread, logging "Setting up fake worker" `[pdf:build/pdf.mjs:12238-12256,12310-12314,12369-12371,12430]`.
- **The artifact's data path needs a real origin and a top-level context.** The reader transport relays application calls to `/__reader/app/api/*` on the same host, renews its grant with same-origin credentials, lets pdf.js read documents on a sealed cookie and uses one top-level navigation for consent `[immo:ui/src/lib/net/artifact-transport.ts:5-19,112-114]`.

`[JUGEMENT]` Consequence for revision 1. In a frame without `allow-same-origin`, every HTTP URL is on another origin than the document. MapLibre would take its `blob:` branch or fail; pdf.js would parse on the main thread without saying so; module scripts would need cross-origin headers that the responses refuse; the reader transport would have neither cookie nor top-level navigation. The opaque frame is withdrawn. The isolation it was meant to give — the preview cannot act with the authority of the product UI — comes from a separate tuple origin instead.

`[JUGEMENT]` Target:

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| Where the build runs | In the browser: the Web Worker of `ArtifactBuilder`, on the origin of the product UI. No server compile |
| Origin of the preview | The public origin of the artifact, where the preview is served as a live session: a tuple origin, distinct from the origin of the product UI. For an immo artifact it is also the origin of the artifact's release — the admission of each request selects which one answers. For a sentropic-hosted artifact it is a host of the consumer's pages domain, distinct from the release origin (VD-6.3). Never an opaque origin, never the product origin |
| Browsing context | Top-level: a tab or window opened by the component with `noopener`, so that neither window holds a reference to the other. No frame: the framing denial stays as it is |
| Transport ("draft push") | The component sends to the API of its own origin, authenticated as the session: (1) the output files that the draft store of the live session does not hold yet, each under its digest; (2) the draft manifest — paths, digests, MIME types, import map, shell. The server recomputes every SHA-256 and applies the release ceilings and the admission of the session. Closure files are not uploaded: the host materialises them from the mirror and the manifest references them by digest |
| Serving | The consumer's edge admits the request and forwards it; `artifact-content` answers `GET` from the current draft manifest, with the application policy unchanged and `worker-src 'self'`. Reader and application paths stay with the consumer's host. The inline import map is admitted by its hash, as in a release `[FAIT: sen:packages/artifacts/src/content/headers.ts:57-64]`. A host offers this mode only where a pages domain is registered for it: preprod has immo's `[FAIT: sen:deploy/k8s/overlays/preprod/patch-artifact-content-config.yaml:15]`; the one of the sentropic host, which geo uses, is delivered by VD-6.3 and K-6 |
| Refresh | The revision channel of the live session (section 2.9), on the same origin. The product page sends nothing to the preview |
| Server work | Hashing, storage and serving, by the content host that already serves every artifact. It compiles nothing in this mode |
| Authority | Preview only (decision D4). A pushed draft is never promoted: publication is re-derived from sources by the server. Only the session principal with write authority may push; an invitee may not `[FAIT: sen:apps/artifact-content/src/live.ts:12-13]` |
| Exposure | Unchanged from today's live sessions. A pushed draft is code chosen by the author, as a draft source compiled by the server is. Both run on the public origin of the artifact, under the same policy, with the reader authority of the viewer. For an immo artifact that origin is also the release's: a draft can read what the release stored there in the same browser profile (origin storage, caches), exactly as the draft served by Vite in a live session can today |

Why the two workers start in this preview:

| Evidence | Status |
|---|---|
| Platform rule. The top-level script of a module worker is fetched in `same-origin` mode: "If destination is "worker", "sharedworker", or "serviceworker", and isTopLevel is true, then set request's mode to "same-origin"". A classic worker script is fetched with "mode is "same-origin"" too. It follows that a document with a tuple origin passes for a worker file of its own origin, and that a document with an opaque origin passes for no HTTP URL — the failure of revision 1 | `[FAIT: HTML Standard, html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html, "fetch a single module script" and "fetch a classic worker script", read on 13/11]` for the two quotations; the last sentence is their consequence |
| The preview is served with the headers of application mode, the regime measured on the real immo UI build with same-origin module workers; a worker enforces the policy of its own script response, which carries `worker-src 'self'` `[sen:packages/artifacts/src/content/headers.ts:32-36,46,94-96]` | `[FAIT]` for a Vite-built release; the preview adds no header and removes none |
| MapLibre 6.4.1. With no setting, the library takes its worker URL from its own module URL: `new URL("./maplibre-gl-worker.mjs", import.meta.url)`. It calls `new Worker(url, {type: "module"})` on that URL as it is when the URL has the origin of `location`, and builds a `blob:` wrapper only otherwise (`ml:dist/maplibre-gl.mjs`, one line; functions `Ci`, `wi`, `Ti` and `Oi`, byte offsets 26949 to 28025). Runtime, worker and shared module are three files of one closure directory in the draft manifest, hence same-origin by construction; the worker's single import is `./maplibre-gl-shared.mjs` | `[FAIT]` on the package files (section 2.6). Re-run: `grep -b -o -e 'function Ci(' -e 'function wi(' -e 'function Ti(' dist/maplibre-gl.mjs` |
| pdf.js 4.10.38. `new Worker(workerSrc, {type: "module"})` receives the URL as given when `_isSameOrigin(window.location.href, workerSrc)` holds, and a `blob:` wrapper otherwise; any failure ends in `_setupFakeWorker`, which logs "Setting up fake worker." and parses on the main thread `[pdf:build/pdf.mjs:12238-12256,12310-12314,12369-12371]`. The worker is one file of the draft manifest with no import statement (`pdf:build/pdf.worker.mjs`, `pdf:build/pdf.worker.min.mjs`) | `[FAIT]` on the package files |
| The MIME type served is the one of the manifest, with `nosniff` `[sen:packages/artifacts/src/content/headers.ts:6-7,101]`; the MIME map of the package gives `text/javascript` to `.js` and `.mjs` `[sen:packages/artifacts/src/server/mime.ts:44-45]` | `[FAIT]` for the map; the mirror manifest reuses it (gate G-Q4) |
| The pair "V8 linker output + live edge" in real engines: a dedicated worker whose URL is the manifest file and not a `blob:` URL; the request of the shared module by that worker; a rendered map and a rendered PDF page; no "fake worker" warning; no CSP violation; one relayed reader call | `unverified`. Not measured in this round either (section 10, last table). First task of VD-2.0 (question 6), then gate G-V8-PREVIEW in the real component, in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit |

Read together: the standard requires a same-origin worker script; both libraries start a real module worker exactly when the worker file has the origin of the page; the preview is a top-level document on a tuple origin whose manifest holds those files; the policy of that origin allows same-origin workers and has been measured with them on a Vite build. Nothing in the chain depends on the bundler. What remains to measure is the chain as a whole, on the output of the new linker, in three engines.

Not in version 1: a preview embedded in the product page. It would need a framing permission on live responses, qualified apart as the h2a spec requires for any embedding `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:233-234]`. The registration already carries a list of embedding origins, used only by the panel host today `[FAIT: sen:api/src/services/artifacts/app-registration.ts:27; sen:api/src/services/artifacts/artifact-panel.ts:20-35]`.
