The live examples of the DS documentation (lot V13c) are islands with no worker and no data path: their frame is the DS's choice and is outside this section.

### 2.12 Committed code: the build input, static and live

`[FAIT: owner answer of 13/11, header]` The V8 builds are "basé sur le code commité". This section and section 2.13 are added by revision 5.

`[FAIT]` What the git mode of `@sentropic/artifacts` does today:

- **A publication builds one exact commit, never a draft.** `artifacts_git_publish` requests "a build job for one commit of the bound branch" `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/git-tool-definitions.ts:9]`. `publishGit` requires a commit SHA, checks that it is the head of the bound branch or one of its ancestors, and persists it in the job, where "the release CAS and the SHA are never caller-editable later" `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/git-tools-publish.ts:18-21,41-48; sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/git-tools-contracts.ts:9-14]`. The build snapshot is made of "files from one exact SHA, rooted at the repository, never the live draft", and the receipt names the commit, its tree, the lockfile digest and the descriptor digest `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/build-contract.ts:31-35,42-51]`.
- **A live session is a draft on top of a commit.** The draft record holds the head it is based on, the tree of that head and an overlay of changed files — path, text or deletion, mode `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-store.ts:17,33-37]`. `artifacts_live_write` applies "text edits, full text files and deletes to the bound owner's live source tree", must "persist the draft before acknowledging", and does "not commit or publish" `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/git-tool-definitions.ts:15]`. A draft is kept 7 days after its last renewal `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-drafts.ts:7]`.
- **A draft has a git identity before it is committed.** `liveTreeSha` is the git tree identifier of the base tree with the overlay applied, computed "without creating forge objects" `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-tree.ts:43-73]`.
- **Saving is one explicit commit, equal to the draft.** `artifacts_git_commit` creates "exactly one commit on the bound branch … under a compare-and-swap on expectedHeadSha", and "the built output is never committed" `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/git-tool-definitions.ts:8]`. The service refuses the commit when the submitted changes differ from the draft overlay (`git_live_snapshot_mismatch`) and when the tree of the created commit is not `liveTreeSha`; the draft then restarts empty on the new commit `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-commit.ts:43-49,70-74,96-100]`.
- **An edit cannot be a commit each time.** A commit is an operation on the forge — immo's registry binds its repository through the forge application `[immo5:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/registry.ts:56]` — and the store admits 30 commits per principal and 60 per artifact per hour `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/adapters/postgres-live.ts:106-120]`.
- **Live writes are admitted today for a personal binding whose executor is `sandbox`, and for no other** `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-service.ts:61]`.

`[JUGEMENT]` Rules of the target. Rules 1 to 3 keep the existing model; rules 4 and 7 add to it.

1. **Static.** The input of a publication is one commit of the artifact branch: the blobs of its tree inside the compile-read scope (section 2.8), its descriptor, and the pins that its descriptor names (rule 4). Nothing else enters a build: no draft, no file submitted by a caller, no state of the host that the commit does not name by a digest. In browser mode the component reads the same commit through the product API, and the server derives the release from the commit itself (decision D4-A).
2. **Live.** The input of a live revision is a commit plus the draft of the session: the base commit `baseHeadSha` and the overlay, identified together by `liveTreeSha`. A live edit made by an AI is a draft write, not a commit, and "saving" is `artifacts_git_commit`. A commit per edit is not retained: it would put a round trip to the forge and a compare-and-swap on the path of every edit, where section 2.13 has no room for them, and the existing quota would refuse the 31st edit of an hour.
3. **What "based on committed code" guarantees.**
   - Nothing is published that is not a commit: a draft is saved before `artifacts_git_publish` can name it.
   - Every live revision can be rebuilt from a commit and a stored overlay, and its sources are exactly those of the commit that a save creates: the same `liveTreeSha`, hence the same source digests in the recipes.
   - A save changes no served byte: the base moves to the new commit, the overlay is empty, the module digests are those of the last revision, and the page receives no revision.
4. **Same commit, same digest.** The version-2 descriptor carries a **build lock** of three pins: the digest of the mirror manifest that the artifact is built against, the SHA-256 of the compiler bootstrap bundle, and the revision of the compile policy — the three values of a recipe that the host derives today `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:186,189,192]`. Revision 5 pinned the first two and left the policy revision to the host. A new revision then changed every recipe key of an unchanged commit and, with the keys, the root manifest digest: naming the event in the receipt did not keep the invariant (round-5 review, finding 1).
   - *Every field of a recipe is fixed by the commit or by the binding* `[FAIT, for the fields: H2A-SPEC:183-192]`:

     | Field of the recipe | Fixed by |
     |---|---|
     | `sources`, `entry`, `kind` | the tree of the commit and its descriptor |
     | `profile` | the descriptor: the Vapor or the classic browser output `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:153]` |
     | `options` | the descriptor (alias table, workspace map, link mode, sourcemaps) and the operation — a publication or a live revision, never a state of the host; every default comes from the option table of the pinned policy revision |
     | `compilers` | the lock: the SHA-256 of the bootstrap bundle |
     | `importMapManifestDigest`; `dsTuple`, `dependencyLockDigest` and `catalogDigest` | the lock: the digest of the mirror manifest. The three other values are computed from the versions that the commit locks and from that pinned manifest, which lists every resource with its digest; none is read from the current state of the mirror |
     | `policyVersion`, `trustLane` | the lock: the compile policy revision, and the lane that this revision enables |
     | `accessPartition` | the binding: tenant, workspace and the digest of its scope. It says who builds; it is not a parameter of the build |

   - *Nothing outside the pins shapes a byte.* The code that shapes one is in the compiler bundle: parse, transform, link, the manifests. The rules that shape one outside the bundle — the default of every option, the limits and the rule that partition a build plan into units, the format of the shell — are named by the policy revision, and the host applies those of the pinned revision. Host code outside the bundle admits, stores and transports; an upgrade of the host that moves no pin moves no digest. A host that no longer carries the rules of a revision, or can no longer drive a bundle, withdraws it from its admitted set.
   - *The lock selects; the host still authorises.* Every check of today stays and is made at every build, against the current state of the host: the principal, the scope and its projection, custody and revocation, the destination of a publication `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:137,208]`; and, for each pin, that the host still admits it. A pin that the host does not or no longer admit — a manifest that is not retained, a revoked dependency, a compiler or a policy revision withdrawn from the admitted set `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:139,200,202]` — is a typed refusal that names the pin, before anything is compiled. It is never a silent move to the current pin, and never a build under a pin that is no longer admitted. A source cannot widen what is admitted `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:139]`: the lock chooses inside the admitted set.
   - *Result.* For one binding and one scope, a commit gives the same recipe keys and the same root manifest digest — in the isolate and in the browser, today, after a renewal of the mirror, after a new policy revision and after an upgrade of the host — or it gives a refusal. Across bindings the keys differ, because the access partition is part of a recipe `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:192]`, and the bytes do not: every output file has the same SHA-256, and the receipt lists them.
   - *Open.* The scope digest of a build must be the registered scope of the binding — repository, source subtree, refused paths — and carry neither a session nor the commit of a graph build; the app-context spec gives a session scope a provenance with a commit `[FAIT: CTX-SPEC:340]`. `unverified`: VD-2.8 fixes it with h-cond (section 8.5), and item 11 of G-V8-APP would show a difference.
   - Moving a pin is a commit of the repository's conductor: immo's descriptor is at the root of the repository, outside the writable paths of a session `[FAIT: immo5:sentropic.artifact.json:1-7; immo5:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/registry.ts:15-20]`.
5. **Live and publication are two profiles of the same sources.** A live revision is built in the live profile (development build of Vue, hot-replacement registration, section 2.9), the publication of the commit in the production profile. Their output digests differ by design; their source digests are equal (rule 3).
6. **Dossiers and visual artifacts of h2a.** Their code is the renderer of a tagged h2a release — a commit of h2a — with its own pins; the dossier document is validated data and never becomes a template `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:228]`. The compile service keeps its virtual-source grant for a submitted blob `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:210]`; that path is not one of a git-mode artifact, static or live.
7. **Browser mode.** The component reads the blobs of the base commit at `baseHeadSha`, applies the overlay of the session, and names `baseHeadSha` and `liveTreeSha` in the draft manifest it pushes; the server refuses a push whose base is not the base of the session. The source edit itself goes to the draft through `artifacts_live_write`, so that it can be saved; a pushed output is never the source of a publication (section 2.11).

Carried by: VD-2.8 (the three pins of the build lock in the descriptor, the refusal that names a pin, the unchanged authorisation checks), VD-2.9 and VD-2.16 (live writes of an `isolate` binding), VD-2.11 (base and tree identity in the pushed manifest), item 11 of G-V8-APP and gate G5a-PUB (the equalities above — after a renewal of the mirror, after a new policy revision, after an upgrade of the host — on a fixture and on immo's Focus entry).

### 2.13 Live latency: one AI edit of one file, stage by stage

`[FAIT: owner answer of 13/11, header]` "il faut que le v8 fasse des updates live au sens update par une ia en qq millisecondes". Revision 4 bounded the visible DOM at p95 ≤ 500 ms: that does not meet the requirement.

`[JUGEMENT]` **Reading, in numbers.** The requirement bears on what V8 does and on what this plan builds around it, from the moment the host receives the edit to the moment the patched module has replaced the old one in the page. "A few milliseconds" has one meaning in this plan: **less than 10 ms**. Three rules apply it, and every gate follows them.

1. **What is claimed.** For one AI edit of one file, in a warm live session, on a component inside the live size profile: the **V8 update** — the server path, from the edit received by the host to the patched module ready — takes less than 10 ms, at p50 and at p95; so does **each processing step after it** — the send by the host, the content host, the edge, and the replacement in the page, whole: from the patch received to the DOM mutated, with the work of Vue's hot-replacement runtime and the render of the component inside it; and their sum, the compute time of the whole update, is under 10 ms at p50. At p95 the sum is not claimed: the p95 criteria of the worst admitted case add up to 15.5 ms, 18.5 ms through a real edge. The p95 criteria of the parts do not bound the p95 of their sum — rare slow edits spread over several intervals can raise it — so the measured p95 of the sum is held to a ceiling: a figure above the range, which is the owner's to set. This plan proposes that sum, 15.5 ms, and puts it before the owner with its number (decision D13). No gate applies it on this plan's authority: the ceiling of a gate is the one that the owner's answer states — the proposed figure, or 9 ms if the owner requires the sum inside the range — and a gate that has no answer is not pronounced (section 5.10).
2. **What cannot be in that range is named, with a number of its own.** The network transit; the refresh of the display; the durable write behind the acknowledgement; the creation of a compile context, which makes an edit cold; a component above the live size profile. None of them is inside a claimed figure and none is subtracted from one: a claimed figure is always an interval between two marks of one process, or a sum of such intervals, taken in the edit itself — never a difference with another run. And nothing is outside a claimed figure unless two marks delimit it and it alone. Since revision 9 the render of the component is not in this list: no pair of marks isolates it from the work of the hot replacement, so it is inside the claimed replacement, and nothing that the page does between `p0` and `p2` is outside the claim. The render is still measured apart, as information: it tells where the time of a replacement goes, and it never excuses an overrun.
3. **No pass above the range.** No criterion of a claimed quantity is 10 ms or more — in any size class, any edit kind, any engine, any gate. What this plan cannot put under 10 ms is never counted as inside the range: it goes to the owner as a numbered decision with its numbers — decision D13 for what is known today, a component above the profile and the sum at p95; decision D12 for what a measurement shows, an overrun of the replacement `p0 → p2` among it. No lot moves a quantity from rule 1 to rule 2, and no gate applies a figure that such a decision puts before the owner until the owner has answered.

Revision 5 did not follow rule 3. It gave class L and the script edits of class M criteria above 10 ms, let two engines pass at twice the figure, and subtracted from its paths a "wire time" that could hold a wait of the server (round-5 review, findings 3 and 4). Revision 6 still broke rule 2 in the page and rule 3 in the schedule: it took the swap as `p0 → p2` less the time of a control run, a difference from which a cost common to both runs disappears, and it let the gates that apply the ceiling of decision D13 pass before the owner's answer (round-6 review, findings 1 and 2). Revision 7 still broke rule 2 in the page: its two marks of the render enclosed the whole hot-replacement call of Vue and the flush behind it, so that the runtime's own work — definitions, instances, ancestors, caches, scheduling — was counted as the render of the component and left the claimed swap (round-7 review). Revision 8 broke it once more, one level down: it kept outside the claim the intervals between the entry and the return of the outermost `setup` and `render` calls, and a render function of the Vapor output creates the children of its template between those two marks — each creation runs work of the hot replacement before the `setup` of the child (round-8 review). Three rounds tried to hold the work of the hot replacement on one side of a mark and the render on the other. Revision 9 stops, on the conductor's decision: the fallback of the review is taken, and the criterion of the replacement bears on the whole of `p0 → p2`.

`[FAIT]` What the path costs today:

- **Write.** Before it stores the draft, `artifacts_live_write` resolves the head of the branch on the forge, reads the base tree outside its lock — and the ancestry when the head moved — and may read the base text of an edited file `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-service.ts:102,116,183-196; sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-tree.ts:79-86]`. It then encrypts and stores the whole overlay in a store transaction `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-drafts.ts:39-59]`, and asks the worker to project the draft, which the worker reads back from the store `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-service.ts:164-167; sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-contracts.ts:16-19]`. The duration of these round trips was not measured by this lane: `unknown`.
- **Request.** The MCP endpoint builds one server per request and registers the tools on it `[sen4:packages/cluster-mesh/src/compose/mcp.ts:208-236]`; the registry itself is compiled once at start `[sen4:api/src/services/artifacts/transport.ts:13-16]`. Cost per request: `unknown`.
- **Isolate.** The runtime creates a new isolate and a new context for every call and compiles its bootstrap each time `[sen4:packages/skills/src/sandbox/runtime.ts:372-374,392-398]`. The confirmed limits of the compile service keep at most 2 warm isolates per host, each disposed after 60 s without a job, after 64 jobs or after 10 minutes, with a fresh context per job `[H2A-SPEC:212,319-322]`. A live session stays alive up to 900 s between two accesses `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-store.ts:5-6]`.
- **Pod.** The API container requests 100m of CPU and 256Mi, and is limited to 500m of CPU and 512Mi `[sen4:deploy/k8s/base/30-api.yaml:148-154]`. The resources of the immo MCP host and the flavor of the nodes that carry both hosts were not read: `unknown`. The reference of the lane brief is a d2-4 node — 2 vCPU and about 3.8 GiB usable `[DOSSIER:30]` — and the API pod budget.
- **Page.** Live responses admit scripts of the artifact's own origin and hashed inline scripts only — "never enable unsafe-inline/eval" — and admit inline styles `[sen4:apps/artifact-content/src/live.ts:46,104; sen4:packages/artifacts/src/content/headers.ts:38-41]`. A socket path per session exists `[sen4:apps/artifact-content/src/live.ts:36]`. immo's edge signs a fresh assertion for every forwarded request, caches the grant for 30 s and abandons an upstream request after 10 s `[immo5:api/src/artifacts/live-edge-forward.ts:25-26,44,110]`.
- **Sizes.** immo's UI has 69 `.svelte` files and 28,628 lines, 415 on average (section 2.9); its three largest files have 3,039, 2,974 and 2,830 lines, and the port decomposes the first `[PLAN:652-654]`. By size, at `immo5:`: 26 files of at most 150 lines, 35 of 151 to 600 lines, and 8 of more than 600 lines, which hold 15,182 of the 28,627 lines (`git ls-tree` and `wc -l` on `immo5:ui/src`). Five of those eight are among the 17 files of the Focus-signal entry, with 12,284 of its 14,364 lines; its twelve other files have 363 lines at most (read on `fix:`).

`[JUGEMENT]` **Marks.** Each process on the path stamps the revision on its own monotonic clock, and the marks travel with the revision.

| Process | Marks |
|---|---|
| MCP host | `h0` first byte of the tool call · `h1` invocation verified, session authority checked, edit validated · `h2` edit applied to the draft held in memory, on its pinned base commit, and identifier of the new blob computed · `h3` compiled module and CSS returned by the isolate of the session · `h4` final bytes, digests, draft manifest switched · `h5` revision written to the connection of the content host |
| Content host | `c0` revision received · `c1` patch written to the pending request of the page, or style frame written to its socket |
| Edge of the consumer's host | `e0` patch received from the content host · `e1` patch forwarded to the page |
| Page | `p0` patch received · `p1` module evaluated · `r0` new definition handed to the hot-replacement runtime of Vue · `f0` the runtime enters a `setup` or a `render` function of a component, `f1` that function returns — one pair for each outermost call, the *render intervals* of the edit · `r1` end of the flush that follows the hand-over: the runtime has re-rendered or re-created the affected instances and run what the replacement queued · `p2` component replaced, DOM mutated · `p3` start of the next frame of the display. Between `p0` and `p2` the claimed interval is one, the whole; `p1`, `r0`, `r1` and the pairs `f0`, `f1` lie inside it and split it for the report only |

`[JUGEMENT]` **Budget.** One edit of one file, made by an AI through `artifacts_live_write`, in a warm session, server mode. Every figure is a criterion, `unverified` until VD-2.0 measures it on 14/11 at 08:00. Size classes of the edited SFC: S, up to 150 lines; M, up to 600 lines — the reference, above the average immo file. Together they are the **live size profile**. Edit kinds, defined by what is recompiled (section 2.7): *style* — a plain style edit, one style block; *template* — a plain template edit, the template; *whole* — a script edit or a crossing edit, up to the whole SFC.

| # | Stage | Interval | p50 | p95 | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Edit received | `h0 → h1` | ≤ 0.5 ms | ≤ 1 ms | claimed. The transit from the AI to the host is the AI's own link: not a stage |
| 2 | Write | `h1 → h2` | ≤ 0.2 ms | ≤ 0.5 ms | claimed |
| 3 | Compile of the changed SFC, in the isolate of the session; a wait behind the previous edit is inside it | `h2 → h3` | class M — style ≤ 1 ms; template ≤ 2.5 ms; whole ≤ 4 ms | class M — style ≤ 3 ms; template ≤ 5 ms; whole ≤ 6.5 ms | claimed, for classes S and M |
| 4 | Link and invalidate | `h3 → h4` | ≤ 0.3 ms | ≤ 1 ms | claimed |
| 5 | Delivery — processing | `h4 → h5`; `c0 → c1`; `e0 → e1` | host ≤ 0.2 ms; content host ≤ 0.3 ms; edge ≤ 1 ms | host ≤ 0.5 ms; content host ≤ 1 ms; edge ≤ 3 ms | claimed, each |
|   | Delivery — transit | `h5 → c0`, `c1 → e0` and `e1 → p0`, added | ≤ 2 ms in the gates | ≤ 5 ms in the gates | **outside the claim: network.** Exactly one one-way transit from the host to the page; 0 fallback |
| 6 | Hot replacement in the page | `p0 → p2`, whole: the evaluation of the patch module and the hand-over; the work of Vue's hot-replacement runtime, the registration of the children that a render creates included; the `setup` and `render` functions of the components; the hooks that the replacement runs; every wait | ≤ 2 ms | ≤ 5 ms | claimed, whole. The figures are those that revisions 5 to 8 gave to the swap; since revision 9 they also hold the render of the component, and they are not widened for it |
|   | of which the component's own render | The render intervals `f0 → f1` of the edit, added | reported | reported | **information only**: inside stage 6, with no criterion of its own; subtracted from nothing; it excuses no overrun of stage 6 |
| 7 | Visible DOM | `p2 → p3` | ≤ 16.7 ms | ≤ 33.4 ms | **outside the claim: the display**, one frame at 60 Hz, two at p95 |

Stage 3 and the server path, by class and edit kind (p50 / p95):

| Class | Stage 3 — style | Stage 3 — template | Stage 3 — whole | Server path `h0 → h4` — style | Server path — template | Server path — whole |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S — up to 150 lines | ≤ 0.5 / ≤ 1.5 ms | ≤ 1 / ≤ 2.5 ms | ≤ 2 / ≤ 4 ms | ≤ 1.5 / ≤ 4 ms | ≤ 2 / ≤ 5 ms | ≤ 3 / ≤ 6.5 ms |
| M — up to 600 lines | ≤ 1 / ≤ 3 ms | ≤ 2.5 / ≤ 5 ms | ≤ 4 / ≤ 6.5 ms | ≤ 2 / ≤ 5.5 ms | ≤ 3.5 / ≤ 7.5 ms | ≤ 5 / ≤ 9 ms |

- **What the claim gives, end to end.** Worst admitted case, a whole edit of class M: the server path takes ≤ 5 ms at p50 and ≤ 9 ms at p95; the processing after it — the whole replacement in the page among it — 2.5 ms at p50 and 6.5 ms at p95 by sum of its criteria, 3.5 ms and 9.5 ms through a real edge. The compute time of the whole update — every claimed interval between `h0` and `p2`; only the transits are not in it, and since revision 9 the render of the component is — has a criterion at p50: ≤ 9 ms. At p95 this plan proposes to hold it to the sum of the p95 criteria of its parts — 15.5 ms for class M and 13 ms for class S, 3 ms more through a real edge: a ceiling, not a claim, and the one compute figure of a qualified component that is above 10 ms. That figure is the owner's: a gate applies the ceiling of the answer to decision D13, and none applies one before it. The figures of this paragraph are those of revision 8: none is widened for the render that they now hold. Around it, each against its own number: one transit and one frame.
- **Above the live size profile.** A component of more than 600 lines has no criterion and passes nothing. This plan expects it outside the range — for a file of 3,000 lines, about 12 ms at p50 and 30 ms at p95 for a template edit and 25 ms and 60 ms for a whole edit, in stage 3 alone: the figures of revision 5, `unverified` — and gives it no wider criterion. Three consequences follow. Such a file is never in the edit set of a live gate. It is decomposed before it is qualified live: VD-5.8 for the edit set of G5a, the worklist of VD-5.5 for the rest. And the owner decides what a live edit of such a file may be until then: decision D13. Of the two views that G5a edits, one is today the largest file of the UI, `SignauxMapView`.
- **Network.** The delivery crosses the network once, from the host to the page: two hops inside the cluster and one to the browser. In the gates the harness browsers run in the region of the host, and the added transit has a criterion of its own: ≤ 2 ms at p50 and ≤ 5 ms at p95. For a reader elsewhere the transit is their link: it is measured and reported with every series run from there, it has no criterion, and it is `unknown` for a reader in Montréal until question 16 of VD-2.0.
- **Edge.** On a real host the page talks to the edge of the consumer's host, which forwards to the content host: immo's edge for immo, the edge of VD-6.3 for an artifact that the sentropic host carries. What an edge adds is processing, between its own marks: ≤ 1 ms at p50 and ≤ 3 ms at p95. The fixture gates run with the stub edge of the harness; the two real edges are measured by G5-HOST and G6-HOST.
- **The replacement in the page** is one claimed interval, `p0 → p2`, whole — from the patch received to the DOM mutated — held to ≤ 2 ms at p50 and ≤ 5 ms at p95. The work of Vue's hot-replacement runtime, the registration of the children that a render creates and the render of the component itself are inside it. Nothing is separated from it, and nothing is subtracted from it.

  `[FAIT]` At the pin the hand-over is one call, `rerender` or `reload` of `__VUE_HMR_RUNTIME__` `[rt36:@vue/runtime-core/dist/runtime-core.esm-bundler.js:457-461]`, the call that the Vue plugin of a bundler's dev server emits `[rt36:@vitejs/plugin-vue/dist/index.mjs:1384-1388]`. That call is more than the render of the component:
  - `rerender` installs the new function on the record and on each instance, then enters each instance in turn `[rt36:@vue/runtime-core/dist/runtime-core.esm-bundler.js:507-528]`;
  - `reload` rewrites the registered definition property by property, copies the set of instances and walks the ancestors of each. When the component and the parents of its instances are Vapor, it re-renders those parents inside the call. Otherwise it marks the instances, deletes three caches of the application context and queues one job per parent, which is queued again when an instance waits for an asynchronous dependency `[rt36:@vue/runtime-core/dist/runtime-core.esm-bundler.js:529-623,624-627]`; `nextTick` returns the promise of the pending flush `[rt36:@vue/runtime-core/dist/runtime-core.esm-bundler.js:271-274]`;
  - the re-render of a Vapor instance looks its place up, stops the scope of the previous render, removes the old block, calls the render function, applies fallthrough attributes, scope identifiers and CSS variables, and inserts the new block `[rt36:@vue/runtime-vapor/dist/runtime-vapor.esm-bundler.js:3907-3947]`.

  Inside all of it the runtime enters the code of a component through two functions of its definition, `setup` and `render` `[rt36:@vue/runtime-vapor/dist/runtime-vapor.esm-bundler.js:4195-4200,4282-4294; rt36:@vue/runtime-core/dist/runtime-core.esm-bundler.js:3967,6546]`; what the component registered from them — hooks, watchers — is entered apart, by the mount or by the scheduler `[rt36:@vue/runtime-vapor/dist/runtime-vapor.esm-bundler.js:4563,4568]`. In the Vapor output a render function also creates the components that its template holds, each through its own `setup` and `render` `[rt36:@vue/runtime-vapor/dist/runtime-vapor.esm-bundler.js:4002,4126-4127]`. Each creation runs work of the hot replacement before the `setup` of the child, as read in round 9: in the development build `createComponent` calls `registerHMR` on the new instance, binds its two hot-replacement entries, opens a measure and prepares its props and emits options `[rt36:@vue/runtime-vapor/dist/runtime-vapor.esm-bundler.js:4100-4112]`; `registerHMR` reads the identifier of the definition, looks its record up, creates the record when it is absent and adds the instance `[rt36:@vue/runtime-core/dist/runtime-core.esm-bundler.js:476-484]`. The classic runtime calls it when it mounts a component whose definition carries an identifier `[rt36:@vue/runtime-core/dist/runtime-core.esm-bundler.js:4900]`. The components of the previous render are unregistered when their block is removed `[rt36:@vue/runtime-vapor/dist/runtime-vapor.esm-bundler.js:3697-3704,3924,4603; rt36:@vue/runtime-core/dist/runtime-core.esm-bundler.js:485-487]`.

  Three revisions tried to hold that work on one side of a mark and the render on the other. Revision 6 subtracted a control run. Revision 7 took the render as `r0 → r1`, the whole call and its flush: the runtime's own work listed above was counted as the component's, so that a slow hot replacement left the claimed swap and was judged against the wider figure of the render. The counter-example of the round-7 review: 12 ms of that work on one edit in ten leave the swap of revision 7 at 1.5 ms at p95 and its render at 14 ms, both inside their criteria, while the replacement takes 13.5 ms. Revision 8 took the render as the render intervals, between the entry and the return of the outermost `setup` and `render` calls: the registration of the children runs between those two marks of the parent and was still counted as render. The counter-example of the round-8 review: 1.5 ms outside the marks, 2 ms of render and 12 ms of nested registration on one edit in ten give a declared swap of 1.5 ms at p95 and a render of 14 ms; both pass the criteria of revision 8, while the replacement, the work of the hot replacement included, takes 13.5 ms.

  `[JUGEMENT]` *One interval, by decision of the conductor (round 9).* The separation is given up and the fallback of the review is taken. The criterion of the replacement — the figures that revisions 5 to 8 gave to the swap, ≤ 2 ms at p50 and ≤ 5 ms at p95 — bears on the whole of `p0 → p2`, an interval between two marks of the page in the edit itself. It holds everything that the page does for the edit: `p0 → r0`, the evaluation of the patch module, the registration and the hand-over; `r0 → r1`, the hot-replacement call and its flush — the runtime's own work listed above, the registration and the unregistration of the children, the `setup` and `render` functions of every component that the replacement enters, the lifecycle hooks, watchers and directive hooks that it runs, the patch of the virtual tree in the classic output, the scheduler and every wait, the one of an asynchronous dependency included, after which the re-creation is queued again `[FAIT: rt36:@vue/runtime-core/dist/runtime-core.esm-bundler.js:587-593]`; and `r1 → p2`, what follows the flush — `p2` is taken at the mutation of the DOM. A style-only revision hands nothing over: its replacement is `p0 → p2` as well. The figures are not widened to make room for the render: revision 8 allowed it ≤ 8 ms at p50 and ≤ 16.7 ms at p95 outside the claim, and those two figures are withdrawn — they are not a tolerance of the replacement, and no figure replaces them. On both counter-examples above the whole of `p0 → p2` is 15.5 ms at p95 — the 13.5 ms of the reviews plus the 2 ms of render — and 3.5 ms at p50: the criterion fails at both. The same criterion holds on a real view: one whose replacement runs a heavy hook — a map created when its component is mounted — or whose own render is long misses it, and is not excused as render.

  *The render, measured apart, for information.* The client runtime still wraps the `setup` and the `render` function of every definition that the live profile registers, at the registration and at each hand-over. The emitted module is not changed, and the function handed over is the one that the runtime then calls: `rerender` installs it, `reload` copies it onto the registered definition `[FAIT: rt36:@vue/runtime-core/dist/runtime-core.esm-bundler.js:510-514,534,624-627]`. The wrapper changes nothing that the runtime reads on the function: it keeps its arity — at the pin the classic runtime builds the setup context only when `setup` declares a second parameter, and tests the arity of a functional component `[FAIT: rt36:@vue/runtime-core/dist/runtime-core.esm-bundler.js:3972,6544]`. The wrapper takes `f0` when the runtime enters such a function and `f1` when it returns. Only an outermost call is marked: a call nested in another — a child that a render function creates — belongs to it. The render intervals of an edit are those whose marks fall between `p0` and `p2`: zero, one or several. Their sum is reported with every series as the component's own render, beside the three parts of the replacement. These figures have no criterion. They lie inside the replacement and are subtracted from nothing. They hold more than the code of the component — the registration of the children that it creates is among it, which is the finding of the round-8 review: they say where the time of a replacement goes, and they never excuse an overrun. Where the wrapper cannot be installed — question 10 of VD-2.0 lists the cases, per engine and per output — the render is not reported, and the criterion is unchanged: it never depended on the wrapper.

  *An overrun.* A replacement above its criterion — `p0 → p2` above 2 ms at p50 or above 5 ms at p95, the resolution of the page clock added — fails the gate that measures it and opens decision D12 with the numbers: p50, p95, p99 and maximum of `p0 → p2`, its three parts and the render measured apart, per edit kind, size class, engine and path of the pinned runtime. No lot answers an overrun by taking the render, or any other part, out of the interval. Whether another figure applies is option D of that decision, the owner's alone.

  The live profile still emits nothing inside a `render` or a `setup` function: its registration is made of statements at module level, which the gate of VD-2.13 asserts by a diff with the same compile without hot replacement. The wrapper is the only code of this chain on the path of a render, and its two marks are its only work; that work is inside the claimed interval. No control run is a measure, and nothing is subtracted.
- **Display.** Stage 7 is one frame. Nothing in the build can shorten it.
- **Acknowledgement.** The tool call returns to the AI after the draft is durable, as its contract requires. The durable write is a transaction on the store, bounded by its network and its disk: ≤ 5 ms at p50 and ≤ 20 ms at p95. It runs beside stages 3 to 6 and is not on the path of the display. The acknowledgement follows the later of the two: ≤ 8 ms at p50 and ≤ 25 ms at p95 from `h0`, measured at the host.
- **Browser mode.** A person, or an assistant inside the product UI, edits in `ArtifactBuilder`. The worker path — from the edit handed to the Web Worker to the final bytes and digests — has the criteria of the server path, the same in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit. The product API hashes and admits the push in ≤ 1 ms at p50 and ≤ 3 ms at p95; content host, edge and replacement in the page are those of the server mode. The delivery crosses the network twice — the push of the changed outputs, then the revision to the preview — and each transit is held to the network criterion. This budget is gated on the harness, in the three engines: item 3 of G-LIVE-MS. Through a real edge the browser mode is gated on what it does and not on its latency (item 4 of G5-HOST, item 7 of G6-HOST): `not covered` (section 8.6).
- **Cold edits.** An edit that finds no warm context — after an eviction, a restart of the host, a terminated worker — re-creates the context first: the engine must load and compile the compiler itself. It is not an update of a warm session and it is outside the claim, with a bound of its own: server path p95 ≤ 500 ms, the figure that revision 4 gave to every edit; VD-2.0 reports the measured value. A session that stays inside the idle rule has no cold edit after its start, and the gates count them: 0 in every series. A session beyond the number of warm sessions that its host holds has only cold edits: that number is a memory budget, in decision D5 with what each session costs.
- **Queues.** Edits of one session are compiled one at a time and applied in order; the page applies the latest revision it holds and never an older one after it. An edit waits for nothing else: the live profile counts its edits apart from the compile jobs of the tenant, whose limit of one active compile `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:315]` keeps governing dossier compiles and publications. Revision 5 queued the edits of two sessions of a tenant behind each other under that limit, and so an edit behind a build. Where an edit does wait, the wait lies between `h2` and `h3`: inside stage 3, never outside a mark. G-LIVE-MS runs its series again beside a second session and beside a publication (item 6).

`[JUGEMENT]` **What the path needs that revision 4 did not have.**

1. *An isolate that stays warm for the session* (VD-2.15). The compile time of stage 3 is the time of a compiler whose code the engine has already optimised. A fresh context per job, or a new isolate per call, starts from unoptimised code each time. The live profile therefore gives each live session one isolate of its own, created at the start of the session, used by every edit of that session and by nothing else, and disposed at its stop, at its idle expiry, on revocation, on a change of policy or compiler, and on a memory or deadline fault. What stays in it between two edits is the compiler and the sources of that one session. The compile profile of the h2a spec is unchanged: a dossier compile and a publication keep the fresh context per job. The live profile is a new entry of `AMD-ISO`, with its own limits: the idle rule of the session instead of 60 s, no job count, its own accounting — one edit at a time per session, counted apart from the compile jobs of the tenant — and a number of warm sessions per host that K-2 publishes — 2 at first, the confirmed number of warm isolates. A session beyond that number is admitted with every edit on the cold path, outside the range, and `artifacts_live_status` says so: the number is part of decision D5.
2. *A block memo* (VD-2.15, section 2.7): a plain template or style edit does not pay for the script; an edit that crosses blocks recompiles what depends on it, and the scope identifier does not move.
3. *A pinned base and a draft in memory* (VD-2.16). The session reads the head, the tree and the base blobs of its graph once, at its start. A write makes no call to the forge. A head moved by someone else is detected where it already is — at the commit, by `git_head_moved` — and, in addition, at the start of a session and by a periodic check that `artifacts_live_status` reports; immo consumes no forge webhook `[FAIT: immo5:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/registry.ts:59-60]`. The edit is applied to the overlay in memory; the durable draft is written behind it, one write in flight at a time, and the call is acknowledged after it. A durable write that fails returns the session and the page to the last durable revision and fails the call.
4. *A projection in process* (VD-2.16). The live port receives the changed files with the write. The read of the draft from the store, which VD-2.9 builds, stays the path of a restart.
5. *A push at every hop, with the bytes* (VD-2.16, VD-2.13). The host pushes each revision to the content host on a persistent connection inside the cluster, signed as its existing pushes are, with the changed outputs; the content host keeps them in memory and delivers the revision to the page — a module by the pending patch request of item 6, a style and the announcement on the socket of the session. Nothing on the path polls, and nothing reads a store. The push is attached to the switch of the draft manifest, whoever wrote the outputs — the isolate or a browser push — so that both modes share one delivery (VD-2.16).
6. *A patch applied without a second round trip, under the unchanged policy* (VD-2.13). A style-only revision is applied from the frame itself: live responses admit inline styles. A module must come from a same-origin URL: the policy admits nothing else and this plan does not change it. The page therefore keeps one request pending — the *pre-armed patch request*, an `import()` of the next revision of the session — which the content host answers with the patch module when the revision exists. The patch crosses the network once. The socket announcement stays, and the page falls back on it when no request is pending; the fallback costs one more round trip and is counted as a miss of the delivery. A pending request is re-armed before the 10 s upstream timeout of an edge. Whether the three engines evaluate a module whose response was held, and whether the edges forward it, is `unverified`: question 15 of VD-2.0. Excluded: a `blob:` or inline script source and any form of `eval` on live responses.
7. *A request path that fits stage 1* (VD-2.16): if the server built per request exceeds the target, the host keeps one prepared per registration.
8. *A CPU budget* (K-2, decision D5): the pod limit of 500m lets the kernel suspend the process once it has used 50 ms of CPU in a 100 ms period. A p95 of a few milliseconds needs a published CPU budget beside the memory one, sized so that one publication can build beside the edits of the warm sessions.

`[JUGEMENT]` **Measurement.**

- *Intervals.* The marks of the table above are recorded for every revision and travel with it: in the frame that the host pushes, in the response of the patch request, and back to the AI with the tool result when the session has its diagnostic flag; the isolate's own CPU time is added when the addon exposes it. A claimed figure is an interval between two marks of one process, or a sum of such intervals of the same edit: no clock offset enters it and nothing is subtracted from it. Revision 5 defined a "wire time" as `t5 − t4` less the clock offset and took it out of the paths. That interval also held the routing, the processing and the waits between the manifest ready and the patch received: the gate could subtract the very slowness it must detect (round-5 review, finding 3). Revision 6 kept one subtraction, in the page — the swap as `p0 → p2` less a control run (round-6 review, finding 1). Revision 7 replaced it by two marks, `r0` and `r1`, that enclosed the whole hot-replacement call and its flush: the runtime's own work was inside them (round-7 review). Revision 8 took the render as the render intervals `f0 → f1`, around the outermost `setup` and `render` calls: the registration of the children that a render creates was inside them (round-8 review). Since revision 9 no mark separates anything in the page: the claimed interval is `p0 → p2`, whole, and `p1`, `r0`, `r1`, `f0` and `f1` split it for the report only.
- *Transit and clocks.* A transit lies between the send mark of one process and the receive mark of the next, on two clocks. They are related by a ping on each connection of the path — the push connection between the host and the content host, the socket of the session between the content host and the page — sampled with every edit. The offset is taken from the sample with the smallest round trip, and its uncertainty is half that round trip. A transit is compared with its criterion with the uncertainty added. A series whose uncertainty exceeds 1 ms is void.
- *Clock of the page.* The resolution of the clock on which a page takes its marks is `unknown` in each engine of the harness: a browser may round it. Question 16 of VD-2.0 reads it. An interval of the page is compared with its criterion with that resolution added: a coarse clock can fail a criterion and can never pass one. The replacement is one uninterrupted span, `p0 → p2`: the resolution is added to it once. The render intervals, which are information, are reported with their number and with the resolution beside them. The resolution is part of the report.
- *A wait is processing.* The time a revision waits inside a process — in a queue, behind another compile, before a send — lies between two marks of that process: it is inside a claimed figure and cannot be counted as network.
- *Closure.* For every edit, the processing intervals and the transits — each an interval between two consecutive marks of that edit — add up to `p2 − h0` within the uncertainty. The replacement `p0 → p2` is one of those processing intervals, and the render intervals are inside it: they are not a term of the sum. A series that does not close is void: no time on the path is outside a mark.
- *Witness.* A delay of 20 ms is injected, under the diagnostic flag of the session, in the host between `h4` and `h5`, then in the content host between `c0` and `c1`, then in the edge, then at seven points of the page: (1) in the client runtime before `r0`; (2) inside the hot-replacement call before the runtime starts — the hand-over then goes through a proxy of `rerender` and `reload` that waits after `r0`; (3) at the entry of the wrapper, before `f0` — the runtime has then done its own work and the component has not started; (4) inside the `render` function of the fixture; (5) in `registerHMR` of a child that this render function creates, before the `setup` of that child; (6) at the exit of the wrapper, after `f1`; (7) after `r1`. Each run fails on the criterion of that hop — in the page, at each of the seven points, on the replacement — while the measured transit moves by less than the uncertainty: a slow server cannot pass as a slow network, and nothing that is slow in the page can pass as render. Points 2 and 3 are the case of the round-7 review: the handling of the hot replacement slowed before the component runs. Point 5 is the case of the round-8 review: work of the hot replacement nested in the render of a parent. `[FAIT]` `registerHMR` reads the identifier of the definition, `instance.type.__hmrId`, as its first statement, and the Vapor `createComponent` calls it before the `setup` of the child `[rt36:@vue/runtime-core/dist/runtime-core.esm-bundler.js:476-477; rt36:@vue/runtime-vapor/dist/runtime-vapor.esm-bundler.js:4102,4126-4127]`. `[JUGEMENT]` Under the diagnostic flag the client runtime gives the definition of the child of the fixture an accessor in place of that property; armed at `f0` of the parent, its next read waits 20 ms, once. The delay then runs inside `registerHMR`, between `f0` and `f1` of the parent, in the pinned runtime as published. Whether it lands there in the three engines is `unverified` (question 10 of VD-2.0); if it does not, the witness uses a copy of the runtime file with the delay in `registerHMR`, served to the fixture alone. In revision 8 that delay lay inside a render interval: it left the claimed swap and was judged as render. Here it fails the replacement. The reported render moves by the same 20 ms: it holds work of the hot replacement, which is why it is information and excuses nothing. Point 4 failed the render of revision 8 and left its swap inside its criterion; it now fails the replacement like the others.
- *Series.* N = 200 edits for each pair of edit kind and size class of the profile, after 20 warm-up edits that are discarded, 250 ms apart; one series of crossing edits, held to the criteria of the whole kind; one burst of 50 edits back to back; one forced eviction followed by an edit; the two concurrent series. Reported: p50, p95, p99 and maximum of every interval, the transits, the round trips and the uncertainty; for information, the three parts of the replacement — `p0 → r0`, `r0 → r1`, `r1 → p2` — and the component's own render with the number of its render intervals; and the number of full reloads, of cold edits and of fallbacks of the delivery — each of these three must be 0. A void series is run again and never counted. F-LIVE-L goes through the same series and is reported without a criterion.
- *Fixtures.* F-LIVE-S, F-LIVE-M and F-LIVE-L: three TypeScript SFCs on DS components — 150 lines (60 of script, 70 of template, 20 of style), 600 lines (220, 280, 100) and 3,000 lines (1,100, 1,400, 500) — generated and committed by VD-2.0 and reused by every gate. On immo, the ported Focus views that are inside the live size profile.
- *Environment.* The production image of the MCP host, in preprod, under the limits that K-2 publishes; the same series under the current limits is recorded beside it. The harness browsers — Chromium, Firefox and WebKit — run in the region of the host. Node flavor, pod limits and resident memory are part of the report. So is the flavor of the machine that runs the harness browsers: the replacement in the page runs on it, render included, and that flavor is `unknown` until the k8s session gives the place (section 8.5).
- *Gates.*

| Evidence | Carried by | When |
|---|---|---|
| Cold and warm compile time of F-LIVE-S, -M and -L in the isolate and in the worker of three browsers; fresh context against a kept context; the edit matrix of the block memo; resident memory of a warm session; the transit of each hop; the pre-armed request in three engines and through an edge; the replacement `p0 → p2` of the fixtures, whole, against its criterion, with the render measured apart for information; how the witness slows `registerHMR` of a child; and the state that each edit kind keeps | VD-2.0, questions 10 and 13 to 16 | T+22 · 14/11 08:00 |
| Stages 3 and 4, the counters and the byte equality of the block memo, lifecycle, accounting and isolation of the session isolate | VD-2.15 | T+154 |
| Stages 1, 2 and 4, the send of the host, the server path, the acknowledgement; marks `h0` to `h5` and `c0` | VD-2.16 | T+174 |
| The answer of the content host; the replacement `p0 → p2`, whole — the work of the hot-replacement runtime, the registration of the children and the render of the component inside it; the render intervals of the component, for information; the display; marks `c1`, `e0`, `e1`, `p0` to `p3`, `r0`, `r1`, `f0` and `f1`; clocks, closure and series; the witness of the page, `registerHMR` of a child slowed during the render of its parent among its points | VD-2.13 | T+174 |
| Every claimed interval, the server path and the sum at p50; the ceiling on the sum at p95, the one of the owner's answer to decision D13; the numbers outside the claim; both modes, three engines; the witness and the concurrent series; on F-LIVE-S, F-LIVE-M and the application fixture | **G-LIVE-MS**, carried by VD-2.10 | T+196 · 21/11 14:00 |
| The same criteria through immo's edge, on the real immo MCP | item 10 of G5-HOST | T+197 |
| The same criteria on the ported Focus views inside the profile, with the consumer protocol of G-WRITE | G5a | T+206 |
| The same criteria through the edge of the sentropic host | G6-HOST, G6 | T+202, T+234 |

The five gates of this table, from G-LIVE-MS down, apply the ceiling on the sum at p95. Each has decision D13 as an edge of the schedule (section 5.10) and is pronounced only after the owner's answer, on the ceiling that the answer states; the dates above are those of an answer given before them.

`[JUGEMENT]` **When a criterion is missed.** No lot widens a criterion, and none moves a quantity out of the claim. A measured miss — at VD-2.0, at a lot gate or at G-LIVE-MS — is written with its numbers and goes to the owner as decision D12 (section 7): more CPU or memory for the MCP hosts, a compile deployment of its own, a smaller live size profile with the decomposition it costs, a view restructured so that an edit no longer re-creates what is heavy in it, or renders less — when the miss is in the page: an overrun of the replacement `p0 → p2`, whatever part of it is slow, the runtime, the registration of the children or the render of the component — or a wider figure that the owner states. The render measured apart is among the numbers of the decision; it is not a ground to pass. The lot that measured it stops at that stage until the answer. The same holds for the fallback of the delivery: if the pre-armed request cannot be qualified, the measured cost of the announcement path is put to the owner; it is not adopted as the criterion.

---

## 4. Transition: the k8s builder pool until the target replaces it

### 4.1 What is decided

`[FAIT: owner directive of 13/11 ~17:55, verbatim in the header]` « stoppe les travaux de la branche gvisor et decom le serveur associé », with the scope « Étape 1 + VM ». Four consequences are decided and are not reopened anywhere in this plan:

1. Builder step 1 is stopped: the lanes S1-B1 and G-A stop, and PR #1024 stays unmerged.
2. Gate G-A is cancelled. It has no verdict: it is neither passed nor failed, and nothing in this plan reads the stop as a failure of the VM path.
3. The VM `artifact-builder-vm-1` is deleted. It is a k8s act, done only after the confirmation that the pool serves builds (lane brief of round 8, facts of the conductor).
4. There is no VM bridge. Builds stay on the k8s builder pool until the V8 target replaces them.

`[JUGEMENT]` The directive replaces the conditional transition of the owner precision of 08:55, which revisions 1 to 7 applied: step 1 continued as is to gate G-A, the VM became the bridge on a PASS, and the programme switched to the target on a FAIL. Two things stay of that precision. The target is the one of the first directive, unchanged. And nothing is optimised to make a Svelte build path pass or go faster: the owner did not ask for that optimisation.

The three postures a transition can take now stand as follows. The table is a reading aid, not a list of open options.

| Posture | Where it stands |
|---|---|
| Stop the VM now | **Decided** by the owner on 13/11 at about 17:55: sections 4.3 and 4.5 |
| Keep the VM as a bridge until the target is qualified | Withdrawn with gate G-A. What carries the builds until the target is the pod builder on the k8s pool, which preprod never left (section 4.2) |
| Keep the VM only as a fallback | Withdrawn. After the cutover the rollback target is the pod builder on the pool, during the 7 days of observation (sections 4.6 and 4.7) |

### 4.4 The interim path: the pod builder on the k8s pool

`[JUGEMENT]`, on the facts of section 4.2.

- **What immo gets until the cutover.** Publications and live sessions on the pod builder of `main`, the default backend, as before step 1: several minutes per publication — 6 min 51 s on 11/11 — and a Vite dev server in a pod for a live session. Not the warm sub-second path: that comes with V8 live, at G5a.
- **Nothing is tuned on it.** No agent-hour of this plan goes to the pod path (section 4.3, lot S1-0). The pod builder stays as it is on `main` until VD-7.2 deletes it.
- **Pool sizing is an owner decision (D14).** With the pool at min 0, the first build or live session after an idle period waits for a node: 5 to 8 minutes. With one node kept, it does not, for about 24 CAD per month. `[FAIT]` The owner decided minNodes 1 on 07/11, after that measurement `[TRACK:775, item 01M6V78THAPQNRNHWXVJ109B9D]`; the pool went back to 0 on 12/11, as the hot rollback of the VM `[NIGHT:172]`. `[JUGEMENT]` This plan does not put the decision of 07/11 back in force by itself: it recommends it for the transition, with its cost — about 8 CAD from now to the cutover, at the monthly figure of the VM that is deleted (decision D14, section 7).
- **Cost.** The pool: nodes billed while a builder pod runs, or about 0.80 CAD per day with one node kept. The VM: about 24 CAD per month until K-0 deletes it.
- **Rollback.** None is needed: the pods are the baseline, and no artifact was ever moved to the VM.
- **Effect on the target.** Its lots and its dates do not change: no lot of the target ever depended on G-A (section 5.10). Its priority does. Immo runs on a path measured at several minutes per publication until the cutover: the dedicated sentropic slots of decision D6 are necessary rather than recommended.

### 4.5 Decommission of the VM (K-0)

`[JUGEMENT]` A k8s act. The owner's directive is its GO, and one condition comes with it: the pool serves builds.

**Before the deletion, all of:**

1. *The pool serves builds.* In preprod, through the real MCP operations: one publication of a git-mode artifact completes on the pod builder, and one live session opens and shows an edit — with the pool as it is sized that day, a start from zero nodes included if it is at min 0. s-conductor runs it with i-cond and hands the evidence to the k8s session: job and release identifiers, durations, scale-up time of the node.
2. *Nothing selects the VM.* No selector is set to `execution-node` in the deployed configuration: none in `deploy/**` at `sen8:` (section 4.2), and the k8s session checks the live ConfigMaps of the API and of `artifact-content` against drift.
3. *The agent is idle.* The node is drained (`POST /v1/node/drain` `[FAIT: DOSSIER:587]`) and reports no session and no job of either tenant.

**Acts:** stop and disable the agent service; delete the VM; remove the allowlist entries and revoke the client certificates of the agent link `[FAIT: sen:apps/sandbox-node/OPERATOR.md:81-83]`; delete the per-tenant base prefixes of the bucket `sentropic-execution-bhs` `[FAIT: sen:apps/sandbox-node/OPERATOR.md:143-144]` after the retention that the k8s session records (retention period: `unknown`); remove the VM unit and its cloud-init from the `poc-k8s` git `[FAIT: DOSSIER:301]`.

**The notice of the step-1 spec.** That spec, frozen and co-signed, allows the removal of the node "only with >= 7 days notice plus evidence" `[FAIT: sen8:spec/SPEC_EVOL_SANDBOX_NODE.md:8]`. The owner's directive decommissions the VM without that delay, and this plan follows the later decision. The evidence is kept: it is the three conditions above. The notice protected tenants that would build on the node, and none does.

**Rollback.** None is planned. The VM is reconstructible from its cloud-init, with a new public IPv4 `[FAIT: DOSSIER:301]`, and the code that would use it stays on `main` until VD-7.2; rebuilding it would reopen step 1, which is the owner's decision alone.

**Duration and date:** `unknown`; section 5.10 assumes the act done 24 h after the directive. No lot of the target waits for it; VD-7.2 follows it.

### 4.6 Retirement of the pod builder, of the pool and of the step-1 code

`[JUGEMENT]` A k8s act on owner GO, then a code removal.

**Preconditions, all required:**

1. G-V8-APP passed: the V8 build is qualified at application scale, in both modes (section 5.3).
2. Every consumer has its V8 gate: G4 for dossiers; for immo, G5-HOST — the real immo MCP with the remote builder disabled — and then G5a or G5b according to decision D2; for geo, G6, live session included, on the access chain of VD-6.3 — geo builds nothing on the pods today, but the owner's order places geo before the retirement. The inventory of VD-7.0 shows no other artifact that still needs the pods. Git-mode artifacts of tenant sentropic: `unknown`, established by VD-7.0.
3. Cutover done (VD-7.1): every registered repository builds in V8 in preprod; the pool still exists; the rollback of section 4.7 has been rehearsed in both directions, and every moved artifact has its rollback pair recorded.
4. Stability. The dossier conditions the removal of the pool on at least 7 qualified days and stability evidence `[FAIT: DOSSIER:308,312]`. Evidence here: 7 consecutive days with no job on the pool, and no V8 build refused for a reason the builder would have accepted. The 7 days are counted from the end of the cutover (VD-7.1): until then jobs still run on the pods, so no earlier window can show zero job. The notice of at least 7 days that revisions 1 to 7 set beside this condition came from the step-1 spec and bore on the node (section 4.5): it has no object for the pool, and it never governed the date. The k8s session is told the conditional date by VD-7.0.
5. Owner GO.

**Acts of the k8s session (K-7):** delete the node pool `artifact-builders` and its configuration in the repository of the k8s session. The acts on the VM are those of K-0. Between the cutover and K-7 the pool is a rollback target only: under decision D14-A it returns to min 0 at the cutover.

**Acts of sentropic (VD-7.2), after K-7 and K-0:** delete the pod builder and its composition, the builder variables of the overlays, the two selectors, and the execution-node code of step 1 — the agent `apps/sandbox-node`, the API-side adapter, the data plane of `apps/artifact-content` (section 4.3). The contract module `apps/sandbox-node/src/contract.ts` is not deleted: it carries the isolate profile of the target (section 3.4). It is first moved to `packages/cluster-mesh/src/compile/contract/`, with a test that every re-export still matches, and only then is the agent deleted.

**Rollback at each stage:**

| Stage | Rollback |
|---|---|
| After the cutover, before K-7 | Two levels, both needed (section 4.7). Hosts: revert of the cutover configuration commit in `sentropic` and in `radar-immobilier`, then a restart — minutes, plus the cold start of a node when the pool is at zero. Artifacts: each moved artifact re-attached to its recorded rollback pair, by its owner. Changing configuration alone restores no artifact: revision 3 stated that a change of the selectors did |
| After K-7, before VD-7.2 | Recreate the node pool: a k8s act, duration `unknown`; then the previous row. The rollback pairs are kept until VD-7.2 |
| After VD-7.2 | Revert the removal commit and redeploy, then the previous row |

### 4.7 Rollback after the cutover: what is restored, and what happens to the work done since

`[FAIT]` Why a change of configuration alone is not a rollback (round-3 review, finding 2; it named the two selectors of step 1, which no cutover sets any more):

- **A binding is frozen on its branch and its executor.** An active binding refuses a change of repository, branch, sub-path or executor with `git_binding_conflict` `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/git-tools-attach.ts:58-62]`. Publication asks the build port whether it admits the executor stored in the binding, and requests the job with that executor `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/git-tools-publish.ts:37,45-47]`.
- **The old path admits neither the new executor nor the new descriptor.** Immo's builder client admits `sandbox` only, and only while its base URL is set `[immo4:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/build-api.ts:162-164]`. The validator of the historical path refuses a descriptor whose version is not 1, and any key outside its six fields `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/build-contract.ts:86-87]`.
- **One artifact has one binding; it moves by detach and attach.** A detach moves the binding to `detached`, increments its generation and clears its pending job. The next attach takes a new branch and a new executor, increments the generation again and keeps the release commit `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/git-tools-attach.ts:21-22,35-40,82-86]`. Both are operations of the artifact's owner, with the code right.
- **The builder URL of immo is the base of three clients**: the build client, the live worker client and the live grant client `[immo4:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/compose.ts:273,302; immo4:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/live-forward.ts:227; immo4:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/live-grant.ts:8,113]`. It is set in two files of immo's preprod overlay `[immo4:deploy/overlays/preprod/patch-artifacts-control.yaml:42; immo4:deploy/overlays/preprod/patch-immo-mcp-config.yaml:84]`.
- **A live draft belongs to a binding generation.** The live service derives its identity from the generation of the binding, the draft store is keyed by it, and a draft is kept 7 days after its last renewal `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-service.ts:70; sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-store.ts:4; sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-drafts.ts:7,48]`. After a detach and an attach, the draft of the former generation is no longer the binding's draft.
- **A release is immutable, and the current release can be set back.** `artifacts_revert` publishes a release that restores a named earlier one `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/mcp-core.ts:147; sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/publication.ts:697-699]`.

`[JUGEMENT]` Rule: **the cutover changes selection and never destroys what the builder path needs; until K-7 every moved artifact keeps a rollback pair.**

| What the cutover changes | What the rollback restores | By whom, how |
|---|---|---|
| **Ports.** After VD-2.14, VD-2.9, VD-5.6 and VD-5.4, each host composes two build ports and two live ports; the executor stored in the binding selects — `isolate` the in-process ports, `sandbox` the builder clients. The cutover removes no code | Nothing to restore in code: the builder ports stay composed until VD-7.2 in `sentropic`, and until immo's removal lot in `radar-immobilier`. The pod-bound form of the live assertion and the URL shapes of the Vite dev server stay verified for a `sandbox` session during the same time | — |
| **Configuration.** `sentropic`: the builder flag, in the preprod overlay; no selector is set (section 4.2). `radar-immobilier`: `IMMO_ARTIFACTS_BUILDER_URL`, unset in the two overlay files | The values of before the cutover | The cutover is one configuration commit per repository, made by VD-7.1: s-conductor merges the one of `sentropic`, i-cond the one of `radar-immobilier`, prepared by VD-5.4. The rollback is the revert of each commit, then a restart of the processes that read it — the API reads its configuration at start `[FAIT: NIGHT:126]`. The builder is the pod builder on the pool; a node starts from zero if the pool is at min 0 |
| **Bindings.** An artifact that existed before the cutover is detached from its branch of before (version-1 descriptor, executor `sandbox`) and attached to a branch of the candidate (version-2 descriptor, executor `isolate`) | The binding of before: same repository, branch, sub-path and executor | The **rollback pair**, recorded by VD-7.1 before it moves the artifact: the two attach argument sets, the head of the legacy branch, the release commit and the identifier of the last builder release with its receipt. The owner of the artifact detaches it and attaches it with the legacy arguments, through `artifacts_git_attach`; the return to V8 is the same operation with the other set. The host admits `sandbox` again as soon as the builder URL is set |
| **Sources.** The moved artifact is built from candidate sources, which the builder never built | A snapshot that the version-1 path builds: the head of the legacy branch recorded in the pair — a version-1 descriptor, the sources and the lockfile of the last builder release, whose receipt names commit, tree, lockfile digest, descriptor digest and builder image `[FAIT: sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/build-contract.ts:42-51]` | The legacy branch is kept untouched from the cutover to K-7: not deleted, not rebased, not given a version-2 descriptor. i-cond checks its presence and its head at the rehearsal and before K-7. The candidate sources are never handed to the builder: their Vite build was never qualified on the pod builder, and nothing is optimised on that path (section 4.4) |

What happens to the sessions and the changes made since the cutover `[JUGEMENT]`:

| Item | During a rollback | At the return to V8 |
|---|---|---|
| Open V8 live sessions | Stopped by their owner before the switch; a session left open ends on the idle rule, 900 s `[FAIT: sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-store.ts:5-6]`. Its invitations are bound to the session and end with it `[FAIT: sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-store.ts:50-53]` | A new session is opened |
| Uncommitted V8 drafts | Not reachable after the switch (generation rule above). Before any switch the runbook lists the artifacts whose draft holds changes (`artifacts_live_status`); the owner commits the draft to the candidate branch (`artifacts_git_commit`) or abandons it (`artifacts_live_reset`). No artifact is switched without that choice | A committed draft is in the head of the candidate branch. An abandoned one is gone |
| Commits on the candidate branch | Stay in git, untouched. Not built: the builder does not build the candidate | Built again by the first V8 publication |
| Releases published by V8 | Immutable; they stay stored and served, and their receipts keep executor `isolate`. The current release stays the last V8 release, unless the reason of the rollback is a defective V8 output: the owner then sets the last builder release back with `artifacts_revert` | The next V8 publication becomes the current release |
| Changes made on the legacy pair during the rollback | Commits on the legacy branch, releases with executor `sandbox` | Not carried to the candidate: the framework differs. Their author re-authors them on the candidate, as VD-5.7 does for the three files of Focus-signal. The runbook says so to each owner before the switch |
| An artifact born in V8, with no legacy pair — geo's | Its releases stay served. It publishes nothing and opens no live session: it had no builder path before the cutover either | Unchanged |
| Pending jobs | None is left: each switch waits for the reconciler to report no pending job `[FAIT: immo4:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/compose.ts:282-289]` | Same |

- **Duration.** Host level: minutes. Artifact level: one detach and one attach per moved artifact, by its owner. The number of artifacts to move is given by the inventory of VD-7.0: `unknown` today.
- **Rehearsal.** Part of the exit gate of VD-7.1 (section 5.8), on a witness artifact of each consumer host that has a legacy pair; the same switch is first qualified on immo's host by item 9 of G5-HOST (section 5.6). The 7 days of observation start when the rehearsal has returned to V8.
- **Owners.** s-conductor: the configuration commit of `sentropic`, the record of the pairs, the rehearsal. i-cond: the configuration commit of `radar-immobilier`, the runbook of the immo host, the switch of immo's witness. The owner of each artifact: its own switch.

---

## 5. Lots

### 5.1 Rules common to every lot

`[JUGEMENT]` unless tagged.

- One lot = one branch = one pull request in one repository, with a `BRANCH.md` that lists allowed and forbidden paths. Default forbidden paths in `sentropic` (`Makefile`, `docker-compose*.yml`, `.cursor/rules/**`) need a numbered exception `BRxx-EXn`; the tables mark them "(exception)".
- Lane = repository conductor and builder grade. Build: Sol 6.1 at grade X or H. Review: an independent reviewer assigned by the conductor of the repository. Merge: s-conductor in `sentropic`, h-cond in `h2a`, the DS conductor, i-cond in `radar-immobilier`, geo-cond in `geo` (repository `rhanka/geo`; lane brief, section GEO OWNERSHIP).
- Allowed paths of a lot are the paths of its branch, under the authority of the repository conductor. They are not the devScope of an artifact session: in `radar-immobilier`, what a live session or an artifact branch may write is fixed by the registry alone (section 2.8).
- Elapsed time of a lot = build hours + overhead (4 h, 3 h or 2 h, see Conventions).
- A lot that already exists in another plan keeps its owner's definition and figure; this plan orders it and states its dependencies. Where the owning plan gives no figure, this plan gives a placeholder marked **†**, to be replaced by the figure of the owning conductor.
- No Python in any job or image; every job is Node or an existing native binary.
- Every exit gate names evidence that a reviewer can re-run.
