| VD-2.13 Hot replacement | sentropic · X | 28 = 18 + 8 (revision 5): patch frames, the pre-armed patch request, the consumer clocks and the series; + 1 (revision 7): the marks of the hand-over and of the flush in every edit, in place of the control run, and the witness of the page; + 1 (revision 8): the wrapper that marks the render intervals, the diagnostic proxy of the hot-replacement entry and three more points of the witness; + 0 (revision 9): the criterion bears on the whole `p0 → p2` and the wrapper stays, as an instrument of information — the criterion of the render, its series and the cases of a wrapper that cannot be installed leave the gate, and two points join the witness | VD-2.9 | On the fixture, N = 20 one-file edits of a mounted SFC, template then script: 0 full reload; a state witness in a sibling component (a counter, an open overlay) is intact — at the pin a script edit re-renders the Vapor parent of the edited component and creates its siblings again (section 2.9): if question 10 of VD-2.0 confirms it, the lot reports the witness as lost for that edit kind, the loss follows the amendment rule of section 2.9, and the gate does not count it as intact; an edit of a non-component module updates its importers with no reload; an edit of the shell reloads and is reported as a reload; same results when the draft manifest is switched by the outputs of the isolate and when it is switched by outputs written to the draft store in the form of a browser push — written by the test: the endpoint is VD-2.11. Latency (section 2.13), in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, with a stub host that hands prepared revisions to the patch endpoint, N = 200 per edit kind after 20 warm-up edits: a style-only revision is applied from the frame with no request; a module revision arrives by the pre-armed patch request under the unchanged policy `script-src 'self'`, with 0 fallback on the announcement. Claimed, each under 10 ms: the patch endpoint answers within ≤ 0.3 ms at p50 and ≤ 1 ms at p95 of the revision reaching it — the whole interval `c0 → c1` is closed by G-LIVE-MS, when the receiver of VD-2.16 exists; the replacement in the page — the whole of `p0 → p2`, from the patch received to the DOM mutated: the evaluation of the patch module, the work of Vue's hot-replacement runtime, the registration of the children that a render creates, the `setup` and `render` functions of the components, the hooks that the replacement runs and every wait (section 2.13) — takes ≤ 2 ms at p50 and ≤ 5 ms at p95, the resolution of the page clock added once. Outside the claim, against its own number: `p2 → p3` within one frame at p50 and two at p95. Reported for information, with no criterion: the component's own render — the render intervals `f0 → f1` of the edit, marked by the wrapper of the client runtime around the outermost `setup` and `render` calls of the registered components — and the three parts of the replacement, `p0 → r0`, `r0 → r1` and `r1 → p2`. Neither is subtracted from the replacement and neither excuses an overrun; where the wrapper cannot be installed (question 10 of VD-2.0) the render is not reported, and the criterion is unchanged. No control run and no subtraction. An overrun of `p0 → p2` fails this gate and opens decision D12 with the numbers. The series are run on the three paths of the pinned runtime: a Vapor re-render, a Vapor re-creation under Vapor parents, and a re-creation under a classic parent. Witness of the page, in the three engines: 20 ms injected under the diagnostic flag at each of the seven points of section 2.13 — before `r0`; inside the hot-replacement call before the runtime starts, through a proxy of `rerender` and `reload`; at the entry of the wrapper, before `f0`; inside the `render` function of the fixture; in `registerHMR` of a child that this render function creates, before the `setup` of that child; at the exit of the wrapper, after `f1`; after `r1` — fail the criterion of the replacement each time. The fifth point is the case of the round-8 review — work of the hot replacement nested in the render of a parent: under the marks of revision 8 it passed, as render. A diff witness shows that the live profile adds its registration at module level only: the `render` and `setup` functions of the emitted module are those of the same compile without hot replacement; the wrapper is installed by the client runtime, keeps the arity of the functions it wraps, and its two marks are the only code of this chain on the path of a render: the fixtures render the same DOM with the wrapper and without it. The marks `c1`, `e0` and `e1` of the stub edge, `p0` to `p3`, `r0`, `r1` and the pairs `f0` and `f1` travel with the revision; the ping gives the offset and its uncertainty; a series that does not close, or whose uncertainty exceeds 1 ms, is void. Revision 4 bounded the visible DOM at p95 ≤ 500 ms; revision 6 took the swap as a difference with a control run (round-6 review, finding 1); revision 7 took the render as the whole hot-replacement call (round-7 review); revision 8 took it as the intervals of the outermost `setup` and `render` calls, which hold the registration of the children (round-8 review) | `packages/cluster-mesh/src/compile/live/**` (new), `packages/artifacts/src/server/live-*.ts`, `apps/artifact-content/src/**` (the patch endpoint of the page), tests |
| VD-2.15 Live compile session | sentropic · X | 18 = 16 + 2 (revision 6): the block dependencies of the memo and its edit matrix | VD-2.3 (the adapted runtime), VD-2.7 (the memo it extends); slot after VD-2.14 | On the real addon, in the production image. (1) The live profile is an entry of `AMD-ISO` — one isolate per live session, its lifecycle, its limits, its accounting: one edit at a time per session, counted apart from the compile jobs of the tenant, so that an edit queues neither behind another session nor behind a build — and `execution-node.v1` and the compile profile of VD-2.1 are unchanged: the conformance vectors still validate. (2) The isolate of a session is created at its start and used by every edit of that session and by nothing else: a second session of the same tenant and principal gets another isolate, and a witness shows that a source of the first is unreachable from the second. (3) It is disposed at the stop of the session, at its idle expiry, on revocation, on a change of policy or compiler and on a memory or deadline fault; its reservation is released exactly once, inside the aggregate accounting of the host `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:216-222]`. (4) Block memo (section 2.7). The scope identifier of an SFC is the same before and after every edit of the matrix. Counters assert what each edit recompiles: a plain style edit, that style block alone; a plain template edit, the template alone; a script edit, the script, and the template only when the binding metadata changed; each crossing edit, what the dependency table names — a changed `v-bind()` list the script, a changed `scoped` state or `:slotted` flag the template, a template that starts or stops using an imported binding the script. A block with a `src` attribute, a custom block and a parse error recompile the whole SFC; a `<style module>` block is refused. For every edit of the matrix, on each fixture, the emitted bytes equal those of a compile of the whole SFC in a fresh context. (5) Stages 3 and 4 of section 2.13 on F-LIVE-S and F-LIVE-M: N = 200 per edit kind after 20 warm-up edits — style, template, whole, the crossing edits being held to the whole kind — p50 and p95 inside the criteria of each class, all under 10 ms, under the pod limits that K-2 publishes. F-LIVE-L goes through the same series: it is reported, has no criterion and passes nothing (decision D13). The cold edit is measured against its bound. (6) The resident memory of one warm session and of the published number of warm sessions is reported against the budget of K-2; one session more is admitted on the cold path and reported as such by `artifacts_live_status` | `apps/sandbox-node/src/contract.ts`, `packages/cluster-mesh/src/compile/{contract,core,plan,isolate}/**`, `packages/skills/src/sandbox/**`, `packages/skills/tests/sandbox/**`, `packages/cluster-mesh/tests/compile/**` |
| VD-2.16 Live write fast path | sentropic · X | 16 | VD-2.9 (the live port it completes), VD-2.15 | On a session of an `isolate` binding. (1) A write makes no call to the forge: head, tree and base blobs of the graph are pinned at the start of the session; a head moved on the forge is reported by `artifacts_live_status` after the periodic check and refused at the commit with `git_head_moved`, as today. (2) The edit is applied to the draft in memory and handed in process to the live port with the changed files; the durable draft is written behind, one write in flight at a time, and the call is acknowledged after it — "persist the draft before acknowledging" stays true; a failed durable write returns the session and the page to the last durable revision and fails the call; after a restart of the host the session is rebuilt from the durable draft. (3) The host pushes each revision to the content host on a persistent connection, signed as its existing pushes are, with the changed outputs; the content host answers the page from memory, with no store read. The push is attached to the switch of the draft manifest, whoever wrote the outputs: a switch made by outputs written to the draft store in the form of a browser push — written by the test; the endpoint is VD-2.11 — leaves by the same path. (4) The marks `h0` to `h5` and `c0` are recorded and travel with the revision. Stages 1, 2 and 4, the send of the host (`h4 → h5`: ≤ 0.2 ms at p50, ≤ 0.5 ms at p95) and the server path `h0 → h4` are inside the criteria of section 2.13, all under 10 ms, for the three edit kinds on F-LIVE-S and F-LIVE-M, N = 200 after 20 warm-up edits; the acknowledgement is inside its own number, outside the claim. A delay injected under the diagnostic flag between `h4` and `h5` shows in that interval and fails its criterion. (5) A burst of 50 edits is applied in order, and the final revision equals the durable draft. (6) After a series of edits, `artifacts_git_commit` creates a commit whose tree is the `liveTreeSha` of the session, and the page receives no revision for it (section 2.12, rule 3) | `packages/artifacts/src/server/live-*.ts`, `packages/artifacts/src/adapters/postgres-live.ts`, `packages/artifacts/tests/**`, `api/src/services/artifacts/live/v8-*.ts`, `apps/artifact-content/src/**` (the receiver of pushed revisions and their memory). VD-2.13 runs beside this lot: s-conductor fixes at their launch the split of the files they both name, so that they share none |
| K-2 | k8s | `unknown`; section 5.10 assumes it in service 36 h after the origin | decisions D5 and D9; owner GO for the deployment of the limit change | BHS bucket and route of the mirror in service; RAM, CPU and disk budgets of each MCP host — the sentropic API and the immo host — published, with the number of warm live sessions each may hold and a CPU figure that lets one publication build beside their edits (section 2.13); the changes of the memory and CPU limits deployed | k8s scope; `deploy/k8s/**` (exception) |

The sentropic host is wired by the lots above: VD-2.8 and VD-2.14 compose the build port in `api/src/services/artifacts`, VD-2.9 the live port, and VD-2.16 its fast path. The immo host is wired by VD-5.6 and VD-5.4, and the access chain of an artifact hosted by the sentropic host by VD-6.3.

**Gate G-V8-b** (server). In the production image of the MCP host, on the real addon:

1. The seed corpus of VD-2.2 and the DS reference corpus of V13a compile; cold and warm runs give identical manifests.
2. The adverse suite is refused with typed codes: endless loop, heap exhaustion, import escape, oversize input, missing addon.
3. The witnesses M1 (two resources of one tenant, independent revocation), M3 (two callers, one cancels, the other gets its result) and M4 (aggregate budgets under churn) pass `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:137,214-222]`.
4. A process-level witness shows no child process, no socket and no file read outside the cache directory during a compile.
5. The catalogue hook refuses an invalid document before any render (gate G-Q5 `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:356]`); every response validates against the one shared diagnostic schema (witness M2), and a validator that does not return is really stopped at its deadline (witness M5) `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:295,356]`. G3 needs the six witnesses: M1, M3 and M4 are item 3, M2 and M5 this item, M6 the two snapshot lots.
6. One MCP call builds a recipe and returns its manifest.

**Gate G-V8-a** (browser).

1. `ArtifactBuilder` compiles the same corpus in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, inside a Web Worker.
2. Every manifest digest equals the server's.
3. The compiled output is never executed in the product origin: the component hands it to the draft push only. The preview has its own gate, below.
4. No request leaves for an origin other than the mirror and the product API.
5. Cancel terminates the worker within 250 ms, the acknowledgement budget of the server side `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:328]`.
6. The component uses DS Vue components only.

**Gate G-V8-PREVIEW** (browser preview, in the real component). In Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, with `ArtifactBuilder` running in a page of a product origin:

1. The component builds the fixture in its Web Worker, pushes the draft and opens the preview on the public origin of the artifact, where the session is served live. The preview is a top-level document. Its response headers are those of application mode, unchanged — `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin` — with `worker-src 'self'`.
2. MapLibre, with the pinned 6.4.1 files unbundled: the map fires `load` and renders a GeoJSON source. The browser reports a dedicated worker whose URL is the manifest file `maplibre-gl-worker.mjs`; that worker's request for `maplibre-gl-shared.mjs` succeeds; no `blob:` worker exists.
3. pdf.js 4.10.38: a page of a PDF renders; a dedicated worker with the URL of `pdf.worker.min.mjs` exists; the console holds no "Setting up fake worker" warning.
4. The application worker of the fixture loads one relative import and one closure import, with no import map.
5. No CSP violation is reported. Every module, the inline import map and the preload links load with their integrity.
6. Styles: the witness list of section 2.6 — computed styles, loaded font, order witness — passes against the Vite build of the same sources.
7. Data: one application call relayed through the reader host returns under the session grant, and one request on the artifact's own tile path returns (a stub reader host in this gate, the real one in G5a). No request leaves that origin.
8. Separation: the preview reads neither the cookies nor the storage of the product origin, and neither window holds a reference to the other.
9. Authority: the server refuses to publish the pushed draft as a release, and refuses a push by an invitee.
10. After a one-file edit in the component, the preview shows the change through the revision channel of VD-2.9. This item is functional, as it was in revision 4: the refresh may be a reload here, and the time from the edit to the change is reported without a criterion. Revision 5 put the browser-mode budget in this item, which uses the delivery of VD-2.16 and the patch request of VD-2.13 — two lots that VD-2.11 does not wait for (round-5 review, finding 5). That budget is item 3 of G-LIVE-MS, carried by VD-2.10 after VD-2.11, VD-2.13, VD-2.15 and VD-2.16.
11. The pushed draft manifest names the base commit and the tree of the draft it was built from; a push built on another base than the session's is refused (section 2.12, rule 7).

**Gate G-V8-APP** (application scale). On a Vue Vapor application fixture of at least 150 source modules — the size class of the immo UI (69 + 116 modules, section 2.9) — that includes a private TypeScript workspace, an alias, stylesheet imports, a map view and a PDF view:

1. A cold full build completes in less than 60 s in both modes (owner target `[FAIT: NIGHT:154]`).
2. After a one-file edit, the live manifest is ready inside the server path of section 2.13 — for a whole edit of class M, p50 ≤ 5 ms and p95 ≤ 9 ms: item 1 of G-LIVE-MS, below. Revision 4 asked for less than 1 s at p95.
3. The published release shows the same DOM landmarks as the Vite build of the same sources, with no console or page error.
4. Load time of the V8 output against the Vite output, N = 20, both published. Provisional bound: p95 at most 25 % slower; it is replaced by the thresholds of the immo protocol when immo supplies them `[FAIT: PLAN:296,1036]`.
5. Isolate and browser manifests are equal.
6. File count and bytes of the release are reported against the ceilings `[FAIT: sen:packages/artifacts/src/server/build-output.ts:20-24]`.
7. Hot replacement at this scale, in both modes: gate G-LIVE-MS, below; and the consumer protocol of G-WRITE on the same build — N = 20 one-file edits of a view, 0 full reload, state witnesses intact.
8. The browser preview of this fixture passes items 2 to 8 of G-V8-PREVIEW in the three engines.
9. The release of items 3 and 4 is published by the plan execution of VD-2.14, with every builder variable unset.
10. The DS's own run of its reference corpus in the isolate runtime (lot V13b `[FAIT: PLAN:598]`) is green: the same digests as in the Web Worker.
11. Committed code (section 2.12), on one commit of the fixture and one binding. *Mirror:* two publications, the second after a renewal of the mirror manifest that keeps the pinned one retained, give one root manifest digest; the isolate and the browser give that digest; with the pinned manifest withdrawn from the admitted set, the publication is refused with a typed code that names the pin and nothing is built. *Policy:* the host admits a newer compile policy revision and makes it its default, with other option defaults — a third publication of the same commit gives the same recipe keys and the same root manifest digest; with the pinned revision withdrawn from the admitted set, the publication is refused with a typed code that names it, and nothing is built under the newer one; a commit that moves the lock to the newer revision gives other keys. *Host:* after a restart of the host and after an upgrade of its packages that moves no pin, the digest is the same. *Authorisation:* with every pin admitted, a principal without the right, a revoked custody and a destination that is not authorised are refused as today. *Binding:* the same commit published under a second binding gives other recipe keys and the same SHA-256 for every output file. A draft of several edits saved by `artifacts_git_commit` gives a commit whose tree is the `liveTreeSha` of the session; the publication of that commit lists the source digests of the last live revision; the save sends no revision to the page.

**Gate G-LIVE-MS** (live in milliseconds; carried by VD-2.10 with G-V8-APP). In preprod, in the production image of the MCP host, under the pod limits that K-2 publishes — the same series under the current limits is recorded beside it — with the harness browsers in the region of the host, on F-LIVE-S, on F-LIVE-M and on a view of class M of the application fixture, with the marks, the series and the validity rules of section 2.13:

1. **Claimed — every figure under 10 ms.** Server mode, an edit made through `artifacts_live_write`; for each size class of the profile and each edit kind, N = 200 edits after 20 warm-up edits. Every processing interval is inside its p50 and its p95 criterion: stages 1 to 4, the send of the host, the content host, the stub edge, and the replacement in the page — the whole of `p0 → p2`, ≤ 2 ms at p50 and ≤ 5 ms at p95: the work of Vue's hot-replacement runtime, the registration of the children that a render creates and the render of the component included. The server path `h0 → h4` is inside the criterion of its class and kind — for a whole edit of class M, p50 ≤ 5 ms and p95 ≤ 9 ms. The sum of the claimed intervals between `h0` and `p2` is ≤ 9 ms at p50. Its p95 is not claimed; it is held to a ceiling, so that no tail hides between the intervals. That ceiling is the owner's: the gate applies the figure of the answer to decision D13 — the sum of the p95 criteria of the parts, 15.5 ms for a whole edit of class M and 13 ms for class S, if the owner keeps the ceiling that this plan proposes; 9 ms if the owner requires the sum inside the range (D13-D). Without that answer the gate is not pronounced: decision D13 is an edge of this gate (section 5.10). Revision 6 applied the proposed figure without it (round-6 review, finding 2).
2. **Outside the claim — each against its own number, none taken out of item 1.** The transit of the delivery: exactly one, ≤ 2 ms at p50 and ≤ 5 ms at p95, the clock uncertainty added. The display: one frame at p50, two at p95. The acknowledgement of the tool call: ≤ 8 ms at p50 and ≤ 25 ms at p95. Nothing that the page does between `p0` and `p2` is outside item 1. The component's own render — the render intervals `f0 → f1` of every edit, added — is reported beside the replacement, for information: it has no criterion, it is subtracted from nothing, and it excuses no overrun of item 1.
3. Browser mode, an edit made in `ArtifactBuilder`: the same series in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, with the same criteria in the three engines. The worker path is inside the criteria of the server path; the product API hashes and admits the push in ≤ 1 ms at p50 and ≤ 3 ms at p95; content host, edge and replacement in the page as in item 1; two transits, each against the number of item 2.
4. Validity. Every series closes — its intervals add up to `p2 − h0` within the clock uncertainty — with an uncertainty of at most 1 ms, 0 full reload, 0 cold edit and 0 fallback of the delivery; the state witnesses are intact. A void series is run again; it is never counted.
5. Witness of the measurement. A delay of 20 ms injected in the host after `h4`, then in the content host, then in the edge, then at the seven points of the page (section 2.13) — before `r0`; inside the hot-replacement call before the runtime starts; at the entry of the wrapper, before `f0`; inside the `render` function of the fixture; in `registerHMR` of a child that this render function creates, before the `setup` of that child; at the exit of the wrapper, after `f1`; after `r1` — fails item 1 on the interval of that hop each time, in the page on the replacement; the measured transit moves by less than the uncertainty. No delay of the page passes as render: the two points that the marks of revision 8 counted as render — the render function itself, and `registerHMR` of a child during the render of its parent, the case of the round-8 review — fail item 1 like the five others. The delays of the page use the hooks that VD-2.13 delivers and has run on its stub host: they are run again here, end to end.
6. Concurrency. The series of item 1 on F-LIVE-M is run again while a second live session of the same tenant takes edits at the same rate, and once more while a publication of the same tenant builds on the same host: item 1 holds in both.
7. Crossing edits (section 2.7): one series; inside the criteria of the whole kind.
8. Cold: one edit after a forced eviction of the session isolate; server path p95 ≤ 500 ms over 20 repetitions; reported apart — it is not an update of a warm session.
9. Burst: 50 edits back to back are applied in order; the page ends on the last revision.
10. Above the live size profile: F-LIVE-L goes through the same series and is reported. It has no criterion and passes nothing; its numbers go to decision D13.
11. Opening of a session, cold and warm, from the accepted request to the first render: reported against the bound of the builder dossier `[FAIT: DOSSIER:184]`; no threshold of this plan.
12. The report gives, per interval: p50, p95, p99 and maximum; the transits, the round trips and the uncertainty; for information, the three parts of the replacement and the component's own render; the node flavor, the pod limits and the resident memory of the host; the flavor of the machine that runs the harness browsers.

A criterion that is missed fails the gate; the lot then writes the numbers and decision D12 is opened (section 2.13). An overrun of the replacement `p0 → p2` is such a miss: it is written with `p0 → p2`, its three parts and the render measured apart, and the measured render does not excuse it. The gate is never passed on a widened figure, and no quantity moves from item 1 to item 2. Nor is it passed on a ceiling that the owner has not stated: the lot may finish its series before the answer to decision D13, and the verdict waits for that answer. G-V8-APP holds this gate in its items 2 and 7: it is pronounced with it, not before.

Every merge in `h2a` needs an owner GO, and every tag a separate one `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:387]`. The schedule carries the first as node `GO-H2A`, an edge of each h2a lot, and the second as `GO-TAG` at G3; both are counted at zero wait (section 5.10, assumption 6). Revision 3 carried one GO, at G3.

The two snapshot lots are on the path of G3. Revision 1 set them aside because a dossier delivered to immo renders in the browser; the h2a spec does not allow that shortcut: L3-default follows the snapshot gate `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:435]` and G3 requires witness M6. No change of the h2a spec is requested. Their hours and lanes are in the table above and their slots in section 5.10.

### 5.6 Step 5 — the immo UI on Vue Vapor

Existing lots, owned by the DS plan and immo `[FAIT: PLAN:648-665]`: I0a (6 h), I0b (10), I0 (14), I2a (16), I2b (16), I3a (14), I3b (16), I4 (16), I5 (14), I6 (14), I7 (14), I8 (14), I14 (6), I10a (12), I10b (12), I12 (18) and, on owner GO, I11 (8): 220 h for the Vue pass. Milestone M-IMMO is immo's acceptance of the as-is candidate `[FAIT: PLAN:673]`.

**Where the candidate lives.** `[FAIT]` The DS plan creates the candidate in two new top-level roots, `ui-shared/` and `ui-vue/` (lots I0 and I14 `[PLAN:442-443,650,661]`), a layout it asks immo to agree `[PLAN:1036]`. The registry authorises writes under `ui/**` and in three package families only `[immo:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/registry.ts:15-20]`. `[JUGEMENT]` A candidate in those roots could be built, since compile read needs no writable path; it could not be edited by a live session or an artifact branch, and gates G5a and G5b open live sessions. This plan therefore names no path outside the registry: its lots are written against "the candidate files, inside the writable ceiling of the registry". Whether the candidate stays under `ui/**` or the registry gains paths is decision D11. A new path is the owner's act in immo's `registry.ts`; it is not a choice of this plan, of the DS plan or of a builder.

What makes this order safe, and what it costs. A lot that starts before G-V8-APP works on a V8 build that is not qualified yet. Three things bound that exposure. The anticipated lots are static — a profile, a host wiring for publication, a publication — where a defect of the build shows as a refusal or as a difference of digests. Every such defect goes back to the sentropic lot that owns it and becomes a case of VD-2.10: immo's shell and immo's Focus entry are the first real sources that the build meets. And gate G5a-PUB holds for the compiler pin it was passed on: if G-V8-APP qualifies another pin, VD-5.2 runs the publication items again, which the build lock turns into a comparison of digests (section 2.12). Cost: three more lots, 14 h of build and 9 h of overhead in immo; the lot that revision 6 adds, VD-5.8, is counted apart (10 h and 3 h). Gain: G5a on 22/11 at 00:00 instead of 22/11 at 10:00 in revision 4, although the V8 chain itself ends 16 h later than in that revision. Decision D3 is needed 48 h earlier, at T+132.

**What VD-5.6 and VD-5.4 change in the immo host** `[JUGEMENT]`, on the facts of section 3.7. A package bump leaves all of it untouched, and no lot of revision 2 owned it. Revision 4 gave the whole list to VD-5.4. Since revision 5 two lots deliver it: VD-5.6, anticipated, delivers the build half of items 1 to 3 and item 6; VD-5.4 delivers the live half of items 1 to 3 and items 4, 5, 7 and 8.

1. *Composition.* The host composes two build ports and two live ports, and the executor stored in the binding selects: `isolate` goes to the in-process port of the packages and to the V8 live port, `sandbox` to the two HTTP clients of the remote builder. The in-process ports read the mirror through its client and keep their outputs in the tenant cache and in the draft store of immo's storage scope. The builder clients stay composed, and answer only while `IMMO_ARTIFACTS_BUILDER_URL` is set — before the cutover and during a rollback. They are removed by immo's own lot after K-7, not by this one. Today the host composes one build port and one live worker `[FAIT: immo4:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/compose.ts:272-278,302-307]`.
2. *Executor policy.* The host admits `isolate` for a binding whose branch carries a version-2 descriptor, and refuses `caller` and `ci` as today. `sandbox` stays admitted for a branch whose descriptor is version 1, and answers `builder_unavailable` while the builder URL is unset — the fail-closed behaviour that exists `[FAIT: immo4:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/build-api.ts:162-164]`. Today the policy refuses every executor but `sandbox` before any binding write `[FAIT: immo4:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/compose.ts:176-183]`.
3. *Existing bindings.* A binding attached with `sandbox` is neither rebuilt nor migrated in place: its releases stay served and its receipts stay as they are. It moves to `isolate` by a detach and an attach on a branch whose descriptor is version 2: an active binding refuses a change of branch or executor (section 4.7). Pending jobs reach a terminal state through the existing reconciler before the switch `[FAIT: immo4:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/compose.ts:282-289]`.
4. *Live.* For a session of an `isolate` binding, the edge signs the form of the live assertion without a pod and forwards the draft-manifest paths and the revision socket. For a session of a `sandbox` binding it keeps the pod-bound form, the Vite paths and the HMR socket of today. The live grant client reads its base from the builder URL today `[FAIT: immo4:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/live-forward.ts:227; immo4:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/live-grant.ts:8,113]`: the V8 live port gets a grant source that does not depend on that variable, so unsetting it closes the builder sessions and no other. Reserved paths, the release answer for a reader who is not admitted, and the revocation push do not change.
5. *Browser mode.* Immo's API gains the draft-push route of VD-2.11, bound to the session principal, and the candidate mounts `ArtifactBuilder` behind the permission of an artifact author. The route writes the pushed outputs to the draft store through the interface of VD-2.9. It runs in the process that holds the live port of the session, or hands the outputs to it — i-cond's choice — so that the switch of the draft manifest, and the delivery attached to it, is the same in both modes. Gate G5-HOST checks what the mode does (item 4); its latency through immo's edge is not gated (section 2.13).
6. *Process.* The addon loads in the image of the immo host (spike question 1), and a build plan stays inside the memory budget that K-2 publishes for that host.
7. *Rollback after the cutover* (section 4.7). The lot delivers what immo's side of it needs: the runbook, in `docs/operator/git-artifact-mode.md` — the list of drafts that hold changes, the owner's choice for each, the detach and the attach with the legacy arguments, the check that no job is pending, and the same steps towards V8; the cutover commit of immo's overlay, prepared and not merged; and the format of the rollback pair that VD-7.1 records. It changes no stored binding by itself.
8. *Latency path* (section 2.13). The immo MCP process composes the live port with the fast path of VD-2.16 and pushes each revision to `artifact-content` on a persistent connection. The network policy of the edge admits immo's pods `[FAIT: sen:deploy/k8s/overlays/preprod/artifact-content-live-edge-networkpolicy.yaml:17-28]`; whether it admits the pods of the immo MCP on that port is `unverified` and is settled with K-2. Immo's edge forwards the pre-armed patch request of the page, which the page re-arms inside the 10 s upstream timeout of that edge `[FAIT: immo5:api/src/artifacts/live-edge-forward.ts:110]`. The edge records its marks `e0` and `e1` and lets them travel with the patch: what it adds is processing between those two marks, ≤ 1 ms at p50 and ≤ 3 ms at p95, never a share of the network. Its grant is cached for 30 s and its assertion signed for every request `[FAIT: immo5:api/src/artifacts/live-edge-forward.ts:25-26,44]`.

**Gate G5-HOST** (the real immo MCP host, before G5a). On the preprod immo MCP, through its real MCP operations, with `IMMO_ARTIFACTS_BUILDER_URL` unset — the remote builder is then disabled — on a witness branch whose descriptor is version 2. The branch and its descriptor are created by the lot under i-cond, not by an artifact session. Item 9 alone sets the URL again. Items 1, 2, 5, 6 and 8 are passed first by VD-5.6, on the build port alone, and run again here.

1. `artifacts_git_attach` binds with executor `isolate`; `caller` and `ci` are refused; `sandbox` answers `builder_unavailable`.
2. `artifacts_git_publish` builds in the isolate of the immo host process and publishes. The receipt carries executor `isolate`, the unit recipe keys and the mirror manifest digest. A network witness shows no request to the builder API of sentropic during the run.
3. A live session opens and takes a write; the edit is served on the public host of the artifact through immo's edge; the revision channel announces it; a stop leaves no process and no pod.
4. Browser mode: a draft pushed through immo's API by the session principal is served on the same host; a push by an invitee is refused; the pushed draft cannot be published.
5. A witness binding created with `sandbox` on a version-1 descriptor, with one release built by the remote builder, keeps serving its releases; its publish request gets a typed refusal; its move to the version-2 branch — a detach, then an attach with `isolate` — succeeds, and its legacy pair is recorded first.
6. No job is left pending by the switch: the reconciler reports none.
7. Reader paths, the release answer for a reader who is not admitted, and the revocation push behave as before the lot: the existing live-edge suites stay green.
8. The host process stays inside the memory and CPU budget published by K-2 during a build plan, and with the published number of warm live sessions open.
9. Rollback pair, both directions, on the witness of item 5 and by the runbook. *Back:* a V8 draft left uncommitted is listed by the runbook and committed, and that head is published in the isolate; the builder URL is set again and the host restarted; the witness is detached and attached with its legacy arguments; `artifacts_git_publish` completes through the remote builder at the recorded head of the legacy branch, with executor `sandbox` in the receipt; a live session opens on the builder and shows one edit. *Forward:* the URL is unset and the host restarted; the witness is detached and attached with its V8 arguments; the committed draft is in the head of the candidate branch; a publication in the isolate at that head gives the root manifest digest it gave before the rollback; a V8 live session opens and shows one edit. After each switch the reconciler reports no pending job, the releases published before it are still listed and served, and a draft that was neither committed nor abandoned stops the runbook.
10. Latency (section 2.13), on the witness branch and through immo's edge, with the harness in the region, on a file inside the live size profile: N = 200 edits per edit kind after 20 warm-up edits. Claimed, each under 10 ms: the server path inside its criteria in the immo MCP process, under the budget that K-2 publishes for that host; the send of the host and the content host inside theirs; immo's edge, between its marks `e0` and `e1`, ≤ 1 ms at p50 and ≤ 3 ms at p95; the replacement in the page — the whole of `p0 → p2`, render included — inside its own; the sum of the claimed intervals ≤ 9 ms at p50, and at p95 inside the ceiling of the owner's answer to decision D13 — 18.5 ms through this edge if the owner keeps the proposed ceiling, 9 ms under D13-D. Outside the claim, each against its own number: one transit and the display. The component's own render is reported for information and excuses no overrun. Every series closes, with an uncertainty of at most 1 ms; 0 full reload, 0 cold edit, 0 fallback of the delivery. This item is not pronounced before the answer to decision D13, which is an edge of the gate (section 5.10).

**Gate G5a-PUB** (Focus-signal published in V8; exit of VD-5.7, anticipated). On the immo host wired by VD-5.6, with the builder URL unset. The Focus-signal entry — the two views of F-FOCUS-OPT, ported, with the three files of the artifact branch re-authored on the candidate — publishes through (b) at one commit of its branch, and (a) gives the same manifest digest for that commit. The preprod cases L0054, C-416 and 801-70 render in the published release `[FAIT: DOSSIER:88]`. The map and PDF workers are real dedicated workers under `worker-src 'self'`: items 2 and 3 of G-V8-PREVIEW, on the real views. The reader transport is installed: application calls return through the reader host. Twenty consecutive publications of that commit complete, each in less than 60 s, and each gives the root manifest digest of the first (section 2.12, rule 4). No builder job runs on the pods during the run. The receipt names the compiler pin: the gate holds for that pin.

**Gate G5a** (exit of VD-5.2). On the immo host wired by VD-5.4. The items of G5a-PUB hold on the compiler pin that G-V8-APP qualified; if the pin moved since VD-5.7, they are run again. The Focus-signal entry opens live. A live session edits the two views:

- the consumer protocol of G-WRITE: N = 20 edits, 0 full reload, map camera, selection and open PDF overlay intact `[FAIT: DOSSIER:575]`;
- the series of section 2.13 through immo's edge, N = 200 per edit kind, on the edit set — the parts of `SignauxMapView` that VD-5.8 leaves inside the live size profile, and `SignalFocusPanel`. Claimed, each under 10 ms: every processing interval inside its criterion, immo's edge included; the server path inside the criterion of the class of the edited file — for a whole edit of class M, p50 ≤ 5 ms and p95 ≤ 9 ms; the sum of the claimed intervals ≤ 9 ms at p50, and at p95 inside the ceiling of the owner's answer to decision D13 — 18.5 ms through immo's edge if the owner keeps the proposed ceiling, 9 ms under D13-D; the gate is not pronounced before that answer, which is one of its edges (section 5.10). Outside the claim: one transit against the network criterion, and the display against its own number. The replacement of a real view is the whole of `p0 → p2` — the runtime's work, the registration of its children, the functions of the view and the hooks that the replacement runs — held to the criterion of the fixtures, ≤ 2 ms at p50 and ≤ 5 ms at p95. The views' own render — the render intervals of every edit — is reported per view, for information. A view that overruns fails this item: it is written with `p0 → p2`, its three parts and its render, goes to decision D12, and is not excused as render. Every series closes; 0 cold edit, 0 fallback of the delivery. No file above the profile is in the edit set: a file that the check of VD-5.5 lists is decomposed first, or waits for the owner's answer to decision D13 — it is not measured against a wider figure, as revision 5 allowed. Revision 4 asked for a visible DOM at p95 ≤ 500 ms;
- a save: `artifacts_git_commit` creates a commit whose tree is the `liveTreeSha` of the session; the page receives no revision; the publication of that commit lists the source digests of the last live revision.

The edited files are inside the writable ceiling, and a write outside it is refused. No builder job runs on the pods during the run.

**Gate G5b.** The whole candidate builds in V8. The existing suites pass on the V8-built output: 35 `e2e-qa` specs and 7 `e2e-real` specs `[FAIT: PLAN:147]`. Load comparison as in G-V8-APP. Same no-builder-job witness.

G5a and G5b qualify the build path. They do not replace M-IMMO, which accepts the port, nor I11, the production deployment on owner GO.

Owner of the three geo lots: the session **geo-cond**, on repository `rhanka/geo` (lane brief, section GEO OWNERSHIP). The UI stack read on `geo:` is in section 3.7. VD-6.3 and K-6 are not geo lots: geo has no host, so the chain that lets its artifact open live belongs to the host that carries it, the sentropic MCP host, and to the k8s session.

**What VD-6.3 delivers** `[JUGEMENT]`, on the facts of sections 2.11 and 3.4.

1. *Registration.* The application `geo` in the sentropic host, with its own storage scope, and one entry of the git registry for `rhanka/geo`. Its writable and refused paths are proposed by geo-cond and recorded by s-conductor as host configuration; they are never a tool argument. The lots of this plan need the site sources only (`apps/site/src/**`). The tenant and workspace that carry it: `unknown`, settled between s-conductor and geo-cond, owner informed.
2. *Edge.* In the sentropic API, the part that immo's content host plays for immo: the reader gate, the live admission (owner or invitation, code rights, live authorisation record, readiness, grant at the current revision), a signed assertion per request with the new host key, the forward to `artifact-content` with an allow-list of headers, the socket registry, and the revocation push. The wire format is the one module that immo, the API and the edge already share `[FAIT: sen:apps/artifact-content/README.md:43]`. For the latency path of section 2.13, the edge forwards the pre-armed patch request of the page and the revision socket, records its marks `e0` and `e1`, and the API process — the MCP host of these artifacts — pushes each revision to `artifact-content`.
3. *Domain.* The entry of the new pages domain and its key identifier in the configuration of `artifact-content`, and the network policy, on the acts of K-6.
4. *Qualification.* Gate G6-HOST.

**Gate G6-HOST** (access chain of an artifact hosted by the sentropic MCP host). In preprod:

1. Registration: the git registry of the sentropic host holds one entry for `rhanka/geo`, with the paths recorded by s-conductor; a git tool on another repository answers `git_repo_not_registered`.
2. Domain: `artifact-content` starts with two pages domains; the key identifier of the new one is neither immo's nor the controller's.
3. Authentication: on `<artifactId>.<pages domain>`, a request without a session gets no live content, and neither does a signed-in reader without code rights; the binding owner gets the live session; an invitee gets it inside the invitation only.
4. Assertion: `artifact-content` refuses an assertion signed with immo's key for the new domain, one signed with the new key for immo's domain, and an expired one.
5. Revocation: revoking the session, then an invitation, closes the open sockets at once and the next request is refused; a controller fence does the same.
6. Rotation: the host key is rotated once by the documented procedure, with no refused request in between.
7. Browser mode: the draft push is admitted for the session principal of an artifact of this host and the preview opens on the new pages-domain host; items 1, 5, 8 and 9 of G-V8-PREVIEW pass there.
8. Nothing changes for immo: the existing live-edge suites stay green.
9. Latency (section 2.13), through this edge, on a fixture artifact of the sentropic host, with the harness in the region, on a file inside the live size profile: N = 200 edits per edit kind after 20 warm-up edits. Claimed, each under 10 ms: the server path inside its criteria in the API process, under the budget that K-2 publishes for it; the send of the host and the content host inside theirs; the edge, between its marks `e0` and `e1`, ≤ 1 ms at p50 and ≤ 3 ms at p95; the replacement in the page — the whole of `p0 → p2`, render included — inside its own; the sum of the claimed intervals ≤ 9 ms at p50, and at p95 inside the ceiling of the owner's answer to decision D13 — 18.5 ms through this edge if the owner keeps the proposed ceiling, 9 ms under D13-D. Outside the claim, each against its own number: one transit and the display. The component's own render is reported for information and excuses no overrun. Every series closes; 0 full reload, 0 cold edit, 0 fallback of the delivery. This item is not pronounced before the answer to decision D13, which is an edge of the gate (section 5.10).

**Gate G6.** The site builds in V8 in both modes with equal digests and is published as an artifact through the sentropic MCP host, on the registration and the access chain of VD-6.3. Clean checkout: the build runs on the snapshot of the gate commit, where neither `dist` nor `node_modules` exists. The descriptor declares each local package the site imports — `source` with its subpath table, or `mirror` with an exact version (section 2.5, rule 1) — and the receipt lists the mode of each; removing one declaration gives `workspace_unresolved`; switching one package from one mode to the other changes the recipe key. The entry `@sentropic/geo/catalog` builds from sources only if nothing it reaches imports a Node built-in, which is `unverified` (section 3.7); otherwise geo-cond narrows the import or ships the inventory as data. A live session of the geo artifact opens on its pages-domain host, takes an edit of a site source and shows it with no full reload, inside the criteria of section 2.13 for the size class of the edited file — the claimed intervals under 10 ms, the whole replacement in the page among them, their sum at p95 inside the ceiling of the owner's answer to decision D13, the transit and the display against their own numbers, the page's own render reported for information (N = 200 per edit kind, through the edge of VD-6.3). That part of the gate is not pronounced before the answer to decision D13, one of its edges (section 5.10). The edited file is inside the live size profile: the five pages of the site have 216 lines at most today (`wc -l` on `geo:apps/site/src`); the browser mode pushes a draft and opens the preview on that host. The publication is made at one commit of the artifact branch and gives one root manifest digest when repeated (section 2.12). The map page renders its layers with a real dedicated worker under `worker-src 'self'`; the worker closure of the MapLibre version geo locks (6.14.0) has passed the admission rule of section 2.6. Data: an artifact may connect to its own origin only (`connect-src 'self'` `[FAIT: sen:packages/artifacts/src/content/headers.ts:45]`), and the site reads an OGC API on another origin. In the artifact, the pages therefore render from the bundled snapshot and from a same-origin fixture. Live data inside a geo artifact would need a reader relay to the geo API on the artifact's origin, which no host provides: `not covered` (section 8.3). The V8 build is client-rendered: the prerendered HTML of the public site stays a property of its production build. By decision D1, the Pages workflow keeps building the production site `[FAIT: geo:.github/workflows/pages.yml:55-69]`.

Order. The owner places geo after immo. One geo lot is the necessary exception: the immo map lots I2a to I3b depend on I1a `[FAIT: PLAN:652-655]`, so I1a starts with step 5. The site port is not an exception: VD-6.1 starts after immo's Focus-signal gate G5a, and G6 precedes the cutover VD-7.1 (section 4.6).

### 5.10 Schedule model, dependency graph, critical path and earliest times

`[JUGEMENT]` Every time below is an earliest time under the following assumptions, all `unverified` until the conductors confirm them:

1. The origin is 13/11 at 10:00 (T+0). A node with no prerequisite is released there, and a lot whose state is `unknown` is counted as not done: it carries its full hours.
2. Receipts (section 3.6). A fact dated before the origin counts at T+0: `R-NEXT`, `R-V10a`, and the DS lots merged by PR #279 and PR #280. A fact dated after it counts at the next full hour: V9, merged with PR #281 at 11:51, at T+2.
3. What is not received has its own estimated time, each `unverified` and to be replaced by a date of the DS conductor:
   - `PR-282` at T+6: brought up to date with `main` at 12:07, its checks still running at 12:38; then the 2 h the DS plan counts for review and integration `[FAIT: PLAN:890]`, and a margin of one more CI run;
   - `PR-271`, `PR-277` and `G0-REST` — the steps of the diagram gate that have no pull request, G0.3 and G0.7 — at T+22, that is 14/11 08:00, the date the DS estimates for the closure of that gate and the day 0 that the app plan assumes `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:401; APP-SPEC:1046]`. G0 is their latest;
   - `R-DEPS` at T+8, that is 13/11 18:00: the publish run of `themes` 0.14.0 failed at 12:05 and `diagram-canvas` 0.2.0 has no tag (section 3.6), so both need a fix or a first tag, then a run — the last two runs of the core pair that succeeded took about 50 minutes each (`gh run list --workflow npm-publish.yml`);
   - `DS-ESM` at T+22: the DS gave no date for its new publish gates; the node is carried at the DS's own day 0.
4. Three `sentropic` branch slots are dedicated to this plan (decision D6), beside the four of the app migration `[FAIT: APP-SPEC:1047; sen:rules/workflow.md:55]`: two from T+0, the third from the merge of VD-2.2.
5. Slots elsewhere: h2a 1, geo 1, DS up to 10, radar-immobilier 6 `[FAIT: PLAN:863-866]`. The chat-ui lots C0 to C6a run in the four slots of the app plan with that plan's hours and prerequisites `[FAIT: APP-SPEC:954-961,1054-1055,1072-1077]`; this plan assumes that those slots do not delay them and does not recompute the app plan. That plan starts C0 on 13/11 at 08:00; no branch and no pull request of C0 exists at the read of round 4 (`git branch -r`, `gh pr list`), so C0 is released at the origin like any node without a prerequisite, 2 h later than in revision 3.
6. Builders, reviewers and conductors are available without interruption. Owner waits are counted as zero: every decision that is not taken and every owner GO is a node released at T+0, and the table "Decisions and the date each is needed" gives the date at which an unanswered decision starts to delay a lot. Decision D1, taken on 13/11 at about 14:20, is released at T+5, the next full hour; so is the lot that revision 5 adds without a prerequisite, VD-5.5. Decision D13, raised by revision 6 on 13/11 at about 15:40, is released at T+6: it cannot be answered before it is asked. Since revision 7 it is also an edge of the gates that apply its ceiling: the dates of those gates are those of an answer given before them, and a later answer moves them hour for hour.
7. The k8s session: K-2 in service at T+36, four hours before VD-2.4 starts; K-6 at T+120; and K-0, the deletion of the VM, done at T+32 — 24 h after the owner's directive, which is released at T+8, the next full hour. The three lead times are `unknown`.
8. Immo's acceptance protocol is received before I12 starts.
9. The placeholder hours (†) of the h2a, DS and k8s nodes are those of sections 5.2 to 5.8.

Rule of computation, the only one: **a node starts at the latest of its release time and of the ends of the nodes it depends on; it ends at its start plus its hours.** The order of the lots inside a branch slot is a choice; it is written as an edge, so that every date follows from the table alone.

**Dependency graph.** One table lists every node that a gate of this plan needs, with every edge. This table is the source; the lot tables of sections 5.2 to 5.8 repeat it. Revision 2 spread the graph over two tables and left out V13a and V13b, two prerequisites of V10b, and the build plan under the build port. Revision 3 wrote: "A DS lot that is already published, or in an open pull request, is a receipt: its own prerequisites are behind it." The second half of that rule erased declared prerequisites (round-3 review, finding 1) and is withdrawn. Three rules replace it:

1. **A lot keeps the prerequisites of its owning plan.** A node outside this plan carries every prerequisite that its owning plan declares for it — the DS plan for the DS, immo and geo lots, the app plan for the chat-ui lots, the h2a spec for the h2a lots — plus the edges this plan adds. The second command below checks it against the two plans that have a dependency column.
2. **Only a read effect closes a prerequisite.** A lot is received when its pull request is merged on the default branch or, for a consumer in another repository, when its package version can be installed. A received lot is a `fact` row, with its evidence in section 3.6, and its own prerequisites are closed by the reception. Nothing else closes one: not an open pull request, not a date of a plan, not a commitment.
3. **An open pull request is a node.** A lot whose work sits in an unmerged pull request keeps its declared prerequisites and gains that pull request as an edge. The pull request is a `pr` row with its own estimated time (assumption 3). When it merges, the lot becomes a `fact` row at the hour of the merge.

How to read it:

- **Node**: a lot, a receipt, an open pull request, an owner decision or GO, a wait, or a gate.
- **Depends on**: every prerequisite of the node. `@h` releases the node at T+h: the origin, a fact or a dated assumption. `s:X` says that X is the previous lot of the same branch slot — an edge of resource, not of content.
- **Hours**: elapsed hours, that is build hours plus the overhead of the repository (Conventions); 0 for a receipt, a pull request, a decision, a GO or a gate, and for a lot whose build work is entirely in an open pull request (V7a, V7b); † marks a placeholder.
- **Earliest start**, **Earliest end**: the offset h of T+h, then the Montréal date and hour.
- **Slot**: `sen-A`, `sen-B`, `sen-C` = the three sentropic slots of this plan; `app` = the sentropic slots of the app plan; `ds`; `h-cond` = the single slot of the h2a repository; `immo` (six slots); `geo` (one slot); `k8s`; `owner`; `fact` = received, with evidence; `pr` = an open pull request; `assumed` = a dated assumption that is not a pull request; `wait`; `gate`.

Notes on nodes that are not lots of a table above:

- Received, `fact` rows (evidence in section 3.6): `R-NEXT` and `R-V10a`, the publication of 13/11 09:39 and the statement of the lane brief; `V0b` and `V0c`, PR #279; `V1`, `V2`, `V3`, `V6`, `V4b` and `V4c`, PR #280; `V9`, PR #281, at T+2. The prerequisites that the DS plan gives to these lots are closed by the receptions. The other lots of those three pull requests (V0a, V4a, V5a, V5b, V8) and L1.3 are prerequisites of received lots only and have no row.
- Not received, with their own estimated time (assumption 3): `PR-282`, `PR-271` and `PR-277`, three open pull requests of the DS; `G0-REST`; `R-DEPS`; `DS-ESM`, the delivery of commitments 1 and 4 of the DS agreement (section 2.5). `G0` is a gate over `PR-271`, `PR-277` and `G0-REST`. `PROTO-IMMO`: assumption 8.
- V7a and V7b keep the four prerequisites of the DS plan and have `PR-282` as a fifth edge. V12b and F1 have `PR-271` as an edge beside their declared prerequisites, because both are built on the release that pull request delivers `[FAIT: PLAN:595,633]`. V13a has VD-2.0 as an edge: the format of the types entry (section 2.5, commitment 3).
- Revision 9 changes no node, no edge and no hour: the table is the one of revision 8, and the two commands give the same outputs on it.
- Changed by revision 8. For the owner's directive on step 1: the node K-0 is added — the deletion of the VM, released at T+8 — and is an edge of VD-7.2 beside K-7; the nodes D8, D10 and NOTICE-7D are removed, with their edges under VD-7.2, VD-7.0 and K-7, because each had the VM as its object (section 7); VD-7.2 takes 3 h more, the move of the contract module. For the round-7 review: VD-2.13 takes 1 h more. The table has 148 nodes and 332 edges, against 150 and 335.
- Edges added by revision 7, each a prerequisite that a gate text uses (round-6 review, finding 2): decision D13 under the five gates that apply the ceiling on the sum at p95 — G-LIVE-MS, G5-HOST, G6-HOST, G5a and G6 — and G-LIVE-MS under G-V8-APP, whose items 2 and 7 are that gate. The edges are on the gates and not on their lots: a lot can run its series before the owner's answer, and its verdict cannot be pronounced before it. Revision 6 had D13 under VD-5.8 alone, so that no path led from the decision to the gates that applied its ceiling. Hours changed by revision 7: VD-2.13 (+ 1). No node is added.
- Nodes added by revision 6: the lot VD-5.8 and the decision D13. Edges added by revision 6, each a prerequisite that a gate text uses: VD-5.8 under VD-5.7 and under VD-5.2; G-V8-APP under VD-5.2, whose gate holds for the compiler pin that G-V8-APP qualified; G-LIVE-MS under VD-6.2, whose gate applies the latency criteria. Hours changed by revision 6: VD-2.8 (+ 1), VD-2.15 (+ 2), VD-2.10 (+ 2). No edge is added under VD-2.11: the item of G-V8-PREVIEW that used the work of VD-2.13 and VD-2.16 left that gate for G-LIVE-MS, whose lot VD-2.10 already waits for both (round-5 review, finding 5).
- Nodes added by revision 5: the lots VD-2.15, VD-2.16, VD-5.5, VD-5.6 and VD-5.7, and the gates G-LIVE-MS and G5a-PUB. Edges changed by revision 5: VD-5.1 no longer waits for G-V8-APP and waits for VD-2.6, VD-2.14 and VD-5.5; VD-5.4 gains VD-2.16 and VD-5.6; VD-5.2 gains VD-5.7 and G-LIVE-MS; VD-2.10 gains VD-2.15 and VD-2.16; VD-6.3 follows VD-2.16, which it also needs, where it followed VD-2.14 in its slot.
- Edges added to this plan's own lots by revision 4, each a prerequisite that a gate text already used: K-2 and `DS-ESM` under VD-2.5; `R-DEPS` and `DS-ESM` under VD-2.6; VD-2.12 under VD-2.11; `R-DEPS` under VD-2.10; K-2 under VD-4.1; `DS-ESM` under VD-5.1 and VD-6.2; `R-V10a` and `R-DEPS` under VD-6.1; G5-HOST and G6-HOST under VD-7.1; G1 under L2-snapshot-h2a; `GO-H2A` under each h2a lot.
- `D1` to `D6`, `D9`, `D11` and `D13`: the owner decisions of section 7 that a lot or a gate waits for. D7, D8 and D10 have no node: the owner's directive of 13/11 leaves them without an object. D12 has none, because it exists only if a measurement misses a criterion. D14 has none: it sizes the pool of the interim path and gates no lot of the target. D13 is released at T+6 (assumption 6). D1 is taken: its row is released at T+5 and keeps the slot `owner`, because the second command reads a `fact` row as a received lot and the DS plan has a lot of its own named D1. `GO-K2`, `GO-K6`, `GO-K7`: owner GO for the k8s acts. K-0 has no GO node: the owner's directive is its GO, and its row is released at T+8. `GO-GEO-APP`: the owner's enrolment of the forge application on `rhanka/geo`. `GO-H2A` and `GO-TAG`: the owner GO that the h2a spec attaches to every merge and, separately, to every tag `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:387]`; the first is an edge of each h2a lot, the second of G3.
- `Benchmark`: the delivery benchmark of section 5.4. `C0` to `C6a`: the chat-ui lots of the app plan, which immo's chat host I8 needs. `S2`, `S4` and `L1.0`: DS lots with no prerequisite and an `unknown` state, counted as not done (assumption 1).
- `OBS-7D`: the 7 days of observation of section 4.6, 168 h. The notice node of revisions 2 to 7 is removed: its rule bore on the VM (section 4.6, precondition 4).
- G-A and the step-1 lots were never nodes: no lot of the target depended on them, which is why their stop moves no date of the target (section 4.4). VD-5.0 exists only under decision D11-B: 7 h of elapsed time before VD-5.1.

**Check.** Two comment markers delimit the table in the source of this file. The command below re-derives it: no node is listed twice; every prerequisite names a node of the table; the graph has no cycle; each start is the latest release or end among its prerequisites; each end is its start plus its hours; each date is 13/11 10:00 plus its offset; no slot of one lot at a time (`sen-A`, `sen-B`, `sen-C`, `h-cond`, `geo`) holds two lots at once; `immo`, `ds` and `app` never hold more than 6, 10 and 4 lots. Its output on this revision is the last line of the block.

```sh
awk -F'|' '
function fmt(h,  t,hh,d,mo){t=h+10;hh=(t%24+24)%24;d=13+(t-hh)/24;mo=11;if(d>30){d-=30;mo=12}return sprintf("%d · %02d/%02d %02d:00",h,d,mo,hh)}
function trim(s){gsub(/^[ \t]+|[ \t]+$/,"",s);return s}

on&&/^\|/{id=trim($2);if(id=="Node"||id~/^-+$/)next;if(id in K){print "node listed twice: "id;err++}n++;I[n]=id;K[id]=1;D[id]=trim($3);h=trim($4);sub(/†/,"",h);H[id]=h+0;S[id]=trim($5);E[id]=trim($6);G[id]=trim($7)}
END{cap["immo"]=6;cap["ds"]=10;cap["app"]=4;cap["sen-A"]=1;cap["sen-B"]=1;cap["sen-C"]=1;cap["h-cond"]=1;cap["geo"]=1
 for(i=1;i<=n;i++){m=split(D[I[i]],a,",");for(j=1;j<=m;j++){d=trim(a[j]);if(d~/^@/)continue;edges++;sub(/^s:/,"",d);if(!(d in K)){print "unknown node "d" in "I[i];err++}}}
 for(p=1;p<=n&&c<n;p++)for(i=1;i<=n;i++){id=I[i];if(id in e)continue;m=split(D[id],a,",");ok=1;s="";
  for(j=1;j<=m;j++){d=trim(a[j]);if(d~/^@/)v=substr(d,2)+0;else{sub(/^s:/,"",d);if(!(d in e)){ok=0;break}v=e[d]}if(s==""||v>s)s=v}
  if(ok&&s!=""){b[id]=s;e[id]=s+H[id];c++;if(fmt(b[id])!=S[id]){print id": start is "S[id]", computed "fmt(b[id]);err++}if(fmt(e[id])!=E[id]){print id": end is "E[id]", computed "fmt(e[id]);err++}}}
 if(c<n){print "cycle or unresolved prerequisite: "n-c" node(s)";err++}
 for(i=1;i<=n;i++){x=I[i];if(!(G[x] in cap)||H[x]==0)continue;k=0;for(j=1;j<=n;j++){y=I[j];if(G[y]==G[x]&&H[y]>0&&b[y]<=b[x]&&b[x]<e[y])k++}if(k>cap[G[x]]){print G[x]": "k" lots at T+"b[x]", capacity "cap[G[x]];err++}}
 print n" nodes, "edges" edges, "err+0" error(s)"}' spec/SPEC_EVOL_V8_ARTIFACT_DELIVERY.md
```

The check was also run on five altered copies of this revision's table — one hour figure changed, one prerequisite renamed, two lots put in one slot, one date changed, one cycle added — and reported each. A sixth copy, with decision D13 released at T+300 and nothing else changed, reports G-LIVE-MS, G-V8-APP, G5-HOST and G6-HOST at T+300, G5a at T+335 and G6 at T+363. The same alteration of revision 6 left G-LIVE-MS and G-V8-APP at T+196, G5-HOST at T+197 and G6-HOST at T+202: the command proves the dates from the edges that are present, and those edges were absent (round-6 review, finding 2). A seventh copy, with the row of K-0 deleted, reports the edge of VD-7.2 that names it.

**Check of the declared prerequisites.** The first command proves that the dates follow from the edges that are present. It cannot see an edge that is missing: the round-3 review ran it on revision 3, got 0 error, and still found erased prerequisites. The second command reads the two owning plans that have a "Depends on" column — the DS plan for the rows whose slot is `ds`, `immo` or `geo`, the app plan for the rows whose slot is `app` — and reports every prerequisite they declare that is not an edge of the same node in the table. A `fact` row is skipped: a reception closes its prerequisites (rule 2). Text after a semicolon in a plan's cell is an external condition, not an edge `[FAIT: PLAN:839]`, and `V10a` is read as `R-V10a`. The h2a spec has no such column: its lots are checked by hand against section 5 of that spec. Its output on this revision is the last two lines of the block.

```sh

function trim(s){gsub(/^[ \t*]+|[ \t*]+$/,"",s);return s}
FNR==1{f++}
f==1&&/^<!-- DAG:BEGIN -->$/{on=1;next} f==1&&/^<!-- DAG:END -->$/{on=0}

f>1&&NF==7&&$5~/^ *[0-9]+ *$/{id=trim($2);if(!(id in D))next;g=G[id];if(g=="app"?f!=3:f!=2)next;
 if(g=="fact"){closed++;next} if(g!="ds"&&g!="immo"&&g!="geo"&&g!="app")next;
 seen[id]=1;lots++;dep=$6;sub(/;.*/,"",dep);m=split(dep,a,",");
 for(j=1;j<=m;j++){p=trim(a[j]);if(p==""||p=="—")continue;if(p=="V10a")p="R-V10a";c++;if(index(D[id],","p",")==0){print id": declared prerequisite "p" is not an edge";err++}}}
END{for(i=1;i<=n;i++){id=I[i];g=G[id];if((g=="ds"||g=="immo"||g=="geo"||g=="app")&&!(id in seen))u=u" "id}
 print c" declared prerequisites of "lots" lots checked, "closed+0" received lots skipped, "err+0" missing";print "no row in the DS plan or the app plan:"u}' \
 spec/SPEC_EVOL_V8_ARTIFACT_DELIVERY.md \
```

**Critical path.** `VD-2.1 (24) → VD-2.2 (40) → VD-2.12 (18) → VD-2.7 (36)`, to T+118; then two chains that both end at T+174 — `VD-2.14 (14) → VD-2.15 (22) → VD-2.16 (20)`, which share one slot, and `VD-2.9 (24) → VD-2.13 (32)`; then `VD-2.10 (22)` = 196 h after the origin, that is G-V8-APP and G-LIVE-MS on 21/11 at 14:00. The second chain had 1 h of slack in revision 7; the hour that revision 8 adds to VD-2.13 takes it. The consumer dates leave the path at T+174: `VD-5.4 (23)`, to G5-HOST at T+197; `VD-5.2 (9)`, to G5a at T+206; `VD-6.1 (16) → VD-6.2 (12)`, to G6 at T+234; `VD-7.1 (14)`, to T+248. Then the 7 days of observation, to T+416. Then `K-7 (3†) → VD-7.2 (19)`, to T+438, on 01/12 at 16:00. The qualification itself is not on the path of the cutover: G-V8-APP ends 1 h before VD-5.2 starts and 26 h before VD-6.2 starts.

Each figure below was obtained by changing one node of the table and applying the same rule.
