  - *The owner's order kept.* Slot A: VD-2.0, VD-2.3, VD-2.4, VD-2.5, VD-2.8, L2-snapshot-service, VD-3.1, VD-4.3, VD-2.14, VD-2.15, VD-2.16, VD-6.3. Slot B: VD-2.1, VD-2.2, VD-2.12, VD-2.7, VD-2.9, VD-2.13, VD-2.6, VD-2.11, VD-2.10, then step 7. G-V8-APP comes at T+242, on 23/11 at 12:00, 46 h later, and the consumer dates behind it move by 46 h: G5a 23/11 22:00, G6 25/11 02:00, cutover 25/11 16:00, deletion of the pool 02/12 19:00. G3 comes on 20/11 at 15:00 and G4 on 21/11 at 04:00. The browser binding comes last in its slot: G-V8-a moves to T+198.
  - *The V8 chain first.* Slot A: VD-2.0, VD-2.3, VD-2.4, VD-2.5, VD-2.8, VD-2.6, VD-2.14, VD-2.15, VD-2.16, VD-2.10, then step 7. Slot B: VD-2.1, VD-2.2, VD-2.12, VD-2.7, VD-2.9, VD-2.13, VD-2.11, VD-3.1, L2-snapshot-service, VD-4.3, VD-6.3. G-V8-APP comes at T+218, on 22/11 at 12:00, but G3 and G4 move to 25/11 00:00 and 25/11 13:00, and G5a to 25/11 02:00: the immo host lots follow the provider adoption VD-4.1, so the immo gate waits for step 4 anyway. That order is worse on every consumer date.
- **Decision D2-B** (whole application). The cutover waits for G5b (T+219) instead of G5a (T+206). It does not move at the earliest, because G6 (T+234) comes after both; 13 h of slack are lost.
- **Decision D3.** It is needed at T+132, when VD-5.1 starts, 48 h earlier than in revision 4. VD-5.1 has 18 h of slack: an answer up to T+150 moves VD-5.1 alone. Under D3-B, VD-5.1 waits for the Tailwind removal. Milestone M-W6 comes 51 h of programme time after immo's acceptance `[FAIT: PLAN:911]`, so not before T+223, on 22/11 at 17:00: G5a, G6 and the cutover move by at least 54 h and G5a-PUB by 73 h, plus the time of immo's decision.
- **Decision D11-B.** VD-5.0 (7 h elapsed) and the owner's act in immo's registry precede VD-5.1. Started before T+125, on 18/11 at 15:00, they move nothing; started up to T+143, they move VD-5.1 alone.
- **Decision D14.** It moves no date of this plan. It sets what a build waits for on the interim path: 5 to 8 minutes for a node after an idle period at min 0, nothing with one node kept. The rehearsal of the rollback, at G5-HOST and at the cutover, pays that cold start once if the pool is at zero.
- **Decision D13.** It is needed at T+72, when VD-5.8 starts. An answer up to T+152 moves that lot alone; up to T+171, VD-5.7 and G5a-PUB as well; beyond T+171, G5a comes 35 h after the answer, and G6, the cutover and the deletion follow it. Since revision 7 the answer is also an edge of the gates that apply the ceiling on the sum at p95: beyond T+196 it holds G-LIVE-MS and G-V8-APP, beyond T+197 G5-HOST and beyond T+202 G6-HOST, hour for hour — at T+300, the four gates come at T+300, G5a at T+335, G6 at T+363 and the cutover at T+377. Under D13-B the lot VD-5.8 is dropped: no date moves with an answer before T+196, and beyond T+197 G5a comes 9 h after the answer. Under D13-C the refusal of a live write on a file above the profile is a rule of the live write path: 2 h more in VD-2.16, which is on the critical path, hence 2 h on every date from the qualification on. Under D13-D no date moves by the answer itself: the gates compare the same series with 9 ms, and a miss opens decision D12.
- **The immo host lots.** VD-5.6 and VD-5.4 have no slack: each hour of VD-5.6 moves G5a-PUB, and each hour of VD-5.4 moves G5a, G6 and the cutover. VD-7.1 is on the path: each hour of its rehearsal moves the deletion by one hour. A rollback that is really used stops the 7 days of observation, which start again at the return to V8.
- **The anticipated lots.** If none of the three were anticipated — VD-5.1 after G-V8-APP, the whole host wiring in VD-5.4, the whole Focus entry in VD-5.2, as in revision 4 — G5a would come at T+234, 28 h later than here, and the cutover with it. A defect of the unqualified build that stops VD-5.1, VD-5.6 or VD-5.7 costs at most that gain.
- **Geo order.** VD-6.1 needs only I1a and L1.7. Started before G5a, it would let G6 come at T+214, and the cutover and the retirement 20 h earlier. That would change the owner's order; it is not assumed.
- **The lots of the live path.** VD-2.14, VD-2.15 and VD-2.16 share one slot and have no slack: each hour of one of them moves the qualification and every consumer date. Since revision 8, VD-2.9 and VD-2.13 have none either: each hour of one of them moves the same dates.
- **Spike verdicts.** A negative answer to question 6 (workers in the preview) or to question 10 (hot replacement on Vapor) stops VD-2.11 or VD-2.13 and goes back to the owner or to immo (sections 2.11 and 2.9). A latency criterion that the measurement of questions 13 to 16 misses opens decision D12 (section 2.13), and so does an overrun of the replacement `p0 → p2` that question 10 measures — whole, render included; a dependency between blocks that the edit matrix finds and the table lacks is added to the keys of VD-2.15 before it starts. No date behind them holds in those cases. A wrapper of the render intervals that cannot be installed moves no date and no criterion: the render is information, and the replacement is held whole in every case (section 2.13). A state witness that an edit kind does not keep at the pin (section 2.9) goes to immo with the amendment of G-WRITE, and G5a does not pass on it before immo answers.
- The stop of step 1 changed none of these times: no lot of the target depended on G-A. The deletion of the VM is on no path either.
- Not contained: owner waits; immo's two acceptance decisions; the lead times of the k8s session for K-0, K-2, K-6 and K-7; reviewer and conductor availability; the slots of the app plan for the chat-ui lots; any slip of the DS lots whose state is unknown; the recomputation of the † placeholders by h-cond and by the DS conductor.

---

## 6. Risks and pre-mortem

### 6.1 Risks

The evidence column is `[FAIT]` with its citation; the mitigation column is `[JUGEMENT]`.

| # | Risk | Evidence | Mitigation and gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | **Vue 3.6 release candidate, Vapor.** A defect of the pinned compiler or runtime reaches every artifact at once | No stable 3.6.0: `rc` = 3.6.0-rc.15, `latest` = 3.5.45 `[APP-SPEC:360]`. The Vue team recommends Vapor for partial usage and small new applications `[APP-SPEC:362]`. The owner decided Vapor on the pinned release candidate (AD7) `[PLAN:20]`. Vapor server rendering is not qualified `[H2A-SPEC:159]` | Exact pins, one pin table for the app, the DS, h2a and the compile service `[APP-SPEC:591]`. The classic entry is built by every lot `[APP-SPEC:379]`. A pin bump changes the compiler digest of the recipe, hence every recipe key: old outputs stay addressable and nothing is recompiled silently. G-V8-a and G-V8-b are re-run at every pin bump |
| R2 | **Isolate memory and CPU.** A large component exceeds the cap or the deadline; the host pod is too small | Default 128 MiB, cap 256 MiB, deadline 5 s then 30 s `[H2A-SPEC:312-313]`. The largest immo source has 3,039 lines `[PLAN:149]`; its compile cost is `unknown`. The API pod limits are 512Mi and 500m of CPU `[sen4:deploy/k8s/base/30-api.yaml:153-154]`; the deployment memory of the mesh host is an open unknown of the sentropic review `[rv-sentropic.md:588]` | VD-2.0 measures heap and time per component on sources of that size class. Budgets are published before activation (K-2, decision D5). A build plan never needs more than one isolate per tenant. Exhaustion is a typed refusal, never a silent degradation |
| R3 | **Native addon and the no-Python rule.** `isolated-vm` cannot be installed in a production image without a source build | The skills package is built on an image chosen because it "ships python3 + build-essential" `[sen:Makefile:117-119]`. The API image is alpine `[sen:api/Dockerfile:11,192]`. No production code loads the addon `[sen:api/src/services/docx-generation.ts:6]`. Whether disposal interrupts a compile in 6.1.2 is `unknown` `[rv-sentropic.md:587]` | First task of VD-2.0: load the addon from a prebuilt binary in the exact production images of both MCP hosts. If only a source build works, stop and escalate: the remaining options are a base-image change for the MCP host or decision D5-B. No work-around that brings Python into an image |
| R4 | **Browser build on a large application.** The first build or the page load is too slow | No browser-side compile exists today in the DS or in cluster-mesh (sections 3.2, 3.6). Size of the bootstrap bundle: `unknown`. The output is unbundled: about 185 application modules plus the closure | Compiler loaded lazily in the worker `[FAIT: PLAN:379]`; per-module memo and Cache Storage; limits identical to the server's. G-V8-APP items 1, 2 and 4 measure cold build, edit latency and load. If load misses its bound, the remedy is a linker pass that concatenates one unit into one module — a follow-up lot, never a bundler binary |
| R5 | **Workers.** MapLibre or pdf.js does not start, or starts degraded, in a V8-built page | Both workers come through Vite-only imports today `[immo:ui/src/lib/maps/maplibre-worker.ts:7; immo:ui/src/lib/components/maps/SignalPdfOverlay.svelte:21]`. Live serving refuses workers `[sen:apps/artifact-content/src/live.ts:43]`; the application CSP refuses `blob:` workers `[sen:packages/artifacts/src/content/headers.ts:35]`. The HTML Standard fetches a worker script in `same-origin` mode and gives a worker no import map; both libraries start a module worker directly only from the same origin, and pdf.js falls back to the main thread without an error (section 2.11). Their worker files load without import map (section 2.6). The pair "V8 linker output + live edge" in real engines: `unverified` | Preview and releases are served on a tuple origin, never in an opaque frame (section 2.11). Question 6 of VD-2.0 runs the probe in three engines before any other lot depends on it. The gates assert a real dedicated worker at a manifest URL and the absence of the fallback warning: G-V8-PREVIEW, G5a, G6. `geo-ui-vue` keeps the application-supplied URL contract `[FAIT: geo:packages/geo-ui-svelte/src/maplibre-worker.ts:36-42]` |
| R6 | **Supply chain without npm install.** A dependency has no browser-ESM form, or a range drifts | The immo closure is implicit in a lockfile of 854 entries (section 2.10). `design-system-vue` depends on `lucide-vue-next` by range and peers on `elements`, which is Svelte `[ds:packages/components-vue/package.json:66,72; ds:packages/elements/package.json:57]`. Imported types need a type bundle `[PLAN:372]`. `svelte-streamdown` and `@xyflow/svelte` have planned replacements `[APP-SPEC:578; PLAN:635]` | The mirror manifest is the only resolver; exact versions and digests only; the mirror never builds and owns the manifest, as the DS agreed. The DS committed to publish every transitive dependency as browser ESM with explicit specifiers, under a publish gate, and to refuse a publication whose dependency versions are not on npm (section 2.5): commitments, carried by the nodes `DS-ESM` and `R-DEPS` until a delivery is read — `next.1` is the case where it failed. Licence notices travel in the output manifest `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:165]`. G-Q4 covers tampering, loss and rollback. A package that cannot be admitted is a typed refusal that the author sees at compile time |
| R7 | **Parity.** Framework and build system change together, and a defect cannot be attributed | The immo port is accepted on Playwright suites and screen-by-screen comparison `[PLAN:289-297]`. Reference bundles of the Svelte build exist for the Focus-signal fixture `[DOSSIER:814]` | Two gates, never merged. Framework parity: the Vite-built Vue output against the Svelte reference (M-IMMO; h2a L0 and L1). Build parity: the V8 output against the Vite output of the same Vue sources (G-V8-APP, G5a, G5b) |
| R8 | **Two compile kits** | The DS plan gives the compile kit to the DS `[PLAN:597,1022]`; the h2a spec gives the compile service to sentropic `[H2A-SPEC:255,420]` | The single-core rule of section 2.4, agreed by the DS on 13/11 (lane brief, section DS AGREEMENT): the risk of two kits is closed at the level of the agreement. What stays open is delivery: the format of the types entry, which VD-2.0 fixes and sends to the DS, and the dates. The DS reference corpus and the type bundles come from V13a and are inputs of G-V8-b and G-V8-a; the DS's own run of that corpus in the isolate, V13b, is item 10 of G-V8-APP. Both lots are nodes of the graph, with their edges (section 5.10) |
| R9 | **Tailwind in the as-is immo port** | The port keeps Tailwind and its class lists `[PLAN:273]`; Tailwind removal is lot D7, after immo's acceptance `[PLAN:692]` | Decision D3 |
| R10 | **Whole-application artifact model.** The retirement of the pod builder waits for the last immo view | An immo artifact is the whole application today `[DOSSIER:88]`; the model is an open decision of the builder dossier `[DOSSIER:459]` | Decision D2 |
| R11 | **Branch slots in sentropic** | Four branches per wave `[sen:rules/workflow.md:55]`, all used by the app migration `[APP-SPEC:1047]` | Decision D6 |
| R12 | **The interim path becomes permanent.** Immo keeps building on the pod builder, at several minutes per publication and with the cold start of a node, because the target slips | The pool is the only build path that preprod uses; it scales from zero in 5 to 8 minutes, and a publication took 6 min 51 s on 11/11 (section 4.2); its removal needs 7 days without a job and a GO (section 4.6) | No investment in the pod path (section 4.3); the sizing of the pool is put before the owner with its cost (decision D14); measurable retirement preconditions; the dedicated slots of decision D6 |
| R13 | **Cross-engine determinism** | Path normalisation is specified as NFC `[H2A-SPEC:194]`; four engines are involved | ASCII path rule of section 2.7; one bootstrap bundle; G-V8-a |
| R14 | **Release ceilings against an unbundled closure** | 255 files, 64 MiB `[sen:packages/artifacts/src/server/build-output.ts:20-24]` | Closure shape of section 2.5; item 6 of G-V8-APP decides whether the ceiling changes |
| R15 | **Same-process failure.** An isolate fault or a deployment restarts the MCP host during a build | One API replica with strategy `Recreate` `[sen:deploy/k8s/base/30-api.yaml:74-76]`; a redeploy already cut a live install in flight `[NIGHT:150]`; an isolate is not OS-level confinement `[H2A-SPEC:236]` | A compile is short and idempotent by recipe key: a retry returns the same manifest. A build plan resumes from the per-module memo. Decision D5-B if instability is measured |
| R16 | **Identifier and contract drift between documents** | `AMD-17` designates two amendments (section 2.8). The two step-1 selectors differ in name and in values `[b0a:api/src/services/artifacts/live/sandbox-backend.ts:10-12; c1:apps/artifact-content/src/data-plane.ts:37-39]` | VD-2.1 renumbers the amendment. Section 8.4 lists the divergences |
| R17 | **Adoption belongs to each consumer** | h2a publishes releases; each consumer pins `[H2A-SPEC:11]`. Immo's two acceptance decisions are outside the schedule `[PLAN:920]` | G4, G5a and G5b are written as evidence that immo can re-run; their dates are earliest times, not commitments of immo |
| R18 | **Preview in a separate window.** The author looks at a second window; a browser may block its opening | The existing responses deny framing and the h2a spec forbids relaxing them `[H2A-SPEC:234,482]` (section 2.11) | The component opens the window on a user gesture; the revision channel refreshes it; an embedded preview stays a profile to qualify apart, not planned (section 8.6) |
| R19 | **Hot replacement on Vapor without a bundler.** The pinned runtime does not re-render a re-imported component, or loses state | The consumer requirement forbids a full reload `[DOSSIER:575]`; a bundler plugin emits the registration today; Vapor is a release candidate `[APP-SPEC:360]`. `unverified`. Read in round 8: at the pin a re-render of a Vapor component creates the components of its template again, and a re-creation under Vapor parents re-renders the parent, siblings included (section 2.9) | Question 10 of VD-2.0, first. VD-2.13 is a lot of its own: a negative verdict stops 28 h, not the chain. No silent downgrade: an amendment of G-WRITE goes to immo (section 2.9), and so does a state witness that an edit kind does not keep — named per edit kind, never counted as intact. VD-5.8 and the port lots can keep the edit set of G5a clear of the components that hold such a state |
| R20 | **Stylesheet parity.** A different cascade order, a missed `@import` or a font that does not load changes the look without any error | Three constructs outside SFC blocks in the sources to migrate (section 2.6); the order of lazily loaded styles in a bundled build depends on load time | One deterministic order; witnesses on computed styles, fonts and order against the Vite build (item 6 of G-V8-PREVIEW, G-V8-APP, G5a) |
| R21 | **Write ceiling of immo.** The candidate cannot be edited in a live session if it lies outside the registry | Writable paths: `ui/**` and three package families `[immo:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/registry.ts:15-20]`; the DS layout proposes two new roots `[PLAN:442-443]` | Decision D11, asked now; VD-5.0 under D11-B; G5a asserts an accepted write inside the ceiling and a refused write outside |
| R22 | **Geo data under the artifact policy.** The geo artifact shows its fallback data only | `connect-src 'self'` `[sen:packages/artifacts/src/content/headers.ts:45]`; the site reads an API on another origin `[geo:apps/site/src/lib/catalog.ts:4-9]`; geo has no artifacts host (section 3.7) | G6 is defined on the bundled snapshot and a same-origin fixture; the public site keeps its production build (decision D1); a reader relay for geo is `not covered` (section 8.3) |
| R23 | **Immo host wiring.** The immo MCP keeps building on the remote builder after its packages are bumped | The host admits `sandbox` only and composes the HTTP clients of the remote builder for builds and for live (section 3.7); a package bump changes none of it | VD-5.6 and VD-5.4, lots of i-cond; gate G5-HOST on the real immo MCP with the builder URL unset, before G5a; the cutover unsets that URL |
| R24 | **Live access of an artifact that the sentropic host carries.** Geo's live session and its browser preview have no host | One pages domain is registered, immo's; the live edge expects a host that signs assertions; a pages domain cannot lie under the content base domain (sections 2.11 and 3.4) | VD-6.3 and K-6; gate G6-HOST qualifies authentication, cross-domain refusal, revocation and key rotation; G6 depends on it. K-6 is requested with this spec; its lead time is `unknown` and has 54 h of slack |
| R25 | **A workspace package resolved in the wrong mode.** The artifact carries a published version that differs from the sources of its commit, or a build fails on outputs that a snapshot does not hold, and nobody decided it | Geo's local packages export `dist`, which git ignores; published versions differ from workspace versions `[immo:package-lock.json:2398-2400; geo:packages/geo-core/package.json:3]` (section 2.5) | One fixed priority, an explicit declaration, a typed refusal when a package is not declared, never a fallback; the mode is in the recipe key and in the receipt; G6 builds from a clean checkout |
| R26 | **DS prerequisites that are not received.** The diagram gate, the harness and the manifest, and three open pull requests decide G1 and, through V13a, the two binding gates | PR #271, #277 and #282 are open and in conflict; PR #281 merged on 13/11 at 11:51; the state of G0.3, G0.7, S2 to S5, L1.0, L1.2, L1.5, L1.6 and L1.7 is `unknown`; `next.1` is published and not installable (sections 3.6 and 5.2) | No open pull request and no plan date counts as a reception: each is a node with its own estimated time, every lot that is not received keeps the prerequisites of the DS plan, and a command checks it (section 5.10). The slack of each node is computed. The DS conductor is asked to date each one, to say whether PR #282 is accepted without L1.2 and L1.5, and to release V13a from the spike S5 (section 8.5) |
| R27 | **A rollback after the cutover that restores nothing.** The builder is back in service and no moved artifact can be published on it | A binding is frozen on its branch and its executor; the builder client admits `sandbox` only; the historical validator refuses a version-2 descriptor; a draft belongs to a binding generation (section 4.7) | The rollback pair of each moved artifact, recorded before it moves and kept until K-7; both port sets composed until VD-7.2; the legacy branch untouched; a runbook that switches no artifact while a draft is neither committed nor abandoned. Qualified on immo's host by item 9 of G5-HOST, then rehearsed in both directions by VD-7.1 before the 7 days of observation start |
| R28 | **Live latency criterion missed on the pod budget.** The compile of an SFC, or a stage around it, does not fit under 10 ms on a d2-4 class node under the pod limits. The cell most at risk is the p95 of a whole edit of class M: 6.5 ms for stage 3 and 9 ms for the server path, where revision 5 allowed 12 ms and 16 ms. Since revision 9 the cell most at risk in the page is the replacement itself: the whole of `p0 → p2` — Vue's hot-replacement runtime, the registration of the children, the render of the component and, on a real view, the hooks that a replacement runs — under the 2 ms and 5 ms that revisions 5 to 8 gave to the swap without the render, for which revision 8 allowed 8 ms and 16.7 ms outside the claim | No measurement exists: every figure of section 2.13 is a criterion to verify. The API pod is limited to 500m of CPU and 512Mi `[sen4:deploy/k8s/base/30-api.yaml:148-154]`; the isolate is created per call today `[sen4:packages/skills/src/sandbox/runtime.ts:372-374]`; a write calls the forge `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-service.ts:102,116]` | VD-2.0 measures on 14/11 — cold and warm, isolate and browser, under the current limits, and the replacement `p0 → p2`, whole, with its three parts and the render apart, on the three paths of the hot replacement — more than four days before the first lot of the fast path starts. Each stage has a lot gate, and G-LIVE-MS is end to end. A miss is never absorbed by a wider criterion, a larger class, a transfer to the network or a render taken out of the replacement: decision D12, with the numbers and with the options that keep the claim — more CPU, a smaller live size profile, a view restructured so that an edit renders less |
| R29 | **A warm isolate per live session.** Memory grows with the number of live sessions; the sources of a session stay in a long-lived isolate; the profile departs from the fresh context per job that the h2a spec confirmed | At most 2 warm isolates per host, 60 s idle, a fresh context per job; 128 MiB by default per isolate `[H2A-SPEC:212,312,319-322]` | A separate live profile in `AMD-ISO`, with its own limits; one isolate per session, never shared; the number of warm sessions published by K-2 and counted in the host budget; the compile profile unchanged; isolation and disposal witnessed by the gate of VD-2.15; h-cond informed (section 8.5) |
| R30 | **Anticipated immo lots on an unqualified build.** VD-5.1, VD-5.6 and VD-5.7 meet defects that the qualification would have found, and rework follows | They start 64 h, 46 h and 31 h before G-V8-APP (section 5.10) | Static lots only; a defect goes back to the sentropic lot that owns it and becomes a case of VD-2.10; G5a-PUB holds per compiler pin and VD-5.2 runs it again if the pin moved; the worst case is the loss of the 28 h that the anticipation gains |
| R31 | **A pin named by a commit is no longer admitted, or a build input is not pinned.** An old commit cannot be published again or — worse — is rebuilt on another pin, another policy revision or other host defaults, with another digest | The recipe key covers the whole mirror manifest, the compiler bundle and the policy revision `[H2A-SPEC:186,189,192]`; manifests are retained without time-based deletion, and a revoked dependency or policy loses eligibility `[H2A-SPEC:200,202]`. Revision 5 left the policy revision to the host (round-5 review, finding 1) | The build lock in the descriptor, with three pins; every field of a recipe fixed by the commit or by the binding; option defaults, limits and shell format named by the pinned policy revision; a typed refusal that names the pin, never a substitution; the authorisation checks unchanged; moving a pin is a commit of the repository's conductor; item 11 of G-V8-APP asserts the equality across a renewal of the mirror, a new policy revision and an upgrade of the host, and the refusals |
| R32 | **A draft shown before it is durable.** The page shows an edit whose durable write then fails | The tool contract requires the draft to be persisted before the acknowledgement `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/git-tool-definitions.ts:15]`; the store write is a network transaction | The acknowledgement still waits for the durable write; on a failure the session and the page return to the last durable revision and the call fails; item 2 of the gate of VD-2.16 |
| R33 | **The pre-armed patch request is not portable.** An engine or an edge does not keep a module request pending, and every patch pays a second round trip | `unverified`. Immo's edge abandons an upstream request after 10 s `[immo5:api/src/artifacts/live-edge-forward.ts:110]`; the live policy admits no inline and no `blob:` script `[sen4:apps/artifact-content/src/live.ts:46,104]` | Question 15 of VD-2.0, in three engines and through an edge; the announcement path stays as the fallback and every use of it is counted; this plan does not relax the policy: a miss goes to the owner (decision D12) |
| R34 | **A block memo that serves a stale result.** A template or a style edit changes what another block must emit, and the page gets bytes that a whole compile would not give | The blocks of an SFC depend on each other: binding metadata, used imports, `v-bind()` lists, the `scoped` state, `:slotted` `[sfc36:dist/compiler-sfc.cjs.js:1530-1532,1624-1628,3285-3290,15785-15787,16040-16043]`. Revision 5 keyed each block by its own digest and tied the scope identifier to the whole source (round-5 review, finding 2). The same table on the Vapor output of the pin: `unverified` | Every cross-block input is in the key of the result it reaches; a scope identifier derived from the path alone; whatever the table does not name recompiles the whole SFC; byte equality with a whole compile on a fixed edit matrix, in VD-2.0 (question 13) and in the gate of VD-2.15 |
| R35 | **A measurement that excuses the server or the page.** A wait or a slow step of a server is counted as network, or slow work of the hot replacement as the render of the component, and the gate passes | Revision 5 defined the wire time as `t5 − t4` less a clock offset and subtracted it from its paths (round-5 review, finding 3). Revision 6 took the swap as `p0 → p2` less a control run, a difference from which a cost common to both runs disappears (round-6 review, finding 1). Revision 7 marked the render around the whole hot-replacement call: in the counter-example of the review, 12 ms of the runtime's own work on one edit in ten passed as render (round-7 review). Revision 8 marked it around the outermost `setup` and `render` calls: the registration of the children that a render creates stayed between the marks, and in the counter-example of the review 12 ms of it on one edit in ten passed as render (round-8 review) | Every claimed figure is an interval between two marks of one process, or a sum of such intervals of one edit, never a difference with another run; in the page there is one claimed interval, the whole of `p0 → p2`, from which nothing is separated — the render is measured apart for information and has no criterion behind which an overrun could hide; a transit is compared with its own criterion, the clock uncertainty added; a series must close; a delay injected in each server and at seven points of the page — the render function itself, and `registerHMR` of a child during the render of its parent, among them — must fail the gate on the interval of that hop (gate of VD-2.13; item 5 of G-LIVE-MS) |
| R36 | **Components above the live size profile.** The largest views of immo are edited live outside the range, and a gate measures them against a wider figure | 8 of the 69 `.svelte` files of immo's UI have more than 600 lines, 15,182 of 28,627 lines; five are in the Focus-signal entry and one of the two views of G5a is the largest (`immo5:`, `fix:`; section 2.13). Revision 5 gave class L criteria of 25 ms and 60 ms (round-5 review, finding 4) | No criterion and no pass above 600 lines; VD-5.8 decomposes the edit set of G5a before it is qualified live, with 80 h of slack; the worklist of VD-5.5 names the other files; decision D13 puts the choice and its numbers before the owner |
| R37 | **A gate passes on a figure that the owner has not accepted.** The ceiling on the sum at p95 is above the range and is only proposed; a gate applies it before the answer, and the answer then changes it | Revision 6 made decision D13 an edge of VD-5.8 alone. With the decision answered at T+300, its table still passed G-LIVE-MS at T+196, G5-HOST at T+197 and G6-HOST at T+202, while option D13-D replaces their ceiling by 9 ms (round-6 review, finding 2) | Decision D13 is an edge of the five gates that apply the ceiling, and G-LIVE-MS of G-V8-APP (section 5.10); each gate text applies the ceiling of the answer and is not pronounced without it; a lot can run its series before the answer, and a changed ceiling is compared with the same series, with no new run |
| R38 | **Leftovers of step 1.** The VM stays up and billed, or is deleted while a deployed configuration still names it; the unused code drifts on `main` until step 7 | No file under `deploy/` selects the execution node, but the live ConfigMaps were not read; fourteen files under `api/src`, the data plane of `apps/artifact-content` and the agent stay on `main`; PR #1024 is an open draft (section 4.2) | K-0 has three conditions and an owner each (section 4.5); both selectors keep their defaults and nothing sets them; VD-7.2 removes the code once and keeps the contract module; PR #1024 is closed unmerged; the request to the k8s session leaves with this spec |

### 6.2 Pre-mortem

`[JUGEMENT]` It is 08/12, a week after the earliest retirement, and the builder pool still builds. The plausible reasons, each with its early signal and the action taken now:

1. **The addon did not load in the production image without a source build**, and two days went into an image change. Signal: VD-2.0, first day. Action: the addon test in the production image precedes every other spike item.
2. **The browser and the isolate produced different bytes on a few files.** Signal: the corpus run of G-V8-a. Action: the ASCII path rule, the single bootstrap bundle, and a byte-diff tool inside VD-2.2 from its first day.
3. **The DS dates assumed here did not hold**: the diagram gate closed later, PR #271 or PR #282 stayed in conflict, the harness L1.2 was not started, or the dependencies of `next.1` stayed off npm, and G1, then the two binding gates, slipped behind them. Signal: no dated statement from the DS conductor on 14/11 at 08:00; `R-DEPS` not readable on 13/11 at 18:00. Action: each of these prerequisites is a node with its own estimated time and its slack (section 5.10); the request leaves with this spec, with the release of V13a from the spike S5 (section 8.5).
4. **The immo candidate kept Tailwind and no static sheet was allowed.** Signal: decision D3 unanswered on 18/11 at 22:00, when the anticipated build profile starts. Action: D3 is asked now.
5. **The whole-application model was kept and the last immo view was ported late.** Signal: decision D2 unanswered; lots I7 or I8 slipping. Action: D2 is asked now; G5a is defined on the Focus-signal entry.
6. **The sentropic slots were not available**, and the 196 h chain ran behind the app migration. Signal: VD-2.1 not started on 13/11. Action: D6 is asked now.
7. **The map or PDF worker did not start in the preview of a real engine**, although the package files allow it: a header, a MIME type or an engine difference. Signal: question 6 of VD-2.0, on its first day. Action: the probe runs before VD-2.6 starts; the remedy is on the serving side (headers, MIME types, paths), never a `blob:` allowance and never a return to the opaque frame.
8. **The unbundled output loaded too slowly on the immo map view.** Signal: item 4 of G-V8-APP. Action: preload links from the first version; the unit-concatenation pass is kept ready as a follow-up lot.
9. **The VM stayed up after the directive**, because nobody ran the confirmation build on the pool, or because a live ConfigMap still named the execution node. Signal: K-0 not acknowledged on 14/11; no evidence of a publication on the pod builder. Action: the three conditions of section 4.5 have an owner each, and the request leaves with this spec.
10. **The lead time of the k8s session for the mirror bucket or the pod limit was not planned.** Signal: K-2 not acknowledged on 14/11. Action: the K-2 request leaves with this spec.
11. **Hot replacement did not work on Vapor components**, and the amendment was put to immo late. Signal: question 10 of VD-2.0. Action: the question is answered in the spike, five days before VD-2.13 starts.
12. **The candidate was created outside the registry's paths**, and live edits were refused at G5a. Signal: D11 unanswered when I0 starts (14/11 22:00). Action: D11 is asked now.
13. **The immo MCP still built on the remote builder at G5a**, because a bump of the packages was taken for the wiring of the host. Signal: VD-5.6 not started when VD-2.14 and VD-4.1 are merged (19/11 16:00), or VD-5.4 not started when VD-2.13 and VD-2.16 are merged (20/11 16:00). Action: the wiring is made of lots of its own, with a gate run on the real immo MCP while the builder URL is unset.
14. **The pages domain of the sentropic host was not ready**, and geo had no live session at G6. Signal: K-6 not acknowledged on 14/11, or no domain in service on 18/11. Action: the K-6 request leaves with this spec; VD-6.3 ends 20 h before the geo lot that needs its gate.
15. **The geo build was refused because a local package exports build outputs** — or, worse, passed by taking a published version that nobody chose. Signal: the first build of VD-6.2. Action: the selection rule and its refusal are qualified in VD-2.12, more than five days earlier, and G6 builds from a clean checkout.
16. **A defect of the V8 path appeared during the 7 days, the builder was put back in service, and no artifact could be published on it** — the bindings were on candidate branches with the new executor, and a legacy branch had been deleted. Signal: item 9 of G5-HOST not run, or a rollback pair missing in the record of VD-7.1. Action: the pair is recorded before each move, the legacy branch is kept until K-7, and the switch is run both ways twice — at G5-HOST and at the cutover — before the observation starts.
17. **The compile of a real immo view took 40 ms warm, not 5**, and the figure appeared at G5a. Signal: questions 13 and 14 of VD-2.0, on 14/11 at 08:00; the worklist of VD-5.5 on 14/11 at 06:00. Action: the spike measures three sizes on the real addon, under the real pod limits, on its first day; a miss inside the profile opens decision D12 on that day; a view above the profile is in no gate, VD-5.8 decomposes the one that G5a edits, and decision D13 is asked now.
18. **The live host kept calling the forge at every write**, because the fast path was treated as an optimisation after the functional lot and was cut when time ran short. Signal: VD-2.16 not started when VD-2.15 merges (19/11 20:00). Action: the fast path is a lot with a gate of its own; G-LIVE-MS cannot pass without it, and G5a waits for G-LIVE-MS.
19. **One commit gave two digests** after a renewal of the mirror, a new policy revision or an upgrade of the host, and a publication differed without anyone deciding it. Signal: item 11 of G-V8-APP; the first repeated publication of G5a-PUB. Action: the build lock is in VD-2.8, 52 h before the first publication of an immo entry starts.
20. **The anticipated immo lots waited anyway**: decision D11 or D3 was not taken, or the prerequisites that the DS plan gives to the port slipped. Signal: D11 unanswered on 14/11 at 22:00, D3 on 18/11 at 22:00, no dated statement of the DS conductor on 14/11 at 08:00. Action: both decisions are asked now; VD-5.5 starts without them.
21. **The latency gate passed and live edits were slow**: a wait of the server had been counted as network, a slow hand-over in the page or the hot-replacement runtime's own work had been counted as the render of the component, or a template edit had served the CSS of the previous scope identifier. Signal: a series that does not close; the delay witness of G-LIVE-MS not run, in a server or in the page; the edit matrix of VD-2.0 not run on the Vapor output. Action: claimed figures are intervals of one process and never a difference with a control run; in the page the claimed interval is the whole of `p0 → p2`, render included, so that nothing can be counted as render; the witness — `registerHMR` of a child slowed during the render of its parent among its seven points — is an item of the gate; and the matrix is run in the spike, four days before VD-2.15 starts.
22. **The latency gates were pronounced on the proposed ceiling, then the owner asked for the sum under 10 ms at p95**, and the qualifications had to be withdrawn. Signal: decision D13 unanswered on 21/11 at 14:00, when the series of G-LIVE-MS end. Action: the answer is an edge of every gate that applies the ceiling; no verdict is given on a proposed figure, and the series are kept, so that the ceiling of the answer is applied without a new run.
23. **Immo waited eight minutes for a node at the first build of each day**, and the transition was felt as a step back. Signal: decision D14 unanswered when the confirmation build of K-0 runs. Action: D14 is asked now, with the owner's own decision of 07/11 and its cost.
24. **The replacement missed its criterion** — on the fixtures, because the render of a 600-line component and of its children did not fit in 5 ms at p95 beside the runtime's work; or on the real Focus views, because an edit re-created the map and ran its mounted hook, or a script edit lost the state of a sibling. Signal: question 10 of VD-2.0 on 14/11 at 08:00, which gives `p0 → p2` whole, its three parts, the render apart and the state that each edit kind keeps. Action: an overrun opens decision D12 on that day, with those numbers, five days before VD-2.13 starts; nothing is excused as render; VD-5.8 and the port lots keep the edit set of G5a made of small components, clear of those that hold a map or such a state; what the pinned runtime cannot keep goes to immo as an amendment, named per edit kind.

## 7. Owner decisions left

**Not reopened here** `[FAIT]`: the direction of the directive (the delivery chain, Vue Vapor first, V8 in both modes); the four consequences of the owner's directive of 13/11 ~17:55 on step 1 and the VM (section 4.1); Vapor on the pinned release candidate (AD7 `[PLAN:20]`); the release-only boundary of h2a `[H2A-SPEC:11]`; k8s as operator and BHS as residency `[DOSSIER:299; NIGHT:164]`; decision D1, taken on 13/11, with its two requirements — committed code, live updates in a few milliseconds — and its instruction to anticipate immo's migration.

Each decision below has options, one recommendation, and one line of consequence per option. Options and recommendations are `[JUGEMENT]`.

**D1 — What "everything runs in V8" covers. DECIDED by the owner on 13/11 at about 14:20** (verbatim in the header): "artefacts - statiques / et live".

- **Decided scope.** Every artifact build: h2a dossiers and visual artifacts, immo and geo artifacts, publication ("static") and live. It is option A of revision 4, whose wording is kept because it matches. The production images of the product UIs keep their own CI builds: option B of revision 4, which added them, is not retained. → The pod builder and its pool retire on the dates of section 5.10; no CI changes.
- **Requirements attached by the owner to the decision.** The build input is the committed code, for static and for live (section 2.12). A live update made by an AI takes a few milliseconds (section 2.13). The migration of immo to the language that V8 builds is anticipated (section 5.6).
- **What the answer does not decide.** Decisions D2 to D11 stayed open — D7, D8 and D10 are closed since, by the directive of 17:55 — and the requirement on latency opens two more: decision D12, if a measurement misses a criterion, and decision D13, on what the claim does not cover. Two of the open decisions are touched: decision D4, where only option A keeps a release derived from the commit; decision D3, which the anticipation needs 48 h earlier.

**D2 — The immo artifact model in the target** (decision D14 of the builder dossier, open `[FAIT: DOSSIER:459]`).

- **A (recommended).** Entry-based: an artifact is the module graph reachable from a declared entry. → On immo's side the cutover needs only the Focus-signal entry (G5a, 22/11 00:00); it follows the geo gate and ends on 23/11 at 18:00.
- B. Whole application, as today. → The cutover waits for the whole candidate (G5b, 22/11 13:00): same earliest date, 13 h of slack lost, and a later date with D3-B.

**D3 — Tailwind in the immo candidate until its removal** (the as-is port keeps it `[FAIT: PLAN:273]`; removal is lot D7 `[FAIT: PLAN:692]`).

Needed by 18/11 at 22:00, when the anticipated build profile VD-5.1 starts — 48 h earlier than in revision 4.

- **A (recommended).** A static utility stylesheet, frozen from the class inventory of the as-is port, committed, and checked by CI (VD-5.5); no new utility class in V8-built sources; deleted by lot D7. → V8 builds of immo start without waiting for W6: the build profile on 18/11 at 22:00, the publication of Focus-signal on 20/11. A live edit cannot use a utility class that the frozen sheet lacks: it writes a style block or uses a DS component.
- B. Wait for the Tailwind removal. → G5a-PUB moves by at least 73 h, and G5a, G6 and the cutover by at least 54 h, plus the time of immo's decision: milestone M-W6 comes 51 h of programme time after immo's acceptance `[FAIT: PLAN:911]`, so not before 22/11 at 17:00, and the build profile VD-5.1 waits for it (section 5.10). The anticipation of the owner's answer is lost for immo's V8 builds.
- C. Run Tailwind inside V8. → `unverified`; the toolchain rule excludes native and WebAssembly tools `[FAIT: PLAN:1030]`; adds a spike with no assured outcome.

**D4 — Authority of a browser build at publication.**

- **A (recommended).** The server re-derives every published output in the isolate, from the commit, and compares manifest digests; a browser build is authoritative for preview only. → One trust anchor; a few seconds of server compute per publication. It is the only option under which a release is derived from the committed code by the party that vouches for it, as the answer to decision D1 requires (section 2.12).
- B. Accept browser outputs signed by the user session. → No server compile at publication; a compromised browser can publish arbitrary code under a valid recipe key; and the server no longer derives the release from the commit: it would take the browser's word that the bytes come from it.

**D5 — Where the server-side isolate runs, with its memory and its CPU** (a k8s act; the API pod is limited to 512Mi and 500m of CPU today `[FAIT: sen4:deploy/k8s/base/30-api.yaml:153-154]`).

- **A (recommended).** Inside each MCP host process, with a published memory and CPU budget and raised limits; the budget states the number of warm live sessions per host (section 2.13). → Matches "dans le MCP"; no new service; no network hop on the path of a live edit; an isolate fault can restart the host. The figures are those that VD-2.0 measures on 14/11: resident memory of a warm session, CPU of a compile. The number of warm live sessions is part of this decision, because it bounds where the latency claim holds: 2 per host at first, the confirmed number of warm isolates, that is up to 2 × 256 MiB of isolate heap at the cap `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:312,319]` beside the host's own memory, against a pod limit of 512Mi today. A third concurrent live session on a host has no warm isolate: each of its edits is cold — bound p95 ≤ 500 ms, outside the few-millisecond range — and `artifacts_live_status` says so. Each session more costs that memory; K-2 publishes the number with the budget.
- B. A dedicated compile deployment per environment, called by the MCP hosts. → Protects the API from compile faults and gives the compile its own CPU; adds a service, a contract, and two network transits inside the server path of every live edit, which then need a number of their own outside the claim (section 2.13); the build no longer runs in the MCP process.

**D6 — Branch slots in sentropic.**

- **A (recommended).** Three slots dedicated to this plan, beside the four of the app migration: two from 13/11 10:00, the third from the merge of VD-2.2 (16/11 02:00) until G-V8-APP; then two until the access chain VD-6.3 is merged (21/11 20:00), then one. → The dates of section 5.10; steps 3 and 4 stay ahead of step 5 (G3 19/11 14:00, G4 20/11 05:00, G5a 22/11 00:00); review and conductor load grows by three quarters. With the two lots of the live fast path, slot B has no idle hour before 21/11 at 20:00, and its three lots after the build plan are the critical path. Since revision 8 the chain of the hot replacement, in slot A, is critical beside it.
- B. Two dedicated slots, the choice of revision 1. → With the owner's order kept, G-V8-APP moves by 46 h, to 23/11 12:00, and the consumer dates behind it move with it: G5a 23/11 22:00, deletion of the pool 02/12 19:00. With the V8 chain first, G-V8-APP comes on 22/11 at 12:00, but G3, G4 and G5a come later still (25/11 00:00, 25/11 13:00, 25/11 02:00), because the immo host lots follow the provider adoption. The two slot orders are written in section 5.10.
- C. No dedicated slot: the four slots of the app migration are shared. → Either the app migration slips by the hours its lots wait, or G-V8-APP comes after the app acceptance (22/11 17:00 `[FAIT: APP-SPEC:1182]`) and the interim pod path lasts that much longer.

**D7 — WITHDRAWN by the owner's directive of 13/11 ~17:55.** It asked how much more to invest in the VM bridge after a PASS of G-A. Step 1 is stopped and G-A is cancelled: there is no bridge to invest in. S1-B2 and the later lots of step 1 are dropped (section 4.3).

**D8 — CLOSED.** It asked whether the agent `apps/sandbox-node` stays, frozen, for the gVisor `sandbox` class of step 2, or goes with the VM. Your directive stops the gVisor lane and decommissions its server. On the instruction of the lane brief, this plan then decides what happens to the merged code: the agent, the API adapter and the data plane are removed at step 7, and the contract module is kept, because it carries the isolate profile of the V8 build (section 4.3; choice T40). That choice is reversible until VD-7.2 starts, on 30/11 at 21:00 at the earliest: if you want the agent kept in the repository for a later programme, say so before that date — VD-7.2 then leaves `apps/sandbox-node` in place and takes 3 h less. Remote CLI hosting and agent loops, if you open them, start from a new design: they are not a decision of this plan.

**D9 — The ESM mirror in Canada** (decision D12 of the builder dossier, open `[FAIT: DOSSIER:401]`).

- **A (recommended).** D12-A: an immutable, SRI-pinned mirror at OVH BHS, operated by k8s; no public CDN at run time. → Less than 1 CAD per month `[FAIT: DOSSIER:416]`; prerequisite of both V8 modes.
- B. No mirror: dependencies fetched from npm at build time. → Needs egress during a build, which contradicts the isolate without egress; not compatible with this plan.

**D10 — CLOSED.** It set the date of a notice of at least 7 days before the removal. That notice is a rule of the step-1 spec on the removal of the node `[FAIT: sen8:spec/SPEC_EVOL_SANDBOX_NODE.md:8]`, and your directive decommissions the VM without it (section 4.5). For the pool, the conditions are the 7 days of observation after the cutover and your GO (section 4.6). In revision 7 neither option of this decision moved a date.

**D11 — Where immo's Vue candidate lives, with respect to the registry.** The registry is immo's file and the settled write ceiling `[FAIT: CTX-SPEC:667; immo:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/registry.ts:15-20]`. The DS plan proposes two new roots, `ui-shared/` and `ui-vue/`, and asks immo to agree `[FAIT: PLAN:442-443,1036]`. A new writable path is your decision, made in immo's `registry.ts`; no plan and no builder adds one.

- **A (recommended).** The candidate stays inside the paths the registry already authorises: under `ui/**`, at a location immo chooses with the DS. `registry.ts` is not touched. → No migration and no new admission; a live session can edit the candidate as soon as it exists. The DS plan adapts its layout and its no-reference check to sub-roots of `ui/` (lots I0 and I14).
- B. You add the new roots to the writable paths of immo's `registry.ts`, and their build outputs to its refused paths. → Lot VD-5.0 (4 h, immo): registry change, redeployment of the immo MCP host, a graph build at the new commit, fresh admissions; sessions pinned to the old commit are invalidated `[FAIT: CTX-SPEC:667-669]`. Until it is done, the candidate can be built but not edited in a live session.

**D12 — Conditional: a live latency criterion that a measurement misses.** It exists only if VD-2.0 (14/11 08:00), a lot gate or G-LIVE-MS measures a claimed interval, the server path or the sum outside its criterion of section 2.13, or a number outside the claim above its own. It then reaches you with the measured p50 and p95 of each interval, in the production image, under the current pod limits and under the budget of K-2, and with the cost of each option in figures. No lot widens a criterion while it is open, and none moves a quantity out of the claim. Since revision 9 one of those intervals is the replacement in the page, held whole — from the patch received to the DOM mutated, Vue's hot-replacement runtime and the render of the component included — at 2 ms at p50 and 5 ms at p95. An overrun of it opens this decision with `p0 → p2`, its three parts and the render measured apart; the measured render explains the overrun and does not excuse it. That work runs in the browser: options A and B do not reach it, and the answers are C or D.

- **A (recommended as the first answer, to be confirmed on the numbers).** Give the MCP hosts the CPU and the memory that the measurement asks for: a k8s act, with the new requests and limits and the node capacity they take. → The claim stands; the cost is a larger pod on the existing nodes, or a node.
- B. A compile deployment of its own, with reserved CPU (decision D5-B). → The claim stands for the compile stage; one more service, and two transits inside the server path.
- C. A smaller live size profile: the claim is kept, for components of fewer lines, and the files above the new ceiling join the decomposition worklist of decision D13, with their count and their hours. → The claim stands where it applies; the cost is decomposition work in the consumer's sources. The same answer holds for an overrun of the replacement in the page, whatever part of it is slow — the render of a component that is too large, a heavy hook, a component re-created with its map: the components are made smaller, or the view is restructured so that the edit no longer re-creates that component, with its files and its hours.
- D. A wider figure for the interval or the size class that misses, stated by you. → The plan records your figure as the criterion; it is the only option that takes something out of "a few milliseconds", and it is yours alone. For the replacement in the page it covers a figure of your own for the render of the component, apart from the rest: revision 8 had 8 ms at p50 and 16.7 ms at p95 for it, and no revision of this plan puts such a figure back by itself.
- E. For the delivery only, if the pre-armed patch request cannot be qualified: admit a script source of the page itself on live responses, so that a patch is applied from the frame. → One transit instead of three; live responses then admit code that no release admits, a change of the security policy of section 2.11 that this plan does not make by itself. Not recommended: option D on the measured cost of the announcement path comes first.

**D13 — What the few-millisecond claim does not cover.** Not conditional: these are figures of this plan, known today, that are outside "a few milliseconds" and that no measurement is needed to see. Revision 5 stated them and passed gates on them (round-5 review, finding 4); revision 6 put them before you and still let the gates apply the ceiling it proposed (round-6 review, finding 2). They are yours to arbitrate, and no gate applies them before you do. The answer is needed twice. By 16/11 at 10:00, when VD-5.8 starts; an answer up to 19/11 at 18:00 moves that lot alone. And before the first latency verdict: the five gates that apply the ceiling on the sum at p95 — G-LIVE-MS on 21/11 at 14:00, and G-V8-APP with it; G5-HOST at 15:00; G6-HOST at 20:00; G5a on 22/11 at 00:00; G6 on 23/11 at 04:00 — have this decision as an edge of the schedule (section 5.10). Without your answer none of them is pronounced; a later answer holds each of them until it comes.

`[JUGEMENT]` The reading that the gates apply (section 2.13): less than 10 ms, at p50 and at p95, for the V8 update — edit received to patched module ready — and for each processing step after it, the whole replacement in the page among them, render included; less than 10 ms at p50 for their sum. `[FAIT]` and `[JUGEMENT]` Two things are outside it:

- *The sum at p95.* The compute time of a whole update is under 10 ms at p50. At p95 this plan proposes to hold it to the sum of the p95 criteria of its parts: 15.5 ms for a 600-line component and 13 ms for a 150-line one, 3 ms more through a real edge. It is the one compute figure of a qualified component that is above 10 ms; it is a ceiling, not a claim, and the gates enforce the one that you state.
- *Components above 600 lines.* This plan expects a whole edit of a 3,000-line file at about 25 ms at p50 and 60 ms at p95 in the compile alone (`unverified`; VD-2.0 measures F-LIVE-L on 14/11). `[FAIT]` 8 of the 69 files of immo's UI are above 600 lines today, with 53 % of its lines (15,182 of 28,627); five are in the Focus-signal entry; one of the two views that G5a edits is the largest (`immo5:`, `fix:`).

- **A (recommended).** Keep the reading, with its ceiling of 15.5 ms on the sum at p95; decompose before qualifying live. No gate passes on a file above 600 lines. The edit set of G5a is decomposed by VD-5.8: 10 h in immo, an upper bound, ended on 16/11 at 23:00, 80 h before the lot that needs it. The seven other files follow the worklist of VD-5.5: about 34 h (†, 2 h per part taken out; to be costed by the DS conductor and i-cond), on no gate of this plan. Until a file is decomposed, a live edit of it is served, measured and named outside the range by `artifacts_live_status`. → The claim holds for 61 of 69 files today, and for all of them once the worklist is done; no date moves.
- B. Accept a numeric exception for components above 600 lines: a whole edit at up to 26 ms at p50 and 62.5 ms at p95 on the server path, a template edit at up to 13 ms and 32.5 ms (the figures of revision 5 for stage 3, plus stages 1, 2 and 4). → No decomposition: VD-5.8 is dropped, 10 h less; G5a measures the largest views against these figures; live updates of 8 files, more than half the lines of the UI, are not in a few milliseconds, by your decision.
- C. Refuse a live write on a file above 600 lines until it is decomposed. → No live edit outside the range, ever; the eight files cannot be edited in a live session before their decomposition (44 h in all, † for 34 of them); a rule of the live write path, 2 h more in VD-2.16 and on every date from the qualification on.
- D. Also require the sum under 10 ms at p95, in place of the ceiling of 15.5 ms. → No size class has criteria that guarantee it today: by sum, 13 ms for a whole edit of 150 lines. The gates then hold the measured p95 of the sum to 9 ms, and the measurement of 14/11 says for which sizes it is met; where it is not, decision D12 follows, and a profile of 150 lines would leave 43 of the 69 files above it. It is added to A, B or C. Not recommended before that measurement.

The answer has two parts, and the gates apply both as you state them: for the files above 600 lines, A, B or C; for the ceiling on the sum at p95, the proposed figure — which A, B and C keep as written — or option D.

| Your answer | Ceiling on the sum at p95 that the five gates apply | Files above 600 lines in a live gate |
|---|---|---|
| A | 15.5 ms for a 600-line component and 13 ms for a 150-line one; 18.5 ms and 16 ms through a real edge | None: decomposed first |
| B | As A for a component of at most 600 lines. For a file above: the sum of its exception figures, 69 ms for a whole edit, 72 ms through a real edge | Measured against the exception figures |
| C | As A | None: their live write is refused |
| D, added to A, B or C | 9 ms, through a real edge too | As the letter it is added to |
| No answer | None: the five gates, and G-V8-APP with G-LIVE-MS, are not pronounced | — |

**D14 — The k8s builder pool during the transition** (added by revision 8). `[FAIT]` Since your directive of 13/11 the pool `artifact-builders` carries every build until the V8 cutover, and it is at min 0: the first build or live session after an idle period waits 5 to 8 minutes for a node. You decided minNodes 1 on 07/11, after that measurement, for about 24 CAD per month `[TRACK:775, item 01M6V78THAPQNRNHWXVJ109B9D]`. The pool went back to 0 on 12/11, as the hot rollback of the VM `[NIGHT:172]`; the night plan also records, dated 12/11, that you judged k8s too costly and put the growth of the node pool on hold `[NIGHT:163]`. `[JUGEMENT]` This plan does not put the decision of 07/11 back in force by itself: it is yours to confirm, with its cost. Needed now: each idle period already costs the next build a cold start.

- **A (recommended).** minNodes 1 from now to the cutover (23/11 18:00 at the earliest), then 0 until the pool is deleted: your decision of 07/11, applied for the transition. → No node cold start for immo's builds and live sessions. About 24 CAD per month, the cost of the VM that is deleted: about 8 CAD to the cutover. No node is added beyond that one, and the maximum stays 2. After the cutover the pool is a rollback target only, and a rollback pays one cold start.
- B. Keep min 0, as the pool is today. → 0 CAD at rest, nodes billed only while a builder pod runs. The first build or live session after an idle period waits 5 to 8 minutes, the rehearsals of the rollback included.
- C. minNodes 1 until the pool is deleted (30/11 21:00 at the earliest). → As A, and a rollback without a cold start during the 7 days of observation; about 14 CAD in all.

Not in this decision: lot S1-0, the sealed cache of the pod path (8 h), which would shorten the publications of the interim path. It stays parked (section 4.3) and is resumed only if you ask for it.

**Acts that will need a GO later, when their gate is reached** (not decisions today): each h2a merge and tag `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:387]`; the deployment of the memory and CPU limit changes (K-2); the opening of a new public pages domain for the artifacts that the sentropic host carries (K-6), and the enrolment of the forge application on `rhanka/geo` that the registry entry of VD-6.3 needs; the deletion of the pool (K-7) — the deletion of the VM has your GO since the directive of 13/11 (K-0); the production deployment of the immo candidate (I11 `[FAIT: PLAN:665]`). Section 5.10 counts each at T+0.

**Immo's write ceiling is not among them.** The registry in `packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/registry.ts` is the source of truth: writable `ui/**`, `packages/radar-domain/src/**`, `packages/radar-scoring/src/**` and `packages/radar-sources/src/**` `[FAIT: immo:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/registry.ts:15-20]`. `[JUGEMENT]` No lot of this plan needs another writable path under decision D11-A, and the lots that revisions 3 and 5 add in immo's host and in immo's CI (VD-5.4, VD-5.5 and VD-5.6) change files through immo pull requests, never through an artifact session. If a path were needed, it would be your decision in that file — decision D11-B — and nobody else's.

Since revision 8 those marks enclose the functions of the component alone (T42). Since revision 9 no mark separates the render from the claim: the page has one claimed interval (T43) | 2.13, 5.3 |

### 8.2 Questions the spike VD-2.0 must close

1. Does `isolated-vm` 6.1.2 load from a prebuilt binary, with no compiler toolchain and no Python, in the production images of both MCP hosts — the alpine API image `[FAIT: sen:api/Dockerfile:11,192]` and the image of the immo host?
2. Does the bootstrap bundle (SFC compiler at the 3.6 pin, Sucrase, core) run as a script in the isolate? Its size, cold start, warm compile time and heap per component on the size class of the largest immo sources.
3. Does disposing the isolate interrupt a running compile at every stage `[FAIT: rv-sentropic.md:587, unknown U3]`?
4. Are outputs byte-identical between the isolate and workers of Chromium, Firefox and WebKit on the corpus? Is NFC normalisation equal in the four engines?
5. Does imported-type resolution work from a declared type bundle through a virtual file-system hook `[FAIT: PLAN:372]`? Which format does the hook need from a package: one rolled-up `.d.ts` per package, or a JSON map from specifier to `.d.ts` text? The answer is the format of the types entry that the DS committed to deliver (section 2.5, commitment 3): the lot sends it to the DS conductor, and V13a waits for it.
6. Preview probe, first task of the lot. A page served on an origin of its own, with the exact headers of application mode, loads the unbundled files of `maplibre-gl` 6.4.1 and `pdfjs-dist` 4.10.38 through an import map. In Chromium, Firefox and WebKit: does each library start a dedicated module worker at the file's own URL, with no `blob:` URL, no "fake worker" warning and no CSP violation? Does the MapLibre worker fetch its shared module by relative URL? Does a worker ignore the import map of its document? Does the same page fail as expected inside a frame without `allow-same-origin` — the control revision 1 lacked? Same check on the MapLibre version geo locks (6.14.0). The standard already answers two of these on paper — a worker script is fetched in `same-origin` mode, and a worker has no import map (section 2.11); the probe measures the three engines and the two libraries.
7. Do import maps with integrity metadata, or preload links with `integrity`, work in the three engines under the application CSP with an inline import map admitted by hash?
8. How many files and bytes does the closure of a DS-based view have, against the 255-file and 64 MiB ceilings?
9. Do a stylesheet imported from JavaScript, a package `@import` rewritten to a same-origin URL and a relative font `url()` in a closure stylesheet give the computed styles and the loaded font of the Vite build, in the three engines?
10. Does the hot-replacement runtime of the pinned Vue development build re-render and re-create a Vapor component from a module re-imported at a new URL, without a bundler, while a sibling component keeps its state? Visible-DOM time of one edit on a component of the size class of the Focus views. Added by revision 7 and rewritten by revisions 8 and 9, on the same runs. *The criterion, since revision 9:* on F-LIVE-S and F-LIVE-M, in the three engines, for each edit kind and on the three paths of the pinned runtime — a re-render, a re-creation under Vapor parents, a re-creation under a classic parent — p50 and p95 of the replacement `p0 → p2`, whole: the work of the hot-replacement runtime, the registration of the children that a render creates and the render of the component included. One verdict per series against ≤ 2 ms at p50 and ≤ 5 ms at p95 (section 2.13); an overrun opens decision D12 on the day of the verdict, with the numbers. *For information, on the same edits:* the marks of the page at the pin — `r0` at the call of `rerender` or `reload`, a pair `f0` and `f1` around each outermost call of a `setup` or a `render` function of a registered component, `r1` after the flush; the three parts of the replacement and the render intervals; and how much of a render interval is work of the hot replacement — `registerHMR`, which `createComponent` calls before the `setup` of each child. Does the wrapper that takes `f0` and `f1` install in the three engines and in both outputs, and is the wrapped function the one that the runtime calls after `rerender` and after `reload`? Where it cannot be installed the render is not reported, and the criterion is unchanged. *The witness:* does an accessor in place of the `__hmrId` property of a child definition delay `registerHMR` of that child while the render function of its parent runs, in the three engines and with the pinned runtime as published — or must the witness use a copy of the runtime file? In both cases, does that delay fail the criterion of the replacement? And which state each edit kind keeps (section 2.9): the edited component's own on a re-render, that of its children, that of its siblings on a re-creation under a Vapor parent.
11. Does the browser build of the SFC compiler at the 3.6 pin contain the CSS parser and accept caller passes, as the 3.5.35 build does?
12. Do Sucrase and the linker handle the syntax of immo's workspace sources: type-only imports, `.js` specifiers that name `.ts` files, and a JSON import written with the `assert` attribute `[FAIT: immo:packages/radar-sources/src/municipalities.ts:14-15]`? Same question on the four entries that geo's site imports from its local packages, compiled from their sources (section 2.5, rule 1).

Latency questions, added by revision 5 (section 2.13). Each gives numbers and one verdict per criterion of classes S and M; a miss opens decision D12 on the day of the verdict. The numbers of F-LIVE-L get no verdict: they go to decision D13. Nor does what the spike measures of the sum at p95: it is reported for decision D13, whose answer sets the ceiling. Since revision 9 question 10 gives a verdict of the same kind, on the replacement in the page, whole.

13. Compile in the isolate, in the production image of the MCP host. Fixtures F-LIVE-S, -M and -L; style, template and whole edits, and the crossing edits of section 2.7. Added by revision 6, on the same runs and in the Vapor output of the pin: for every edit of the matrix, are the bytes of a block-wise compile — each block keyed by the inputs of the dependency table, the scope identifier derived from the path — equal to those of a compile of the whole SFC? Which result changes when the template alone, a `v-bind()` list, a `scoped` attribute, a `:slotted` selector or the use of an imported binding changes? The answer confirms or corrects the table before VD-2.15 starts. Three states: cold — a new isolate, the bootstrap bundle loaded, then one edit; a fresh context of a warm isolate; a context kept across the edits. p50 and p95 over 200 edits each, under the current pod limits (500m of CPU, 512Mi) and with no CPU limit. Resident memory of one warm session and of two, after the compile of a graph of application size. Does a startup snapshot of the bundle, if the addon offers one, shorten the cold edit? The node flavor is recorded; if the API does not run on a d2-4 node, the same run is made once on one, with the k8s session.
14. Compile in a Web Worker of Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, same fixtures and edit kinds: cold — worker created, bundle loaded — and warm.
15. The pre-armed patch request: an `import()` whose response is held and then answered with a module. Does each of the three engines evaluate it when it arrives, with no limit of its own on the wait? Does it pass an edge that abandons an upstream request after 10 s, when it is re-armed before? The announcement path — a frame, then a request — is measured beside it.
16. Around the compile: the transit of each hop of the delivery — host to content host, content host to edge, edge to the page — from a harness in the region, with the round trip and the clock uncertainty that the ping gives, and the same from a workstation in Montréal; the cost of one request on the MCP endpoint, where a server is built per request `[FAIT: sen4:packages/cluster-mesh/src/compose/mcp.ts:208-236]`; the round trip of one draft write to the store. Added by revision 8: the resolution of the clock on which a page takes its marks, in each engine of the harness, and how the harness obtains the finest one — that resolution is added to every interval of the page (section 2.13). Added by revision 9: the flavor of the machine that runs the harness browsers, recorded with every series — the replacement in the page runs on it.

### 8.4 Divergences between source documents

`[FAIT]`

- **`AMD-17`** names the isolate-profile amendment in the h2a spec `[H2A-SPEC:206,285]` and the memory-floor amendment on `main` `[sen:spec/SPEC_EVOL_SANDBOX_NODE.md:18]`.
- **Compile kit ownership:** the DS in the DS plan `[PLAN:1022]`, sentropic in the h2a spec `[H2A-SPEC:255]`. Settled on 13/11 by the DS agreement: one core, in cluster-mesh (section 2.4).
- **The DS plan and the DS's own merges.** The plan gives V1 to V8 the harness L1.2 and the manifest L1.5 as prerequisites, and V9 the gate G0 `[PLAN:578-591]`; PR #280 and PR #281 merged those lots without them (section 3.6). This plan takes a merge as a receipt and keeps the declared prerequisites of the lots that are not merged.
- **"V10a day 0" and installability.** The lane brief states the publication of `0.41.0-next.1` as V10a day 0 (section DS FACT) and, two hours later, that this version went out before two of its dependencies were on npm (section DS AGREEMENT, commitment 4). Both are carried: `R-V10a` on the first statement, `R-DEPS` on the second.
- **Prerequisites of the chat-ui audit C0.** The DS plan gives it L1.0 and G0 `[PLAN:735]`; the app plan, which owns the chat-ui lots, gives it none and starts it on 13/11 at 08:00 `[APP-SPEC:954,1054]`. This plan follows the owning plan for the prerequisites and releases the lot at the origin, no start being readable (section 5.10).
- **`apps/focus`:** ported to Vue by lot F5 of the DS plan `[PLAN:637]`, outside the migration in the h2a spec `[H2A-SPEC:84]`. This plan follows the h2a spec.
- **The app plan states that the compile service "does not build the application"** `[APP-SPEC:590]`. That stays true: decision D1, taken on 13/11, leaves the production builds of the product UIs to their own CI.
- **The builder dossier places the DS compile need in a `workerd` isolate under gVisor** `[DOSSIER:445]`; the owner decision of 22:03 and the h2a spec place it in an in-process `isolated-vm` isolate `[NIGHT:175; H2A-SPEC:206]`. This plan follows the later decision.
- **Step-1 selector names:** `ARTIFACT_SANDBOX_BACKEND` with value `kubernetes` on the API side, `APP_ARTIFACT_SANDBOX_BACKEND` with value `k8s` on the content side (section 4.2).
- **Owner precision of 08:55 and owner directive of 17:55.** The precision let step 1 continue to gate G-A and made the VM a bridge on a PASS; the directive stops step 1, cancels G-A and decommissions the VM. This plan follows the later statement. Sections 9 to 14 keep the earlier one, as the record of their rounds.
- **The notice before the removal of the node.** The frozen step-1 spec allows it "only with >= 7 days notice plus evidence" `[sen8:spec/SPEC_EVOL_SANDBOX_NODE.md:8]`; the owner's directive decommissions the VM as soon as the pool is confirmed to serve builds. This plan follows the directive and keeps the evidence (section 4.5).
- **The pool: minNodes 1 and min 0.** The owner decided minNodes 1 on 07/11 `[TRACK:775]`; the step-1 spec and the night plan state the pool at min and desired 0 since the VM `[sen8:spec/SPEC_EVOL_SANDBOX_NODE.md:7; NIGHT:172]`. With the VM gone, which of the two holds is the owner's to say: decision D14.
- **Review status of the two input specs.** The app plan's own header states revision 6 with its round-6 review not done `[APP-SPEC:3]`; the night plan records its approval at round 6 `[NIGHT:177]`; the lane brief says round 7. The h2a spec, revision 3, claims no reviewer approval in its own text `[H2A-SPEC:490,526]`; the sentropic review of that revision concludes APPROVE `[rv-sentropic.md:666]`. This plan uses both as read.
- **The DS calendar.** The app plan puts the day 0 of the DS on 14/11 at 08:00 and V10a 55 h later, on 16/11 at 15:00 `[APP-SPEC:1046]`. The lane brief states V10a day 0 on 13/11 at 09:39, while two prerequisites that the DS plan gives to V10a, lots V8 and V9, were in a pull request that merged two hours later and is not published `[PLAN:592]`. This plan follows the brief for V10a, keeps the app plan's date for the diagram gate G0, and does not recompute the app plan.
- **V13b and the corpus.** The DS plan makes V13b the run of the reference corpus "in the sentropic isolate runtime" and makes it wait for "runtime availability from sentropic" `[PLAN:598]`. Revision 2 named V13b as the source of the corpus of gates that precede that runtime. Since revision 3 the corpus and the type bundles come from V13a, and V13b is the DS's run of them (section 5.3).
- **"Live routes only".** The README of the content host says that a pages domain "serves live routes only" `[sen:apps/artifact-content/README.md:38]`; immo's content host serves the release on the same public host when the reader is not admitted live `[immo:api/src/artifacts/live-edge-forward.ts:15-21]`. Both are true, at two layers (section 2.11).
- **Layout of the immo candidate:** two new roots, `ui-shared/` and `ui-vue/`, in the DS plan `[PLAN:442-443]`; the registry that the app-context spec makes the write ceiling `[CTX-SPEC:667]` authorises `ui/**` and three package families `[immo:packages/artifacts-host/src/git-mode/registry.ts:15-20]`. Decision D11.
- **Live refresh:** the builder dossier makes a full reload a blocking failure `[DOSSIER:575]`; the h2a spec and the DS plan say nothing of live sessions. This plan follows the dossier (section 2.9).
- **Latency of a live edit.** The builder dossier gates an incremental edit at a visible DOM of p95 ≤ 250 ms or ≤ 500 ms, according to its decision D4 `[DOSSIER:575]`; the owner's answer of 13/11 asks for a few milliseconds. For the target, this plan gates the stage budget of section 2.13 and runs the consumer protocol of the dossier unchanged; the thresholds of the dossier were those of the step-1 path, which the owner stopped.
- **Warm isolates.** The h2a spec confirms warm isolates that live 60 s without a job and 10 minutes at most, with a fresh context per job `[H2A-SPEC:212,319-322]`; a live session stays alive up to 900 s between two accesses `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-store.ts:5-6]`. This plan adds a live profile and leaves the compile profile as confirmed (section 2.13).
- **Policy revision of a recipe.** The h2a spec makes `policyVersion` a host-derived value, never selected by a caller `[H2A-SPEC:192]`, and forbids a source to override the admitted policy `[H2A-SPEC:139]`. Since revision 6 the host derives it from the build lock of the commit, inside the set of revisions it admits, and refuses a revision outside that set (section 2.12): the lock selects, it does not admit.
- **One active compile per tenant.** The confirmed limits admit one active compile per tenant and two per host `[H2A-SPEC:315,317]`. The live profile counts the edits of live sessions apart from those compile jobs (section 2.13): under the single limit an edit would wait behind a build, outside any few-millisecond figure.
- **"Never HTML".** A compile returns code and assets, never HTML `[H2A-SPEC:153]`; the shell of a release is generated by `@sentropic/artifacts`, outside the compile. Its format is therefore named by the pinned policy revision, so that it is fixed by the commit like the rest (section 2.12).
- **Head of the branch at a live write.** The live service resolves the head on the forge at every write and refuses a draft whose base moved `[sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-service.ts:102; sen4:packages/artifacts/src/server/live-mutations.ts:26-28]`. On the latency path the head is checked at the start of the session, periodically and at the commit, where the existing refusal `git_head_moved` stays (section 2.13).

### 8.6 Not covered by this plan

The Preact passes; the immo datastore redesign (W6) beyond its Tailwind lot; the production builds of the product UIs (decision D1); the gVisor `sandbox` class of step 2, stopped with the gVisor lane (section 4.3); lot S1-0 and any other work on the pod path (section 4.3); a notice period before the deletion of the VM (section 4.5); Vapor server rendering and hydration, which stay unqualified `[FAIT: H2A-SPEC:159]`; a preview embedded in the product page (section 2.11); a reader relay for a geo artifact and the prerendering of the geo site in a V8 build (section 5.7); the removal of the builder clients from immo's host after K-7, which is immo's lot; the carrying of changes made on a legacy branch during a rollback to the candidate, which is their author's work (section 4.7); a host of its own for geo; any change to the app migration plan; a commit at every live edit (section 2.12); a gate on the opening time of a live session, which is reported only (section 2.9); a change of the script policy of live responses (decision D12, option E); a gate on the latency of the browser mode through a real edge — that mode is gated there on what it does, and its budget on the harness (section 2.13); a criterion on the transit to a reader outside the region of the hosts, which is that reader's link; the decomposition of the seven files above the live size profile that no gate of this plan edits (decision D13); a criterion of its own for the render of the component, outside the claimed replacement — the render is reported for information, and only the owner can state such a figure (decision D12, option D).
