Review answered: `.tmp/rv/v8-plan-astra.v1.md` (Astra max, on revision 1): REWORK, 1 BLOCKER, 10 MAJOR, 1 MINOR. One line per finding. This section is the record of round 2 and is kept as written: "l." gives a line of revision 2 (`.tmp/rv/SPEC_EVOL_V8_ARTIFACT_DELIVERY.v2.md`), not of this file, and the dates it quotes are those of revision 2. The round-2 review confirmed eleven of these answers and judged finding 8 not fixed; it is answered again in section 10, with the two statements of this table that revision 3 corrects.

| # | Finding of the review | Status | Where in this spec | Evidence used; what stays open |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | BLOCKER — the preview frame with an opaque origin cannot start same-origin workers | FIXED; the browser build-and-preview mode is kept | Section 2.11 (l. 352: origin l. 368, transport l. 370, worker evidence l. 377); security bullet l. 302; lot VD-2.11 (l. 660); gate G-V8-PREVIEW (l. 683); spike question 6 (l. 1089) | Library code read in the versions immo locks: MapLibre 6.4.1 and pdf.js 4.10.38 start a module worker directly only from the origin of the page, and pdf.js otherwise falls back to the main thread without an error. A live session already has one origin per artifact `[sen:apps/artifact-content/src/live.ts:24]`. No header is relaxed. Open: the measurement in three real engines is `unverified` — this lane had no browser — and is the first task of VD-2.0 |
| 2 | MAJOR — the resolver excludes immo's private workspaces and Vite aliases | FIXED | Section 2.5, rules 1 to 6 (l. 230); lot VD-2.12 (l. 661); VD-5.1 (l. 748); gate G5a (l. 752) | 39, 5 and 3 import statements of `@radar/*` in non-test UI files; the alias that installs the reader transport `[immo:ui/vite.config.ts:83-89]`. Workspace map and alias table are recipe options, hence part of the key |

| 4 | MAJOR — a worker does not inherit the import map of its document | FIXED | Section 2.6, Workers, two cases (l. 263); VD-2.4 (l. 653); VD-2.12 (l. 661); items 2 to 4 of G-V8-PREVIEW (l. 683) | MapLibre's worker has one relative import and pdf.js's none (package files read): third-party workers are admitted as complete closures of relative imports. The gate separates four cases |

Other corrections made while verifying, outside the twelve findings:

Not done, with the reason:

- No measurement in a real browser. The planning lane writes this spec only, and had no browser at hand. Section 2.11 therefore rests on the libraries' code and on the existing serving regime, and leaves the real-engine result `unverified` until VD-2.0.
- The two web-platform rules used (a worker script must be same-origin with its document; a worker does not use its document's import map) were not re-read in the HTML standard during this round. They are tagged `[JUGEMENT]` and are witnessed by question 6 of the spike.

## 13. Round 6 — findings

Review answered: `.tmp/rv/v8-plan-astra.v5.md` (Astra max, on revision 5): REWORK — 0 BLOCKER, 5 MAJOR, 2 MINOR. It confirms that decision D1 is recorded, that the model of a base commit, a draft and an explicit commit matches the code, that the anticipation of immo's static lots is framed, and that the rollback provisions, the two V8 modes, the workers and the registry are still present. One line per finding. The file was edited in place; sections 9 to 12 are kept as written. This section is the record of round 6 and is kept as written too: "l." gives a line of revision 6 (`.tmp/rv/SPEC_EVOL_V8_ARTIFACT_DELIVERY.v6.md`), not of this file, and the hours, dates, counts of edges and latency figures it quotes are those of revision 6. The round-6 review (`.tmp/rv/v8-plan-astra.v6.md`) confirmed the seven findings below as fixed and found 2 MAJOR and 1 MINOR defects in the result, answered in section 14. Two statements below are corrected by revision 7. "Nothing is taken out of a claimed figure" (section 13.1, finding 3; section 13.2, second rule): the swap was still `p0 → p2` less a control run. "DONE" for the numbered owner decision (section 13.2, third rule): decision D13 was raised with its numbers, and the gates that apply its ceiling did not wait for its answer. Nothing was measured in this round: every latency figure is a criterion to verify, and what was read is listed in section 13.5.

### 13.1 The seven findings

| # | Finding of the review | Status | Where in this spec | Evidence used; what stays open |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MAJOR — "same commit, same digest" is still contradicted by the policy of the host: a new policy revision changes the recipe keys of an unchanged commit, hence its root manifest digest; naming the event in the receipt does not keep the invariant; the gate covers a renewal of the mirror, not one of the policy | FIXED | Rule 4 of section 2.12, l. 438 to l. 455: a lock of three pins (l. 438); every field of a recipe fixed by the commit or by the binding (l. 439 to l. 449); nothing outside the pins shapes a byte (l. 451); the lock selects and the host still authorises (l. 452); result (l. 453). Determinism: l. 298. Lot VD-2.8, + 1 h: l. 883. Reproducibility gate — item 11 of G-V8-APP — with the policy scenario and its refusal: l. 940. Gap analysis: l. 576, l. 582, l. 597. Risk R31: l. 1524; pre-mortem 19: l. 1553; choice T27: l. 1688; divergences: l. 1799, l. 1801 | The fields of a recipe were read again in the h2a spec `[H2A-SPEC:183-192]`: the policy revision, the lane and the access partition are host-derived, and the option defaults are part of `options`. The lock now pins the policy revision; option defaults, plan limits and shell format are named by it; a pin that is no longer admitted is a typed refusal that names it, never a build under the newer one; the authorisation checks of today run at every build `[H2A-SPEC:137,139,208]`. The gate adds five scenarios: a newer policy revision made the default (same digest), the pinned one withdrawn (refusal), a restart and an upgrade of the host (same digest), the unchanged authorisation refusals, a second binding (other keys, same bytes). Open: what the scope digest of a build carries (l. 454), fixed by VD-2.8 with h-cond; the name of the refusal code; h-cond's agreement (l. 1810) |
| 2 | MAJOR — the block memo brings back a wrong invalidation: a template edit must recompile the template alone, yet the scope identifier follows the digest of the whole source, so the CSS selectors change too; a `v-bind()` of a style changes the injected JavaScript; the byte equality and the counters of VD-2.15 cannot all hold. A regression of finding R1-5 | FIXED | Section 2.7, item 3, l. 277 to l. 294: the dependencies between blocks, read at the pin (l. 281 to l. 285); a scope identifier derived from the path alone (l. 290); the keys (l. 291); what each edit recompiles, crossing edits included (l. 292); fail closed (l. 293); byte equality on an edit matrix (l. 294). Determinism: l. 297; live profile: l. 340. Counters and budgets: edit kinds defined by what is recompiled (l. 492); the whole kind for a crossing edit (l. 510, l. 511); gate of VD-2.15, + 2 h (l. 890); series of crossing edits (l. 542; item 7 of G-LIVE-MS, l. 950). Spike question 13: l. 1718. Risk R34: l. 1527; choice T30: l. 1691 | Read in `@vue/compiler-sfc` 3.6.0-rc.15, the pin (prefix `sfc36:`, l. 45). The template result takes the binding metadata, the scope identifier and the `:slotted` flag; the script takes the imported bindings that the template uses (`hmrShouldReload`), the `v-bind()` list and, in Vapor, the `<style module>` names; the identifier is an option of the caller; the browser build refuses `<style module>`. The DS build already derives the identifier from the path `[ds4:scripts/vue-sfc-build.mjs:35]`. Every cross-block input is now in the key of the result it reaches, and whatever the table does not name recompiles the whole SFC. Open: the table is `unverified` on the Vapor output of the pin until the edit matrix runs in VD-2.0; the 3.6 package was read in a temporary directory, with the same constructs in 3.5.35 at a stable path |
| 3 | MAJOR — the definition of the wire time neutralises part of the measurement: `t5 − t4` less the clock offset also holds the internal routing, the processing and the waits; taking it out of the measured paths can take out the very slowness that the gate must detect | FIXED | Definitions: l. 58. Marks per process: l. 483 to l. 490. Stage table, processing and transit apart: l. 494 to l. 504. Measurement: intervals of one process (l. 537); transit and clock uncertainty (l. 538); a wait is processing (l. 539); closure (l. 540); delay witness (l. 541). Gates: items 1, 2, 4 and 5 of G-LIVE-MS (l. 944 to l. 948); VD-2.13 (l. 889); VD-2.16 (l. 891); VD-2.10, + 2 h (l. 886); item 10 of G5-HOST (l. 1049); G5a (l. 1056); item 9 of G6-HOST (l. 1094). Marks of the two edges: l. 1036, l. 1080. Spike question 16: l. 1721. Risk R35: l. 1528; pre-mortem 21: l. 1555; choice T35: l. 1696 | No path has a wire time taken out of it any more. A claimed figure is an interval between two marks of one process — host, content host, edge, page — so no clock offset enters it. The transit is what remains between a send mark and a receive mark; it has a criterion of its own, ≤ 2 ms at p50 and ≤ 5 ms at p95 in the gates, and is compared with it with the clock uncertainty added. A series above 1 ms of uncertainty, or whose intervals do not add up to `p2 − h0`, is void. | Rule | Status | Where in this spec | What stays open |
|---|---|---|---|
| No class or gate may pass while it exceeds "a few milliseconds" on the stages that the plan claims in that range | DONE | "A few milliseconds" is less than 10 ms: l. 58, l. 466. What is claimed: l. 468. No criterion of a claimed quantity reaches 10 ms: stage table l. 494 to l. 504, classes l. 508 to l. 511 — the largest is 9 ms, the p95 of the server path for a whole edit of a 600-line component. One figure above 10 ms is enforced on compute, and it is not a claim: the ceiling on the sum of the intervals at p95 (l. 468, l. 513), which decision D13 submits to the owner. Gates that apply them: G-LIVE-MS (l. 942 to l. 957), VD-2.13 (l. 889), VD-2.15 (l. 890), VD-2.16 (l. 891), item 10 of G5-HOST (l. 1049), G5a (l. 1056), item 9 of G6-HOST (l. 1094), G6 (l. 1096). | A stage that physically cannot be in that range is named, has its own numeric target, and stays outside the claim, never inside a pass criterion that hides it | DONE | Rule 2: l. 469. The durable write and the acknowledgement: l. 519 — ≤ 5 ms and ≤ 20 ms, ≤ 8 ms and ≤ 25 ms. The cold start of a compile context: l. 521 — ≤ 500 ms. Nothing is taken out of a claimed figure: l. 537 to l. 541; item 2 of G-LIVE-MS: l. 945 | The transit to a reader outside the region is that reader's link: reported, with no criterion (l. 515) |
| If the target is not reachable on current budgets, a numbered owner decision with numbers | DONE | Decision D13, not conditional — components above 600 lines, and the sum of the intervals at p95, held to a ceiling of 15.5 ms that is above the range: l. 1636 to l. 1646. ## 14. Round 7 — findings

Review answered: `.tmp/rv/v8-plan-astra.v6.md` (Astra max, on revision 6): REWORK — 0 BLOCKER, 2 MAJOR, 1 MINOR. It confirms the seven findings of round 5 as fixed, decision D1 as decided for static and live artifacts, the model of a commit plus a draft, the framing of immo's static anticipation, and that the round-4 corrections on workers, registry, snapshots, host wiring and rollback are kept. One line per finding. The file was edited in place; sections 9 to 13 are kept as written. This section is the record of round 7 and is kept as written too: "l." gives a line of revision 7 (`.tmp/rv/SPEC_EVOL_V8_ARTIFACT_DELIVERY.v7.md`), not of this file, and the hours, dates, counts of edges and latency figures it quotes are those of revision 7. The round-7 review (`.tmp/rv/v8-plan-astra.v7.md`) confirmed findings 2 and 3 below as fixed and judged finding 1 not fixed; it is answered in section 15. Two statements below are corrected by revision 8. "The render is therefore the interval between the hand-over and the end of the flush" (section 14.1, finding 1; section 14.2, second rule): that interval also holds the work of the hot-replacement runtime itself, which is now in the swap. And "no code of the chain runs between `r0` and `r1`": the client runtime now wraps the functions of the component. The VM, the bridge and the decisions D7, D8 and D10 that this section quotes are those of before the owner's directive of 13/11 ~17:55. Nothing was measured in this round: every latency figure is a criterion to verify, and what was read is listed in section 14.5.

### 14.1 The three findings

| # | Finding of the review | Status | Where in this spec | Evidence used; what stays open |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MAJOR — the measurement of the swap can still hide a slow processing. Its criterion bears on `p0 → p2` less the control; the control runs the same replacement with an unchanged definition, so it does not measure the render of the component alone; a cost common to the real replacement and to the control disappears in the subtraction, and adding the control back for the closure does not detect it. This contradicts the rule "an interval between two marks, nothing subtracted" and reaches the gate of VD-2.13 | FIXED, by the first remedy of the review — the render marked in every edit — with the second as the fail-closed case, and the witness added | Two marks of the page, `r0` and `r1`: l. 491. Swap `p0 → r0` plus `r1 → p2`, render `r0 → r1`: l. 503, l. 504. Definition, facts at the pin, no control run, fail closed: l. 518. Never a difference with another run: l. 470, l. 59. Measurement: intervals l. 538, closure l. 541, witness of the page l. 542. Gate of VD-2.13, + 1 h: l. 892. G-LIVE-MS, items 1, 2 and 5: l. 947, l. 948, l. 951. Spike question 10: l. 1739; lot VD-2.0: l. 878. Risk R35: l. 1542; pre-mortem 21: l. 1570; choice T35: l. 1722; unknown: l. 1758 | Read in `@vue/runtime-core` and `@vue/runtime-vapor` 3.6.0-rc.15, the pin (prefix `rt36:`, l. 46). The hand-over is one call, `rerender` or `reload` of `__VUE_HMR_RUNTIME__`, the call that `@vitejs/plugin-vue` emits; a re-render runs inside it; a re-creation runs inside it under Vapor parents and is queued as a job otherwise; `nextTick` returns the promise of the pending flush. The render is therefore the interval between the hand-over and the end of the flush, in the edit itself; the swap is the rest of `p0 → p2`, a sum of two intervals of the page; nothing is subtracted and no control run is a measure. Where the marks cannot delimit the render, the whole `p0 → p2` is held to the criterion of the swap, and a miss is a case of decision D12. Witness: 20 ms injected before `r0`, then after `r1`, must fail the swap; 20 ms inside the render must fail the render alone; a diff shows that the live profile puts no code inside `render` or `setup`. Open: the marks in real engines and the cases they cannot delimit (`unverified`, question 10); every criterion until 14/11 |
| 2 | MAJOR — D13 does not block the gates that apply its proposed ceiling. D13 submits the ceilings of 15.5 ms and 18.5 ms to the owner, yet its only edge goes to VD-5.8: VD-2.10, VD-5.4 and VD-6.3 apply those ceilings without depending on the decision. Counter-test: with D13 alone moved to T+300, the graph still passes G-LIVE-MS at T+196, G5-HOST at T+197 and G6-HOST at T+202, while option D13-D could replace their ceiling by 9 ms. A new occurrence of the dependency problem fixed before the round-4 approval | FIXED | Edges — D13 under G-LIVE-MS, G6-HOST, G5-HOST, G5a and G6, and G-LIVE-MS under G-V8-APP: rows l. 1268, l. 1269, l. 1271, l. 1309, l. 1311, l. 1317; note l. 1333; assumption 6, l. 1150. Gate texts, conditional on the answer: item 1 of G-LIVE-MS l. 947 and its closing l. 960; item 10 of G5-HOST l. 1052; G5a l. 1059; item 9 of G6-HOST l. 1097; G6 l. 1099; their lots l. 889, l. 1026, l. 1027, l. 1074, l. 1075. Section 2.13: rule 1 l. 469, rule 3 l. 471, l. 514, l. 554, l. 559. Decision D13: needed twice l. 1652; what each answer makes the gates apply l. 1664 to l. 1672; no verdict of the spike on the sum at p95, l. 1743. Schedule: gates l. 1397 to l. 1402 and l. 1407; decisions l. 1418; sensitivity l. 1489; output of the check and counter-test l. 1358, l. 1361. French summary: l. 71, l. 94. Risk R37: l. 1544; pre-mortem 22: l. 1571; choice T39: l. 1726; coordination: l. 1840, l. 1841, l. 1843 | The first command gives 150 nodes, 335 edges, 0 error — six edges more than revision 6 — and the second 0 missing prerequisite (l. 1358, l. 1380). The counter-test of the review was run on both tables, with D13 at T+300 and nothing else changed: revision 6 leaves G-LIVE-MS at T+196, G5-HOST at T+197 and G6-HOST at T+202; this table gives T+300 for the three and for G-V8-APP, T+335 for G5a, T+363 for G6 and T+377 for the cutover. The edges are on the gates and not on the lots: a lot can run its series before the answer, and the ceiling of the answer — 15.5 ms, 18.5 ms through a real edge, or 9 ms under D13-D — is compared with the same series. With an answer before T+196 no date moves. Open: the owner's answer |
| 3 | MINOR — the impact of D3-B keeps a stale figure: "at least 56 h" for every gate concerned, where the recomputation gives 73 h for G5a-PUB and 54 h for G5a, G6 and the cutover, as the sensitivity already says | FIXED | Decision D3, option B: l. 1597. ### 14.2 The owner requirement, rule by rule

The requirement: « le v8 fasse des updates live au sens update par une ia en qq millisecondes ». The round-7 brief repeats the three rules of round 6. Their status in revision 6 is in section 13.2; this table gives what the two MAJOR findings changed.

| Rule | Status | Where in this spec | What stays open |
|---|---|---|---|
| No class or gate may pass while it exceeds "a few milliseconds" on the stages that the plan claims in that range | DONE | The criteria are unchanged: every claimed figure is under 10 ms, the largest being 9 ms (l. 497 to l. 512). Closed in this round: the swap is no longer a difference from which a common cost can disappear. It is `p0 → r0` plus `r1 → p2`, two intervals of the page (l. 503, l. 518); where the render cannot be marked, the whole `p0 → p2` is held to its criterion; a delay injected in the page must fail it (l. 542; gate of VD-2.13, l. 892; item 5 of G-LIVE-MS, l. 951) | Whether the criteria are met: nothing is measured before 14/11 (VD-2.0) |
| A stage that physically cannot be in that range is named, has its own numeric target, and stays outside the claim, never inside a pass criterion that hides it | DONE | The component's own render keeps its number, ≤ 8 ms and ≤ 16.7 ms on the fixtures, and is now an interval of its own, `r0 → r1`, in every edit (l. 504, l. 518): it is no longer what a subtraction leaves, and a delay placed in it must fail its own criterion and leave the swap inside its own (l. 542). The transit, the display, the acknowledgement and the cold start are unchanged (l. 502, l. 505, l. 520, l. 522) | The two marks in real engines (`unverified`; question 10, l. 1739) |
| If the target is not reachable on current budgets, a numbered owner decision with numbers | DONE | Decision D13 keeps its numbers — 15.5 ms on the sum at p95, 18.5 ms through a real edge, the files above 600 lines — and now binds the gates: none applies its ceiling before the answer, and each applies the ceiling that the answer states (l. 1652, l. 1664 to l. 1672; edges l. 1268 to l. 1317). Decision D12 is unchanged; it also takes the replacements that the marks cannot delimit, when their whole interval misses the criterion of the swap (l. 518) | The owner's answers |

## 15. Round 8 — findings

Review answered: `.tmp/rv/v8-plan-astra.v7.md` (Astra max, on revision 7): REWORK — 0 BLOCKER, 1 MAJOR, 0 MINOR. It confirms findings 2 and 3 of round 6 as fixed, decision D1 as decided, the model of a commit plus a draft, the framing of immo's anticipation, and that the corrections of round 4 are kept. This round also applies the owner's directive of 13/11 ~17:55 (verbatim: l. 13 to l. 17), recorded in section 15.2. One line per finding. The file was edited in place; sections 9 to 14 are kept as written, the head of section 14 excepted (l. 2200). This section is the record of round 8 and is kept as written too, this paragraph excepted: "l." gives a line of revision 8 (`.tmp/rv/SPEC_EVOL_V8_ARTIFACT_DELIVERY.v8.md`), not of this file, and the hours, dates, counts of edges and latency figures it quotes are those of revision 8. The round-8 review (`.tmp/rv/v8-plan-astra.v8.md`) confirmed the owner's directive as correctly applied (section 15.2) — revision 9 changes nothing of it — and judged the finding of section 15.1 not fixed, a partial correction; it is answered in section 16, with one MINOR finding. Three statements below are corrected by revision 9. "FIXED" for that finding (section 15.1): the render intervals still held work of the hot replacement, the registration of the children that a render creates. "Only isolated render intervals are outside it", and what sections 15.1 and 15.3 build on it — the render as the only time of the page outside the claim, with its own number: since revision 9 nothing of the page between `p0` and `p2` is outside the claim, and the render has no criterion. And, in the comparison with revision 4 that revision 8 left unchanged, G7 at T+435 with 10 h gained: it is T+438 and 7 h (section 15.6 gives T+438 itself). Nothing was measured in this round: every latency figure is a criterion to verify, and what was read is listed in section 15.7.

### 15.1 The finding

| # | Finding of the review | Status | Where in this spec | Evidence used; what stays open |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MAJOR — the cost of the hot replacement itself stays outside the threshold of the replacement. The marks enclose the call to `rerender` or `reload` of `__VUE_HMR_RUNTIME__` and the flush behind it. At the pin that call also updates the definitions, walks the instances and their ancestors, invalidates caches and schedules the updates: operations of the hot replacement, counted as "render of the component", outside the 5 ms at p95 of the swap. Counter-example: 1.5 ms outside the marks, 2 ms of render and 12 ms of that handling on one edit in ten pass both criteria, while the replacement takes 13.5 ms at p95. No witness covers the case; VD-2.13 and G-LIVE-MS inherit it | FIXED, by the first remedy of the review — the handling of the hot replacement and its waits are inside the swap, and only isolated render intervals are outside it — with the second remedy as the fail-closed case, and the witness added | Marks `f0` and `f1`: l. 502. Stage 6: l. 514, l. 515. Definition: what the call does at the pin, l. 531 to l. 536; render intervals, l. 538; the swap in three parts, l. 540; fail closed, l. 542; the diff witness, l. 544. Rules and words: l. 480, l. 481, l. 484, l. 66, l. 25. Measurement: l. 566, l. 570, the witness l. 571, l. 572; gates l. 579, l. 582. Lot VD-2.13, + 1 h: l. 912; VD-2.0: l. 898. G-LIVE-MS, items 1, 2 and 5: l. 967, l. 968, l. 971; item 10 of G5-HOST, l. 1072; G5a, l. 1079; item 9 of G6-HOST, l. 1117. Spike question 10: l. 1771. Schedule: row l. 1285, critical path l. 1450. Risks R28 and R35: l. 1565, l. 1572; pre-mortem 21 and 24: l. 1601, l. 1604; choices T35 and T42: l. 1751, l. 1758; unknowns: l. 1790; compile module: l. 629; French summary: l. 84 | Read again in the pinned runtime (prefix `rt36:`, l. 53). `rerender` installs the function on the record and on each instance, then enters each instance. `reload` rewrites the registered definition, copies the instances, walks their ancestors, and either re-renders the Vapor parents or deletes three caches and queues one job per parent. The re-render of a Vapor instance looks its place up, stops a scope, removes a block, renders, applies attributes, scope identifiers and CSS variables, and inserts a block. The runtime enters the code of a component through two functions of its definition, `setup` and `render`; hooks and watchers are entered apart. The client runtime therefore wraps those two functions of every registered definition and marks each outermost call: the render is the sum of those intervals, and the swap is everything else between `p0` and `p2`, the runtime's own work and its waits included. On the counter-example of the review the swap is 13.5 ms at p95 and fails; the render is 2 ms. The witness injects 20 ms at five points of the page; two of them slow the hot replacement before the component runs — inside the call before the runtime starts, through a proxy of `rerender` and `reload`, and at the entry of the wrapper before `f0`. Each must fail the swap and leave the median of the render unmoved; 20 ms inside the render function must fail the render alone. Fail closed: a hook, a watcher, the patch of the classic output and an unregistered component entered outside a wrapped call stay in the swap; where the wrapper cannot be installed the whole `p0 → p2` is held to the criterion of the swap; a miss goes to decision D12. The criteria of the swap are not widened: ≤ 2 ms and ≤ 5 ms now hold more work. Open: the wrapper in real engines and in both outputs, and whether the unchanged criteria are met with the runtime's work inside them (`unverified`; question 10, on 14/11) |

| Rule | Status | Where in this spec | What stays open |
|---|---|---|---|
| No class or gate may pass while it exceeds "a few milliseconds" on the stages that the plan claims in that range | DONE | The criteria are unchanged and all under 10 ms. Closed in this round: the swap, claimed at ≤ 2 ms and ≤ 5 ms, now holds the work of Vue's hot-replacement runtime, which revision 7 judged against the 16.7 ms of the render (l. 514, l. 540). A hot replacement slowed before the component runs fails the swap in the gate (l. 571; l. 912; item 5 of G-LIVE-MS, l. 971) | Whether the unchanged criteria are met with that work inside: nothing is measured before 14/11 |
| A stage that physically cannot be in that range is named, has its own numeric target, and stays outside the claim, never inside a pass criterion that hides it | DONE | The component's own render keeps its number, ≤ 8 ms and ≤ 16.7 ms on the fixtures. It is now only what two marks isolate around the `setup` and `render` functions of a component (l. 515, l. 538): nothing wider can hide behind it, and what the marks do not isolate stays in the claimed swap (l. 481, l. 542). A coarse clock of the page can only fail a criterion (l. 568). The transit, the display, the acknowledgement and the cold start are unchanged | The wrapper and the clock in real engines (`unverified`; questions 10 and 16, l. 1771, l. 1780) |
| If the target is not reachable on current budgets, a numbered owner decision with numbers | DONE | Decisions D12 and D13 are unchanged in their numbers. D12 now also takes a miss of the swap that comes from what a replacement runs in a view, with the answer that keeps the claim: restructure the view (l. 1669; l. 542). No lot widens a criterion or moves a quantity out of the claim | The owner's answers |

## 16. Round 9 — findings

Review answered: `.tmp/rv/v8-plan-astra.v8.md` (Astra max, on revision 8): REWORK — 0 BLOCKER, 1 MAJOR, 1 MINOR. It judges the finding of round 7 not fixed — a partial correction — and makes it its MAJOR. It confirms the rest of the amendment as coherent: the directive « Étape 1 + VM » applied, the retirement and the sizing of the pool, decision D1, reproducibility, the anticipation of immo, and the corrections of round 4. Its two checks give 148 nodes, 332 edges, zero error, and 96 declared prerequisites, zero missing; its recomputation confirms 196 h to G-V8-APP, the cutover at T+248, the deletion of the pool at T+419 and G7 at T+438. The answer applies a decision of the conductor, given in the round-9 brief and recorded in section 16.1. One line per finding. "l." gives a line of this file, as it stands in revision 9. The file was edited in place; sections 9 to 15 are kept as written, the head of section 15 excepted (l. 2288). The owner's directive of 13/11 ~17:55 is not touched: section 4, step 7 and the decisions D7, D8, D10 and D14 are those of revision 8. Nothing was measured in this round: every latency figure is a criterion to verify, and what was read is listed in section 16.6.

### 16.1 The decision of the conductor

| # | Point of the decision | Status | Where in this spec |
|---|---|---|---|

Definition: l. 529, l. 540. Rule 1: l. 480. Words: l. 66. Reading of the requirement: l. 25. Choice T43: l. 1765 |
| 2 | Render is no longer subtracted from the claim; it is measured separately, as information only, never to excuse an overrun | DONE | Rule 2: l. 481. Information row of stage 6: l. 515. The wrapper kept as an instrument: l. 542. Marks: l. 502. Series and report: l. 572; item 12 of G-LIVE-MS, l. 978. Item 2 of G-LIVE-MS: l. 968. The criterion that revision 8 gave to the render, 8 ms and 16.7 ms, withdrawn: l. 540 |
| 3 | An overrun of `p0 → p2` against its numeric target opens decision D12 with the numbers | DONE | Section 2.13: l. 544; rule 3, l. 482; l. 590. Gates: VD-2.0, l. 898; VD-2.13, l. 912; closing of G-LIVE-MS, l. 980; G5a, l. 1079. Decision D12: l. 1671, l. 1675, l. 1676. Spike: question 10, l. 1778, and l. 1782; sensitivity, l. 1530 |
| 4 | Add the witness that slows `registerHMR` of a child during the parent's render; it must fail the replacement criterion | DONE | Point 5 of the witness of the page: l. 571. Gate of VD-2.13: l. 912. Item 5 of G-LIVE-MS: l. 971. Question 10: l. 1778. Unknown: l. 1799 |
| 5 | Propagate to section 2.13, G-LIVE-MS, VD-2.13, VD-2.0 question 10 and the French owner summary, which stays one page | DONE | Section 2.13: l. 480 to l. 484, l. 502, l. 514, l. 515, l. 525, l. 529 to l. 546, l. 566 to l. 574, l. 579, l. 582, l. 590. G-LIVE-MS: l. 967 to l. 971, l. 978, l. 980. VD-2.13: l. 912. VD-2.0: l. 898; question 10: l. 1778. ### 16.2 The two findings

| # | Finding of the review | Status | Where in this spec | Evidence used; what stays open |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MAJOR — the render of a parent can still hide nested work of the hot replacement. The rule marks only the outermost calls of `setup` and `render` and counts everything that they call as render, outside the replacement. At the pin the Vapor render of a parent re-creates its children through `createComponent`, which calls `registerHMR(instance)` before their `setup`; that function consults the registry, creates its entry when needed and adds the instance. This work lies between the marks of the parent, in the excluded time. Counter-example: 1.5 ms outside the marks, 2 ms of render and 12 ms of nested re-registration on one edit in ten give a declared replacement of 1.5 ms at p95 and a render of 14 ms; both pass, while the replacement, hot-replacement work included, reaches 13.5 ms. No witness requires the nested case, and G-LIVE-MS keeps the false pass | FIXED, by the fallback that the review names — the threshold on the whole of `p0 → p2`, an overrun to decision D12 — on the conductor's decision, with the witness added | One claimed interval: stage 6, l. 514; its information row, l. 515; marks, l. 502. Definition: l. 529; what the runtime does at the pin, the registration of the children among it, l. 531 to l. 536; the three attempts at separation, l. 538; one interval, l. 540; the render for information, l. 542; an overrun, l. 544; the diff witness, l. 546. Rules and words: l. 480 to l. 484, l. 66, l. 25. End to end: l. 525. Measurement: l. 566, l. 568, l. 570, the witness l. 571, l. 572, l. 574; gates, l. 579, l. 582; a missed criterion, l. 590. Lots: VD-2.13, l. 912; VD-2.0, l. 898. G-LIVE-MS, items 1, 2, 3, 5 and 12 and its closing: l. 967 to l. 971, l. 978, l. 980. Item 10 of G5-HOST, l. 1072; G5a, l. 1079; item 9 of G6-HOST, l. 1117; G6, l. 1119. Spike question 10: l. 1778. Decision D12: l. 1671 to l. 1676; decision D13: l. 1681. Risks R28 and R35: l. 1571, l. 1578; pre-mortem 21 and 24: l. 1607, l. 1610. Choices T28, T35, T42 and T43: l. 1750, l. 1757, l. 1764, l. 1765. Unknowns: l. 1797 to l. 1800. Compile module: l. 629. Coordination: l. 1889, l. 1890, l. 1892, l. 1893. French summary: l. 84 | Read again in the pinned runtime (prefix `rt36:`, l. 53): the fact of the review holds. The Vapor `createComponent` calls `registerHMR` on the new instance in the development build, before `setupComponent`, and in the same block binds the two hot-replacement entries of the instance, opens a measure and prepares its props and emits options; `registerHMR` reads the identifier of the definition, looks the record up, creates it when it is absent and adds the instance; the children of the previous render are unregistered when their block is removed. A render interval therefore holds work of the hot replacement for every child that the render creates, and a finer pair of marks would follow the internals of the runtime at every pin bump. The separation is given up: the page has one claimed interval, `p0 → p2`, under the figures that the swap had, ≤ 2 ms at p50 and ≤ 5 ms at p95, not widened. The render is measured apart for information and has no criterion; the 8 ms and 16.7 ms that revision 8 gave it outside the claim are withdrawn. On the counter-example of the review the whole of `p0 → p2` is 15.5 ms at p95 and 3.5 ms at p50, and the criterion fails at both. The witness has seven points in the page and each must fail the replacement; the fifth delays `registerHMR` of a child while the render function of its parent runs, through an accessor in place of the identifier that `registerHMR` reads first — or a copy of the runtime file, if question 10 shows that the accessor does not land there; the render function itself, which failed the render alone in revision 8, now fails the replacement. An overrun opens decision D12 with `p0 → p2`, its three parts and the render measured apart. Open: whether the unchanged figures hold with the render inside them — `unknown`, nothing is measured before question 10 on 14/11 at 08:00, and the exposure to decision D12 grows (risk R28, pre-mortem 24); the accessor of the witness in real engines (`unverified`); the flavor of the machine that runs the harness browsers (`unknown`) |
| 2 | MINOR — the comparison with revision 4 keeps the former date of G7: T+445 → T+435, 10 h gained, where the table and the current schedule give T+438, 7 h gained | FIXED | l. 1491, l. 1492 | The row of G7 gives T+438 (l. 1347); revision 4 had T+445 (section 12.3): 7 h. The five other dates of that line keep their 10 h. G7 alone loses 3 h of them, the hours that revision 8 added to VD-7.2. The file was searched for T+435: outside this line it stands only in the comparison of revision 8 with revision 7 and in the one of revision 6 with revision 5 (l. 1466, l. 1479), and in the records of sections 13 to 15, where it is the figure of those revisions |

### 16.3 The owner requirement, rule by rule

The requirement: « le v8 fasse des updates live au sens update par une ia en qq millisecondes ». Its three rules are those of rounds 6 to 8; their earlier status is in sections 13.2, 14.2 and 15.3. This table gives what this round changed.

| Rule | Status | Where in this spec | What stays open |
|---|---|---|---|
| No class or gate may pass while it exceeds "a few milliseconds" on the stages that the plan claims in that range | DONE | The criteria are unchanged and all under 10 ms. Closed in this round: the replacement in the page is claimed whole. The render, which revision 8 judged against 16.7 ms outside the claim with work of the hot replacement nested in it, is inside the 2 ms and 5 ms of the replacement (l. 514, l. 540). A delay anywhere between `p0` and `p2` — `registerHMR` of a child during the render of its parent among the points — fails the replacement in the gate (l. 571; l. 912; item 5 of G-LIVE-MS, l. 971) | Whether the unchanged figures are met with the render inside them: nothing is measured before 14/11 |
| A stage that physically cannot be in that range is named, has its own numeric target, and stays outside the claim, never inside a pass criterion that hides it | DONE | The list is shorter: the transit, the display, the durable write and the acknowledgement, the cold start, a component above the live size profile (l. 481, l. 513, l. 516, l. 548, l. 550). The render left it. It is not a stage that this plan can declare outside the range by a mark — the marks could not isolate it — so it is inside the replacement, and whether it fits is a measurement. No pass criterion can hide it: it has none (l. 515, l. 542) | The clock of the page and the machine of the harness browsers (`unknown`; question 16, l. 1787) |
| If the target is not reachable on current budgets, a numbered owner decision with numbers | DONE | Decision D12 takes an overrun of the replacement, with `p0 → p2`, its three parts and the render measured apart; its options that keep the claim are smaller components or a restructured view, and the one that does not is a figure stated by the owner — a figure of its own for the render included, which no revision of this plan puts back by itself (l. 1671 to l. 1676; l. 544). Decision D13 keeps its numbers: the proposed ceilings of 15.5 ms and 18.5 ms on the sum at p95 are unchanged and now hold the render (l. 525, l. 1681) | The owner's answers; the verdict of question 10 on 14/11 at 08:00 |
